## I / 一

===Original===
Since we have learned to study the development of human life as we study the evolution of species throughout the animal kingdom, some peculiar phenomena which have puzzled the philosopher and moralist for so long, begin to show themselves in a new light. We begin to see that, so far from being inscrutable problems, requiring another life to explain, these sorrows and perplexities of our lives are but the natural results of natural causes, and that, as soon as we ascertain the causes, we can do much to remove them.

===Chinese===
自从我们学会像研究整个动物界各物种的演化那样来研究人类生活的发展以来，一些长期以来令哲学家与道德家困惑不已的特殊现象，开始以新的面貌显现出来。我们开始看到，我们生活中的这些悲哀与困惑，远非需要另一次生命才能解释的难解之谜，而不过是自然原因造成的自然结果；而且，一旦我们查明这些原因，便能在消除它们方面大有作为。

===Original===
In spite of the power of the individual will to struggle against conditions, to resist them for a while, and sometimes to overcome them, it remains true that the human creature is affected by his environment, as is every other living thing. The power of the individual will to resist natural law is well proven by the life and death of the ascetic. In any one of those suicidal martyrs may be seen the will, misdirected by the ill-informed intelligence, forcing the body to defy every natural impulse﻿—even to the door of death, and through it.

===Chinese===
尽管个人意志有力量与环境抗争、暂时抵御环境、有时甚至战胜环境，但人类个体依然同其他一切生物一样受其环境影响，这一点始终成立。个人意志抗拒自然规律的力量，在苦行者（ascetic）的生与死中得到了充分的证明。在任何一个这样的自戕式殉道者身上，都可以看到被见识贫乏的理智引入歧途的意志，强迫身体违抗一切自然冲动——直到死亡之门，并跨入门去。

===Original===
But, while these exceptions show what the human will can do, the general course of life shows the inexorable effect of conditions upon humanity. Of these conditions we share with other living things the environment of the material universe. We are affected by climate and locality, by physical, chemical, electrical forces, as are all animals and plants. With the animals, we farther share the effect of our own activity, the reactionary force of exercise. What we do, as well as what is done to us, makes us what we are. But, beyond these forces, we come under the effect of a third set of conditions peculiar to our human status; namely, social conditions. In the organic interchanges which constitute social life, we are affected by each other to a degree beyond what is found even among the most gregarious of animals. This third factor, the social environment, is of enormous force as a modifier of human life. Throughout all these environing conditions, those which affect us through our economic necessities are most marked in their influence.

===Chinese===
然而，这些例外虽显示了人类意志所能为者，生活的普遍进程却显示出环境对人类不可抗拒的影响。在这些条件中，我们与其他生物共享物质宇宙的环境：气候与地域，物理、化学、电的作用力，都影响着我们，正如它们影响着一切动植物一样。我们又与动物共同承受自身活动的影响，即运动的反作用力。我们所做的一切，连同施加于我们的一切，造就了我们。但是，在这些力量之外，我们还受制于为人类状态所特有的第三类条件，即社会条件（social conditions）。在构成社会生活的有机互动之中，人与人相互影响的程度，甚至超过了最合群的动物中所见的情形。这第三个因素——社会环境——作为人类生活的塑造力量，作用极其巨大。而在所有这些环绕着我们的环境条件之中，那些通过我们的经济必需（economic necessities）作用于我们的条件，影响最为显著。

===Original===
Without touching yet upon the influence of the social factors, treating the human being merely as an individual animal, we see that he is modified most by his economic conditions, as is every other animal. Differ as they may in color and size, in strength and speed, in minor adaptation to minor conditions, all animals that live on grass have distinctive traits in common, and all animals that eat flesh have distinctive traits in common﻿—so distinctive and so common that it is by teeth, by nutritive apparatus in general, that they are classified, rather than by means of defence or locomotion. The food supply of the animal is the largest passive factor in his development; the processes by which he obtains his food supply, the largest active factor in his development. It is these activities, the incessant repetition of the exertions by which he is fed, which most modify his structure and develop his functions. The sheep, the cow, the deer, differ in their adaptation to the weather, their locomotive ability, their means of defence; but they agree in main characteristics, because of their common method of nutrition.

===Chinese===
暂且不论社会因素的影响，仅把人当作一个个体动物来看，我们便看到，他同其他一切动物一样，受经济条件的改变最大。食草的动物与食肉的动物尽可以在颜色与大小、力量与速度上各不相同，在对次要条件的次要适应上各不相同，但一切以草为生的动物都具有共同的显著特征，一切食肉的动物也具有共同的显著特征——这些特征如此显著又如此普遍，以至于动物的分类依据是牙齿、是营养器官的总体，而非防御或运动的器官。动物的食物供给是其发展中最大的被动因素；动物获取食物供给的过程，是其发展中最大的主动因素。正是这些活动，正是那些赖以果腹的努力的不停重复，最大程度地改变了动物的身体结构并发展了其机能。绵羊、母牛和鹿，在对天气的适应、运动的能力、防御的手段上各不相同；但它们在主要特征上彼此一致，因为它们的营养方式是共同的。

===Original===
The human animal is no exception to this rule. Climate affects him, weather affects him, enemies affect him; but most of all he is affected, like every other living creature, by what he does for his living. Under all the influence of his later and wider life, all the reactive effect of social institutions, the individual is still inexorably modified by his means of livelihood: “the hand of the dyer is subdued to what he works in.” As one clear, world-known instance of the effect of economic conditions upon the human creature, note the marked race-modification of the Hebrew people under the enforced restrictions of the last two thousand years. Here is a people rising to national prominence, first as a pastoral, and then as an agricultural nation; only partially commercial through race affinity with the Phœnicians, the pioneer traders of the world. Under the social power of a united Christendom﻿—united at least in this most unchristian deed﻿—the Jew was forced to get his livelihood by commercial methods solely. Many effects can be traced in him to the fierce pressure of the social conditions to which he was subjected: the intense family devotion of a people who had no country, no king, no room for joy and pride except the family; the reduced size and tremendous vitality and endurance of the pitilessly selected survivors of the Ghetto; the repeated bursts of erratic genius from the human spirit so inhumanly restrained. But more patent still is the effect of the economic conditions﻿—the artificial development of a race of traders and dealers in money, from the lowest pawnbroker to the house of Rothschild; a special kind of people, bred of the economic environment in which they were compelled to live.

===Chinese===
人类这种动物也绝非例外。气候影响他，天气影响他，天敌影响他；但最重要的是，他同其他一切生物一样，受其谋生方式的影响。纵然有后来更广阔生活的全部影响，纵然有种种社会制度的反作用，个体依然不可抗拒地被其谋生手段所塑造：「染工之手，终为所染之物所制。」作为经济条件影响人类的一个鲜明而举世皆知的例证，且看希伯来人（Hebrew people）在过去两千年被迫承受的种种限制之下发生的显著种族变异。这个民族起初以游牧、继而以农耕立国而崭露头角，只是借助于同世界贸易的先驱者腓尼基人（Phœnicians）的族缘联系，才部分地从事商业。在联合起来的基督教世界（Christendom）的社会权力之下——至少在这件最不符合基督教义的行径上是联合起来的——犹太人被迫仅以商业手段谋生。在他身上，可以追溯到他所承受的社会条件的严酷压力所造成的诸多后果：一个没有国家、没有君王、除了家庭别无欢欣与骄傲的容身之所的民族所具有的强烈家庭感情；隔都（Ghetto）里经无情筛选的幸存者那瘦小的体格与惊人的活力和耐力；被如此非人道地禁锢的人类精神中一再迸发出的奇崛天才。但更为显著的还是经济条件的影响——一个人为造就的商人与金钱交易者的种族的形成，从最低贱的当铺老板直到罗斯柴尔德（Rothschild）家族；一个特殊的族群，正是从他们被迫生活于其中的经济环境里孕育出来的。

===Original===
One rough but familiar instance of the same effect, from the same cause, we can all see in the marked distinction between the pastoral, the agricultural, and the manufacturing classes in any nation, though their other conditions be the same. On the clear line of argument that functions and organs are developed by use, that what we use most is developed most, and that the daily processes of supplying economic needs are the processes that we most use, it follows that, when we find special economic conditions affecting any special class of people, we may look for special results, and find them.

===Chinese===
同一原因造成的同一影响，还有一个粗略却为人熟知的例证，我们人人都能看到：任何民族中，牧业的、农业的与制造业的阶层之间存在显著差别，即便他们的其他条件完全相同。沿着一条清晰的论证路线——机能与器官因使用而发展，使用最多者发展最多，而满足经济需要的日常过程正是我们使用最多的过程——便可推出：当我们发现特殊的经济条件作用于某个特定的人群阶层时，我们便可以预期特殊的结果，并且果然能找到它们。

===Original===
In view of these facts, attention is now called to a certain marked and peculiar economic condition affecting the human race, and unparalleled in the organic world. We are the only animal species in which the female depends on the male for food, the only animal species in which the sex-relation is also an economic relation. With us an entire sex lives in a relation of economic dependence upon the other sex, and the economic relation is combined with the sex-relation. The economic status of the human female is relative to the sex-relation.

===Chinese===
鉴于这些事实，现在提请人们注意一种作用于人类、而在有机世界中无可比拟的显著而特殊的经济状况。我们是唯一一个雌性依赖雄性获取食物的动物物种，是唯一一个性关系同时也是经济关系的动物物种。在我们人类之中，整整一个性别生活在对另一个性别的经济依附之中，而经济关系又与性关系结合在一起。人类女性的经济地位是相对于性关系而言的。

===Original===
It is commonly assumed that this condition also obtains among other animals, but such is not the case. There are many birds among which, during the nesting season, the male helps the female feed the young, and partially feeds her; and, with certain of the higher carnivora, the male helps the female feed the young, and partially feeds her. In no case does she depend on him absolutely, even during this season, save in that of the hornbill, where the female, sitting on her nest in a hollow tree, is walled in with clay by the male, so that only her beak projects; and then he feeds her while the eggs are developing. But even the female hornbill does not expect to be fed at any other time. The female bee and ant are economically dependent, but not on the male. The workers are females, too, specialized to economic functions solely. And with the carnivora, if the young are to lose one parent, it might far better be the father: the mother is quite competent to take care of them herself. With many species, as in the case of the common cat, she not only feeds herself and her young, but has to defend the young against the male as well. In no case is the female throughout her life supported by the male.

===Chinese===
人们通常以为这种状况在其他动物中也同样存在，但事实并非如此。在许多鸟类中，营巢季节里雄鸟会帮助雌鸟喂养幼雏，并部分地供养雌鸟；在某些高等食肉动物中，雄性也会帮助雌性喂养幼崽，并部分地供养雌性。但即便在这个季节，雌性也绝非绝对地依赖雄性，唯一的例外是犀鸟（hornbill）：雌鸟坐在空心树中的巢上，被雄鸟用泥封起来，只露出喙；在孵卵期间由雄鸟喂食。但即便是雌犀鸟，也不指望在其他任何时间得到喂食。雌蜂和雌蚁在经济上是依附性的，但并不依附于雄性。工蜂同样是雌性，只是完全特化于经济职能。而在食肉动物中，如果幼崽注定要失去父母之一，那么失去父亲反倒好得多：母亲完全有能力独自照料它们。在许多物种中，比如普通的猫，雌性不仅要养活自己和幼崽，还不得不保护幼崽免遭雄性的伤害。无论何种情况，雌性都从不一生由雄性供养。

===Original===
In the human species the condition is permanent and general, though there are exceptions, and though the present century is witnessing the beginnings of a great change in this respect. We have not been accustomed to face this fact beyond our loose generalization that it was “natural,” and that other animals did so, too.

===Chinese===
在人类这一物种中，这种状况是持久而普遍的，尽管存在例外，尽管本世纪正见证着这方面一场伟大变革的开端。我们一向不习惯于直面这一事实，我们对它至多做一番笼统的泛论：说这是「自然的」，说其他动物也是如此。

===Original===
To many this view will not seem clear at first; and the case of working peasant women or females of savage tribes, and the general household industry of women, will be instanced against it. Some careful and honest discrimination is needed to make plain to ourselves the essential facts of the relation, even in these cases. The horse, in his free natural condition, is economically independent. He gets his living by his own exertions, irrespective of any other creature. The horse, in his present condition of slavery, is economically dependent. He gets his living at the hands of his master; and his exertions, though strenuous, bear no direct relation to his living. In fact, the horses who are the best fed and cared for and the horses who are the hardest worked are quite different animals. The horse works, it is true; but what he gets to eat depends on the power and will of his master. His living comes through another. He is economically dependent. So with the hard-worked savage or peasant women. Their labor is the property of another: they work under another will; and what they receive depends not on their labor, but on the power and will of another. They are economically dependent. This is true of the human female both individually and collectively.

===Chinese===
起初，这种观点在许多人看来并不清晰；人们会举出劳动的农妇或野蛮部落女性的境况，以及妇女普遍的家务劳作，来反驳它。即便对这些例子，也需要一番细致而诚实的辨析，才能把这一关系的本质事实弄清楚明白。马在自由的自然状态下是经济独立的。它凭自身的努力谋生，不依赖于任何其他生物。马在如今被奴役的状态下，则是经济依附的。它的生计得自主人之手；它的辛劳虽然繁重，却与其生计没有直接关系。事实上，喂养得最好、照料得最好的马，与干着最苦劳役的马，是截然不同的两种动物。马确实在干活；但它吃什么，取决于主人的财力与意愿。它的生计经由他人而来。它是经济依附的。操劳过度的部落妇女或农妇也是如此。她们的劳动是他人的财产：她们在他人的意志之下干活；她们所得并非取决于她们的劳动，而是取决于他人的财力与意志。她们是经济依附的。人类的女性，无论作为个体还是作为整体，莫不如此。

===Original===
In studying the economic position of the sexes collectively, the difference is most marked. As a social animal, the economic status of man rests on the combined and exchanged services of vast numbers of progressively specialized individuals. The economic progress of the race, its maintenance at any period, its continued advance, involve the collective activities of all the trades, crafts, arts, manufactures, inventions, discoveries, and all the civil and military institutions that go to maintain them. The economic status of any race at any time, with its involved effect on all the constituent individuals, depends on their worldwide labors and their free exchange. Economic progress, however, is almost exclusively masculine. Such economic processes as women have been allowed to exercise are of the earliest and most primitive kind. Were men to perform no economic services save such as are still performed by women, our racial status in economics would be reduced to most painful limitations.

===Chinese===
把两性作为一个整体来考察其经济地位时，差别最为显著。人作为一种社会动物，其经济地位建立在数量庞大、分工日趋专门化的个体所提供的合并与交换的服务之上。种族的经济进步、其在任何时期的维持、其持续的发展，都有赖于一切行业、手艺、技艺、制造、发明、发现，以及维系这一切的文武制度的集体活动。任何种族在任何时代的经济地位，及其对组成该种族的一切个体所产生的影响，都取决于他们遍及世界的劳动及其自由交换。然而，经济进步几乎完全是男性的事业。妇女被允许从事的经济活动，属于最早、最原始的一类。倘若男人只去从事那些至今仍由妇女从事的经济服务，我们种族的经济状况便会沦落到极为可悲的局限之中。

===Original===
To take from any community its male workers would paralyze it economically to a far greater degree than to remove its female workers. The labor now performed by the women could be performed by the men, requiring only the setting back of many advanced workers into earlier forms of industry; but the labor now performed by the men could not be performed by the women without generations of effort and adaptation. Men can cook, clean, and sew as well as women; but the making and managing of the great engines of modern industry, the threading of earth and sea in our vast systems of transportation, the handling of our elaborate machinery of trade, commerce, government﻿—these things could not be done so well by women in their present degree of economic development.

===Chinese===
从任何社会中抽走男性劳动者，所造成的经济瘫痪，将远比抽走女性劳动者为甚。妇女目前所从事的劳动，男人也能从事，只需把许多先进的劳动者重新安排回较早的产业形态中去；而男人目前所从事的劳动，妇女若非经一辈辈的努力与适应便无法接手。男人做饭、打扫、缝纫，能做得同女人一样好；但现代工业那些巨型机器的制造与管理，庞大交通体系中跨越陆地与海洋的贯通，以及贸易、商业、政府那套精密机器的操作——这些事情，以妇女目前的经济发展程度，是无法做得同样出色的。

===Original===
This is not owing to lack of the essential human faculties necessary to such achievements, nor to any inherent disability of sex, but to the present condition of woman, forbidding the development of this degree of economic ability. The male human being is thousands of years in advance of the female in economic status. Speaking collectively, men produce and distribute wealth; and women receive it at their hands. As men hunt, fish, keep cattle, or raise corn, so do women eat game, fish, beef, or corn. As men go down to the sea in ships, and bring coffee and spices and silks and gems from far away, so do women partake of the coffee and spices and silks and gems the men bring.

===Chinese===
这并非由于缺少取得这类成就所必需的基本人类能力，也并非由于任何天生的性别缺陷，而是由于妇女目前的处境不容许这种程度的经济能力得到发展。男性在经济地位上领先女性数千年。整体而言，男人生产并分配财富；妇女则从他们手中领受财富。男人狩猎、捕鱼、畜牧、种植玉米，妇女便吃野味、吃鱼、吃牛肉、吃玉米；男人乘船出海，从远方带回咖啡、香料、丝绸和宝石，妇女便享用男人带回的咖啡、香料、丝绸和宝石。

===Original===
The economic status of the human race in any nation, at any time, is governed mainly by the activities of the male: the female obtains her share in the racial advance only through him.

===Chinese===
任何民族、任何时代，人类的经济地位主要都由男性的活动所支配：女性在种族进步中所得到的份额，只能经由男性而来。

===Original===
Studied individually, the facts are even more plainly visible, more open and familiar. From the day laborer to the millionaire, the wife’s worn dress or flashing jewels, her low roof or her lordly one, her weary feet or her rich equipage﻿—these speak of the economic ability of the husband. The comfort, the luxury, the necessities of life itself, which the woman receives, are obtained by the husband, and given her by him. And, when the woman, left alone with no man to “support” her, tries to meet her own economic necessities, the difficulties which confront her prove conclusively what the general economic status of the woman is. None can deny these patent facts﻿—that the economic status of women generally depends upon that of men generally, and that the economic status of women individually depends upon that of men individually, those men to whom they are related. But we are instantly confronted by the commonly received opinion that, although it must be admitted that men make and distribute the wealth of the world, yet women earn their share of it as wives. This assumes either that the husband is in the position of employer and the wife as employee, or that marriage is a “partnership,” and the wife an equal factor with the husband in producing wealth.

===Chinese===
若从个体层面考察，这些事实更为显而易见，更为公开而熟悉。从做日工的劳工到百万富翁，妻子穿旧的衣裳或闪烁的珠宝、低矮的屋檐或气派的宅邸、疲惫的双脚或华贵的车马——这些都述说着丈夫的经济能力。妇女所享受的舒适、奢华，乃至生活必需品本身，都由丈夫挣得，再由丈夫给予她。而当妇女孤身一人、没有男人「供养」她、试图自行满足自己的经济需要时，她所面对的种种困难，便确凿地证明了妇女的一般经济地位究竟如何。没有人能否认这些明白的事实——就总体而言，妇女的经济地位有赖于男性的经济地位；就个体而言，妇女的经济地位有赖于与之相关的那一个个男性的经济地位。但我们随即又遇到一种流俗之见：虽然必须承认男人制造并分配了世界的财富，但妇女作为妻子挣得了自己的一份。这种看法要么假定丈夫处于雇主的位置、妻子处于雇员的位置，要么假定婚姻是一种「合伙关系」，妻子是与丈夫平等的财富生产要素。

===Original===
Economic independence is a relative condition at best. In the broadest sense, all living things are economically dependent upon others﻿—the animals upon the vegetables, and man upon both. In a narrower sense, all social life is economically interdependent, man producing collectively what he could by no possibility produce separately. But, in the closest interpretation, individual economic independence among human beings means that the individual pays for what he gets, works for what he gets, gives to the other an equivalent for what the other gives him. I depend on the shoemaker for shoes, and the tailor for coats; but, if I give the shoemaker and the tailor enough of my own labor as a house-builder to pay for the shoes and coats they give me, I retain my personal independence. I have not taken of their product, and given nothing of mine. As long as what I get is obtained by what I give, I am economically independent.

===Chinese===
经济独立充其量是一种相对的状态。就最广的意义而言，一切生物都在经济上依附于他者——动物依附于植物，人则兼依附于二者。就较窄的意义而言，一切社会生活都在经济上相互依存，人集体地生产着他绝无可能单独生产的东西。但就最贴切的解释而言，人与人之间的个体经济独立，意味着个体为其所得而支付、为其所得而劳动，以等价之物回报他人给予自己的东西。我的鞋有赖于鞋匠，衣服有赖于裁缝；但倘若我作为房屋建造者，付出足够多的自身劳动来偿付鞋匠与裁缝给我的鞋和衣服，我便保住了我的个人独立。我没有只取他们的产品而不付出自己的产品。只要我之所得以我之所予换来，我在经济上就是独立的。

===Original===
Women consume economic goods. What economic product do they give in exchange for what they consume? The claim that marriage is a partnership, in which the two persons married produce wealth which neither of them, separately, could produce, will not bear examination. A man happy and comfortable can produce more than one unhappy and uncomfortable, but this is as true of a father or son as of a husband. To take from a man any of the conditions which make him happy and strong is to cripple his industry, generally speaking. But those relatives who make him happy are not therefore his business partners, and entitled to share his income.

===Chinese===
妇女消费经济财物。她们拿什么经济产品来交换她们所消费的东西呢？那种认为婚姻是一种合伙关系、缔结了婚姻的两人生产出任何一方单独都无从生产的财富的主张，是经不起推敲的。一个幸福而舒适的男人能比一个不幸而不适的男人生产得更多，但这一点对父亲或儿子而言，同对丈夫一样成立。一般说来，夺去一个人使其幸福强壮的任何条件，都会损害他的勤劳。但那些使他幸福的亲眷，并不因此就成了他生意上的合伙人，有权分享他的收入。

===Original===
Grateful return for happiness conferred is not the method of exchange in a partnership. The comfort a man takes with his wife is not in the nature of a business partnership, nor are her frugality and industry. A housekeeper, in her place, might be as frugal, as industrious, but would not therefore be a partner. Man and wife are partners truly in their mutual obligation to their children﻿—their common love, duty, and service. But a manufacturer who marries, or a doctor, or a lawyer, does not take a partner in his business, when he takes a partner in parenthood, unless his wife is also a manufacturer, a doctor, or a lawyer. In his business, she cannot even advise wisely without training and experience. To love her husband, the composer, does not enable her to compose; and the loss of a man’s wife, though it may break his heart, does not cripple his business, unless his mind is affected by grief. She is in no sense a business partner, unless she contributes capital or experience or labor, as a man would in like relation. Most men would hesitate very seriously before entering a business partnership with any woman, wife or not.

===Chinese===
对所受的幸福报以感激，并不是合伙关系中的交换方式。男人与妻子相处所得的慰藉，不属于生意合伙的性质，她的节俭与勤勉也不是。一个处在她位置上的女管家，尽可以同样节俭、同样勤勉，却不会因此成为合伙人。夫妻在共同承担对子女的义务上——在他们共同的爱、责任与服务上——确是伙伴。但一个结了婚的制造商、医生或律师，在接受为人父母的伴侣时，并不等于在他的生意中接纳了一位合伙人，除非他的妻子同时也是制造商、医生或律师。在他的生意上，未经训练与历练，她甚至无法提出明智的建议。爱她的作曲家丈夫，并不能使她会作曲；而丧妻虽可能令男人心碎，却不能损害他的生意，除非他的心智被悲伤摧垮。除非她像处于同样关系中的男人那样投入资本、经验或劳动，否则无论从何种意义上说，她都不是生意上的合伙人。大多数男人在与任何女人——无论是否妻子——结成生意合伙之前，都会极为郑重地踌躇。

===Original===
If the wife is not, then, truly a business partner, in what way does she earn from her husband the food, clothing, and shelter she receives at his hands? By house service, it will be instantly replied. This is the general misty idea upon the subject﻿—that women earn all they get, and more, by house service. Here we come to a very practical and definite economic ground. Although not producers of wealth, women serve in the final processes of preparation and distribution. Their labor in the household has a genuine economic value.

===Chinese===
那么，倘若妻子并非真正意义上的生意合伙人，她以何种方式从丈夫那里挣得她从他手中得到的食物、衣着与居所呢？人们会立刻回答：靠家务劳役。这便是人们对这个题目普遍持有的模糊观念——妇女靠家务劳役挣得了她们所得的一切，甚至更多。至此，我们来到了一块非常实际而明确的经济地面上。妇女虽不是财富的生产者，却在财富制备与分配的最终环节中效力。她们在家庭中的劳动具有真实的经济价值。

===Original===
For a certain percentage of persons to serve other persons, in order that the ones so served may produce more, is a contribution not to be overlooked. The labor of women in the house, certainly, enables men to produce more wealth than they otherwise could; and in this way women are economic factors in society. But so are horses. The labor of horses enables men to produce more wealth than they otherwise could. The horse is an economic factor in society. But the horse is not economically independent, nor is the woman. If a man plus a valet can perform more useful service than he could minus a valet, then the valet is performing useful service. But, if the valet is the property of the man, is obliged to perform this service, and is not paid for it, he is not economically independent.

===Chinese===
让一定比例的人为其他人服务，以使受服务者得以生产更多，这是一种不容忽视的贡献。妇女在家中的劳动固然使男人得以生产出比原本更多的财富；就此而言，妇女是社会的经济要素。但马也是如此。马的劳动使男人得以生产出比原本更多的财富。马是社会的经济要素。但马在经济上并不独立，妇女也不独立。倘若一个男人加上一名贴身男仆能比没有男仆时提供更多有用的服务，那么这名男仆就是在提供有用的服务。但是，倘若这名男仆是这个人的财产，被迫提供这种服务，又得不到报酬，那他在经济上就是不独立的。

===Original===
The labor which the wife performs in the household is given as part of her functional duty, not as employment. The wife of the poor man, who works hard in a small house, doing all the work for the family, or the wife of the rich man, who wisely and gracefully manages a large house and administers its functions, each is entitled to fair pay for services rendered.

===Chinese===
妻子在家庭中所做的劳动，是作为其职能义务的一部分付出的，而非作为受雇的职务。穷人家的妻子在小屋子里辛苦操劳，为全家包揽一切活计；或者富人家的妻子明智而得体地管理着一座大宅、执掌着它的各项事务——无论哪一位，都理应为其所提供的服务获得公允的报酬。

===Original===
To take this ground and hold it honestly, wives, as earners through domestic service, are entitled to the wages of cooks, housemaids, nursemaids, seamstresses, or housekeepers, and to no more. This would of course reduce the spending money of the wives of the rich, and put it out of the power of the poor man to “support” a wife at all, unless, indeed, the poor man faced the situation fully, paid his wife her wages as house servant, and then she and he combined their funds in the support of their children. He would be keeping a servant: she would be helping keep the family. But nowhere on earth would there be “a rich woman” by these means. Even the highest class of private housekeeper, useful as her services are, does not accumulate a fortune. She does not buy diamonds and sables and keep a carriage. Things like these are not earned by house service.

===Chinese===
若要站上这个立场并且诚实地守住它，那么妻子们作为以家内服务挣钱的人，有权获得厨师、女仆、保姆、缝纫女工或女管家的工钱，但也仅此而已。这当然会缩减富人之妻的零用钱，也会使穷人根本无力「供养」一个妻子——除非那穷人彻底正视处境，把家内佣工的工钱付给妻子，然后她与他把两人的钱合起来抚养子女。他便是雇了一名仆人：她则是在帮忙养家。但无论在世上何处，靠这些手段都不会出现「一个富有的女人」。即便是最高级的私人女管家，其服务再有用，也积攒不起一份家产。她买不起钻石和貂皮，也养不起一辆马车。这样的东西不是靠家务劳役挣得来的。

===Original===
But the salient fact in this discussion is that, whatever the economic value of the domestic industry of women is, they do not get it. The women who do the most work get the least money, and the women who have the most money do the least work. Their labor is neither given nor taken as a factor in economic exchange. It is held to be their duty as women to do this work; and their economic status bears no relation to their domestic labors, unless an inverse one. Moreover, if they were thus fairly paid﻿—given what they earned, and﻿—no more﻿—all women working in this way would be reduced to the economic status of the house servant. Few women﻿—or men either﻿—care to face this condition. The ground that women earn their living by domestic labor is instantly forsaken, and we are told that they obtain their livelihood as mothers. This is a peculiar position. We speak of it commonly enough, and often with deep feeling, but without due analysis.

===Chinese===
但这场讨论中最突出的事实是：无论妇女的家务劳作具有怎样的经济价值，她们都没有得到它。干活最多的妇女得钱最少，而钱最多的妇女干活最少。她们的劳动既不被当作经济交换中的一个要素来付出，也不被当作这样的要素来接受。人们认为做这些工作乃是她们身为女人的本分；而她们的经济地位与其家务劳动毫无关系——倘若有，也只是一种反向的关系。况且，倘若她们果真由此获得公允的报酬——得到她们挣下的，也仅止于此——那么所有以这种方式工作的妇女，都将沦落到家内佣工的经济地位。无论妇女还是男人，都少有人愿意正视这种处境。于是，「妇女靠家务劳动谋生」的立场立刻被弃守，我们又被告知：她们是以母亲的身份获得生计的。这是一个特殊的立场。我们对它谈得够多了，而且常常满怀深情，却缺乏应有的分析。

===Original===
In treating of an economic exchange, asking what return in goods or labor women make for the goods and labor given them﻿—either to the race collectively or to their husbands individually﻿—what payment women make for their clothes and shoes and furniture and food and shelter, we are told that the duties and services of the mother entitle her to support.

===Chinese===
在讨论一种经济交换、追问妇女以何种财物或劳动回报给予她们的财物或劳动——无论对整个种族还是对她们各自的丈夫——追问妇女以什么来偿付她们的衣履鞋袜、家具、饮食与居所时，我们得到的回答是：母亲的职责与服务使她有权获得供养。

===Original===
If this is so, if motherhood is an exchangeable commodity given by women in payment for clothes and food, then we must of course find some relation between the quantity or quality of the motherhood and the quantity and quality of the pay. This being true, then the women who are not mothers have no economic status at all; and the economic status of those who are must be shown to be relative to their motherhood. This is obviously absurd. The childless wife has as much money as the mother of many﻿—more; for the children of the latter consume what would otherwise be hers; and the inefficient mother is no less provided for than the efficient one. Visibly, and upon the face of it, women are not maintained in economic prosperity proportioned to their motherhood. Motherhood bears no relation to their economic status. Among primitive races, it is true﻿—in the patriarchal period, for instance﻿—there was some truth in this position. Women being of no value whatever save as bearers of children, their favor and indulgence did bear direct relation to maternity; and they had reason to exult on more grounds than one when they could boast a son. Today, however, the maintenance of the woman is not conditioned upon this. A man is not allowed to discard his wife because she is barren. The claim of motherhood as a factor in economic exchange is false today. But suppose it were true. Are we willing to hold this ground, even in theory? Are we willing to consider motherhood as a business, a form of commercial exchange? Are the cares and duties of the mother, her travail and her love, commodities to be exchanged for bread?

===Chinese===
倘若如此，倘若母职是妇女付出的一种可交换的商品，用以偿付衣服与食物，那么我们当然必须能在母职的数量或质量与报酬的数量或质量之间找到某种关系。若这一点成立，那么不曾为母的妇女便根本没有任何经济地位；而为母者的经济地位，也必须被证明与其母职相关。这显然荒谬。无子的妻子与多子的母亲拥有同样多的钱——甚至更多；因为后者的子女消耗掉的，本可以归她所有；而不称职的母亲所受的供养，也并不比称职的母亲少。显而易见、一目了然的是，妇女并非按其母职的比例享有经济上的丰裕。母职与其经济地位毫无关系。在原始民族中——例如在父权制时期——这个立场倒确实有几分道理。彼时妇女除了生育子女之外一无所值，她们所受的宠爱与优待确实与生育直接相关；当她们能夸耀生了一个儿子时，她们确实有不止一方面的理由欢欣。然而今天，妇女的供养已不再以此为条件。男人不得因妻子不育而休弃她。把母职当作经济交换中的一个要素的主张，在今天是不成立的。但姑且假定它成立。我们愿意守住这个立场吗，哪怕只在理论上？我们愿意把母职视为一门生意、一种商业交换的形式吗？母亲的操劳与职责、她的分娩之苦与她的爱，是可以用来换取面包的商品吗？

===Original===
It is revolting so to consider them; and, if we dare face our own thoughts, and force them to their logical conclusion, we shall see that nothing could be more repugnant to human feeling, or more socially and individually injurious, than to make motherhood a trade. Driven off these alleged grounds of women’s economic independence; shown that women, as a class, neither produce nor distribute wealth; that women, as individuals, labor mainly as house servants, are not paid as such, and would not be satisfied with such an economic status if they were so paid; that wives are not business partners or co-producers of wealth with their husbands, unless they actually practise the same profession; that they are not salaried as mothers, and that it would be unspeakably degrading if they were﻿—what remains to those who deny that women are supported by men? This (and a most amusing position it is)﻿—that the function of maternity unfits a woman for economic production, and, therefore, it is right that she should be supported by her husband.

===Chinese===
如此看待它们，令人憎厌；而且，倘若我们敢于直面自己的思想，把它推到逻辑的终点，我们便会看到，没有什么比把母职变成一门行当更违背人类的情感，也没有什么比这在社会与个人两方面危害更深。在这些所谓的妇女经济独立的根据上一一败退之后——在被指出妇女作为一个阶级既不生产也不分配财富；被指出妇女作为个体主要像家内佣工一样劳作，却未按佣工获得报酬，而且即便按此付酬她们也不会满意于这样的经济地位；被指出妻子并非丈夫生意上的合伙人或财富的共同生产者，除非她们确实从事同一职业；被指出她们并不作为母亲领取薪水，而且倘若如此将有说不出口的辱没——那么，对于那些否认妇女由男人供养的人，还剩下什么呢？只剩下这个（而且这是一个极为滑稽的立场）——生育的机能使妇女不宜从事经济生产，因此，她由丈夫供养是正当的。

===Original===
The ground is taken that the human female is not economically independent, that she is fed by the male of her species. In denial of this, it is first alleged that she is economically independent﻿—that she does support herself by her own industry in the house. It being shown that there is no relation between the economic status of woman and the labor she performs in the home, it is then alleged that not as house servant, but as mother, does woman earn her living. It being shown that the economic status of woman bears no relation to her motherhood, either in quantity or quality, it is then alleged that motherhood renders a woman unfit for economic production, and that, therefore, it is right that she be supported by her husband. Before going farther, let us seize upon this admission﻿—that she *is* supported by her husband.

===Chinese===
所持的立场是：人类女性在经济上并不独立，她由本物种的雄性供养。为否认这一点，人们起初辩称她在经济上是独立的——她确实靠自己在家庭中的勤劳养活自己。在被指出妇女的经济地位与她在家庭中所付出的劳动并无关系之后，人们又辩称：妇女不是以家内佣工、而是以母亲的身份谋生。在被指出妇女的经济地位与其母职无论在数量上还是质量上都毫无关系之后，人们又辩称：母职使妇女不宜从事经济生产，因此她由丈夫供养是正当的。在继续前进之前，让我们抓住这个承认——她确实（*is*）是由丈夫供养的。

===Original===
Without going into either the ethics or the necessities of the case, we have reached so much common ground: the female of genus *homo* is supported by the male. Whereas, in other species of animals, male and female alike graze and browse, hunt and kill, climb, swim, dig, run, and fly for their livings, in our species the female does not seek her own living in the specific activities of our race, but is fed by the male.

===Chinese===
暂且不问此事的伦理与必要性，我们已达成如下的共识：人属（genus *homo*）的雌性由雄性供养。在其他动物物种中，雌雄同样地吃草嚼叶、捕猎杀生、攀爬、游泳、掘穴、奔跑和飞翔以谋生；而在我们这个物种中，雌性并不以我们种族特有的活动去谋取自己的生计，而是由雄性喂养。

===Original===
Now as to the alleged necessity. Because of her maternal duties, the human female is said to be unable to get her own living. As the maternal duties of other females do not unfit them for getting their own living and also the livings of their young, it would seem that the human maternal duties require the segregation of the entire energies of the mother to the service of the child during her entire adult life, or so large a proportion of them that not enough remains to devote to the individual interests of the mother.

===Chinese===
现在来谈所谓的必要性。据说人类女性由于其母亲职责而无法自谋生计。既然其他雌性动物的母亲职责并不妨碍它们为自己谋得生计、还为幼崽谋得生计，那么照此说来，人类的母亲职责想必是要求把母亲的全部精力在其整个成年生活中都隔绝起来、专用于对子女的服务，或者占用其中如此大的一部分，以至于所剩无几，无从倾注于母亲自身的个人利益。

===Original===
Such a condition, did it exist, would of course excuse and justify the pitiful dependence of the human female, and her support by the male. As the queen bee, modified entirely to maternity, is supported, not by the male, to be sure, but by her co-workers, the “old maids,” the barren working bees, who labor so patiently and lovingly in their branch of the maternal duties of the hive, so would the human female, modified entirely to maternity, become unfit for any other exertion, and a helpless dependant.

===Chinese===
这样的状况倘若真的存在，当然足以为人类女性可悲的依附及其受雄性供养一事开脱，并使之正当化。正如完全特化为生育的蜂王，确乎不是由雄性供养，而是由她的同伴们——那些「老处女」、不育的工蜂——供养；它们在蜂巢母职的分支里如此耐心而充满爱意地劳作。同样，完全特化为生育的人类女性，也将变得不宜于任何其他的劳作，成为一个无助的依附者。

===Original===
Is this the condition of human motherhood? Does the human mother, by her motherhood, thereby lose control of brain and body, lose power and skill and desire for any other work? Do we see before us the human race, with all its females segregated entirely to the uses of motherhood, consecrated, set apart, specially developed, spending every power of their nature on the service of their children?

===Chinese===
这是人类母职的状况吗？人类母亲是否因其母职而就此失去对头脑与身体的支配，失去从事其他任何工作的能力、技艺与愿望？我们眼前看到的人类种族，是否其全部雌性都被完全隔离于母职的用途之中，被奉献、被分别出来、被特别地发展，把天性中的每一分力量都倾注于为子女的服务？

===Original===
We do not. We see the human mother worked far harder than a mare, laboring her life long in the service, not of her children only, but of men; husbands, brothers, fathers, whatever male relatives she has; for mother and sister also; for the church a little, if she is allowed; for society, if she is able; for charity and education and reform﻿—working in many ways that are not the ways of motherhood.

===Chinese===
并非如此。我们看到的人类母亲，操劳远胜于母马：她终生劳作，服务的不仅是她的子女，还有男人们——丈夫、兄弟、父亲，以及她所拥有的一切男性亲眷；也为母亲和姐妹操劳；若蒙允许，还为教会稍尽绵力；若力所能及，还为社会效力；还为慈善、教育与社会改良——她以许多并非母职之道的方式劳作着。

===Original===
It is not motherhood that keeps the housewife on her feet from dawn till dark; it is house service, not child service. Women work longer and harder than most men, and not solely in maternal duties. The savage mother carries the burdens, and does all menial service for the tribe. The peasant mother toils in the fields, and the workingman’s wife in the home. Many mothers, even now, are wage-earners for the family, as well as bearers and rearers of it. And the women who are not so occupied, the women who belong to rich men﻿—here perhaps is the exhaustive devotion to maternity which is supposed to justify an admitted economic dependence. But we do not find it even among these. Women of ease and wealth provide for their children better care than the poor woman can; but they do not spend more time upon it themselves, nor more care and effort. They have other occupation.

===Chinese===
使家庭主妇从清晨一直站到天黑的，并不是母职；那是家务劳役，而非对子女的服侍。妇女比大多数男人工作得更久、更苦，而且并非仅在母亲职责之内。野蛮部落的母亲背负重担，为部落做一切卑贱的杂役。农妇在田间操劳，工人的妻子在家中操劳。许多母亲即使在今天，也既是家庭的生育养育者，又是家庭的挣工资者。而那些并非如此操劳的妇女、那些属于富人的妇女——在她们身上，或许能找到那种被认为足以为公认的经济依附辩护的、倾尽一切的献身于生育的精神吧。但即使在她们中间，我们也找不到它。安逸富有的妇女为子女提供的照料，比穷妇所能提供的更好；但她们亲自花在上面的时间并不更多，心思与气力也并不更多。她们另有事业。

===Original===
In spite of her supposed segregation to maternal duties, the human female, the world over, works at extra-maternal duties for hours enough to provide her with an independent living, and then is denied independence on the ground that motherhood prevents her working!

===Chinese===
尽管被假定隔绝于母亲职责，世界各地的女性人类，花在母职之外的职责上的劳动时数，已足以使她谋得独立的生计——然后却以母职妨碍她工作为由，拒绝给予她独立！

===Original===
If this ground were tenable, we should find a world full of women who never lifted a finger save in the service of their children, and of men who did *all* the work besides, and waited on the women whom motherhood prevented from waiting on themselves. The ground is not tenable. A human female, healthy, sound, has twenty-five years of life before she is a mother, and should have twenty-five years more after the period of such maternal service as is expected of her has been given. The duties of grandmotherhood are surely not alleged as preventing economic independence.

===Chinese===
倘若这个立场站得住，我们本应看到一个遍布这样的女人的世界：她们除了为子女效力之外从不抬一下手指；还应看到包揽其余*一切*（*all*）劳动的男人，伺候着那些因母职而无法照料自己的女人。这个立场是站不住的。一个健康健全的女性，在做母亲之前有二十五年的人生，而在付出人们期望于她的那段母亲职责的服务之后，还应再有二十五年。人们总不至于声称，为人祖母的职责妨碍了经济独立吧。

===Original===
The working power of the mother has always been a prominent factor in human life. She is the worker *par excellence*, but her work is not such as to affect her economic status. Her living, all that she gets﻿—food, clothing, ornaments, amusements, luxuries﻿—these bear no relation to her power to produce wealth, to her services in the house, or to her motherhood. These things bear relation only to the man she marries, the man she depends on﻿—to how much he has and how much he is willing to give her. The women whose splendid extravagance dazzles the world, whose economic goods are the greatest, are often neither houseworkers nor mothers, but simply the women who hold most power over the men who have the most money. The female of genus *homo* is economically dependent on the male. He is her food supply.

===Chinese===
母亲的劳动能力始终是人类生活中一个突出的要素。她是卓越的劳动者（*par excellence*），但她的工作并不足以影响其经济地位。她的生计、她所得的一切——食物、衣着、饰物、娱乐、奢华——这些都与她生产财富的能力、与她在家庭中的服务、与她的母职毫无关系。这些东西只与她所嫁的那个男人、她所依附的那个男人相关——与他拥有多少、他愿意给她多少相关。那些以惊人的挥霍令世界眩目的妇女、那些拥有经济财物最多的妇女，往往既非家务劳动者，亦非母亲，而不过是那些最能支配最有钱的男人的女人罢了。人属（genus *homo*）的雌性在经济上依附于雄性。他就是她的食物来源。

## II / 二

===Original===
Knowing how important a factor in the evolution of species is the economic relation, and finding in the human species an economic relation so peculiar, we may naturally look to find effects peculiar to our race. We may expect to find phenomena in the sex-relation and in the economic relation of humanity of a unique character—phenomena not traceable to human superiority, but singularly derogatory to that superiority; phenomena so marked, so morbid, as to give rise to much speculation as to their cause. Are these natural inferences fulfilled? Are these peculiarities in the sex-relation and in the economic relation manifested in human life? Indisputably these are—so plain, so prominent, so imperiously demanding attention, that human thought has been occupied from its first consciousness in trying some way to account for them. To explain and relate these phenomena, separating what is due to normal race-development from what is due to this abnormal sexuo-economic relation, is the purpose of the line of study here suggested.

As the racial distinction of humanity lies in its social relation, so we find the distinctive gains and losses of humanity to lie also in its social relation. We are more affected by our relation to each other than by our physical environment.

Disadvantages of climate, deficiencies in food supply, competition from other species—all these conditions society, in its organic strength, is easily able to overcome or to adjust. But in our inter-human relations we are not so successful. The serious dangers and troubles of human life arise from difficulties of adjustment with our social environment, and not with our physical environment. These difficulties, so far, have acted as a continual check to social progress. The more absolutely a nation has triumphed over physical conditions, the more successful it has become in its conquest of physical enemies and obstacles, the more it has given rein to the action of social forces which have ultimately destroyed the nation, and left the long ascent to be begun again by others.

===Chinese===
既已知晓经济关系在物种演化中是何等重要的一项因素，又发现人类物种中的经济关系如此独特，我们自然可以预期找到为人类这一种族所独有的效应。我们有理由预期，在人类的性关系（sex-relation）与经济关系中发现一类独一无二的现象——这些现象无法归因于人类的优越性，反而恰恰有损于这种优越性；它们如此显著、如此病态，以致其成因引发了大量臆测。这些自然的推论是否得到了印证？性关系与经济关系中的这些特性，是否在人类生活中显现了出来？无可置疑地显现了——它们如此显豁、如此突出、如此不容忽视地索求着人们的注意，以至于人类思想自其意识发端以来，便一直忙于设法对它们作出解释。辨析并关联这些现象，把源于正常种族发展（race-development）的部分与源于这种反常的性-经济关系（sexuo-economic relation）的部分区分开来，正是此处所倡议的这条研究路径的目的所在。

正如人类的种族区分（racial distinction）在于其社会关系，我们发现人类特有的得失也同样系于其社会关系。与物质环境相比，我们受彼此之间关系的影响更深。

气候的不利、食物供给的匮乏、来自其他物种的竞争——所有这些状况，社会凭其有机体的力量都足以轻易克服或调适。但在人与人的关系上，我们却远没有这般成功。人类生活中的严重危险与困扰，源自与社会环境调适的艰难，而非与物质环境调适的艰难。迄今为止，这些困难一直充当着社会进步的持续性阻碍。一个民族对物质条件赢得的胜利越彻底，它征服物质敌人与物质障碍的成就越卓著，它便越是放纵了种种社会力量的作用，而这些社会力量最终毁灭了这个民族，把漫长的攀升留待他人重新开始。

===Original===
> There is the moral of all human tales:
 

 ’Tis but the same rehearsal of the past—
 

 First Freedom, and then Glory; when that fails,
 

 Wealth, Vice, Corruption—barbarism at last.
 

 And History, with all her volumes vast,
 

 Hath but *one* page.
> — —*Childe Harold’s Pilgrimage*, Canto IV, CVIII

===Chinese===
> 这就是一切人间故事的教训：
 

 不过是往昔旧事的一再重演——
 

 先是自由，继而是荣耀；待荣耀凋零，
 

 便是财富、罪恶、腐化——终归于野蛮。
 

 而历史，纵然卷帙浩繁，
 

 却只有*一*页。
> ——《恰尔德·哈罗尔德游记》（*Childe Harold’s Pilgrimage*, Canto IV, CVIII）

===Original===
The path of history is strewn with fossils and faint relics of extinct races—races which died of what the sociologist would call internal diseases rather than natural causes. This, too, has been clear to the observer in all ages. It has been easily seen that there was something in our own behavior which did us more harm than any external difficulty; but what we have not seen is the natural cause of our unnatural conduct, and how most easily to alter it.

Rudely classifying the principal fields of human difficulty, we find one large proportion lies in the sex-relation, and another in the economic relation, between the individual constituents of society. To speak broadly, the troubles of life as we find them are mainly traceable to the heart or the purse. The other horror of our lives—disease—comes back often to these causes—to something wrong either in economic relation or in sex-relation. To be ill-fed or ill-bred, or both, is largely what makes us the sickly race we are. In this wrong breeding, this maladjustment of the sex-relation in humanity, what are the principal features? We see in social evolution two main lines of action in this department of life. One is a gradual orderly development of monogamous marriage, as the form of sex-union best calculated to advance the interests of the individual and of society. It should be clearly understood that this is a natural development, inevitable in the course of social progress; not an artificial condition, enforced by laws of our making. Monogamy is found among birds and mammals: it is just as natural a condition as polygamy or promiscuity or any other form of sex-union; and its permanence and integrity are introduced and increased by the needs of the young and the advantage to the race, just as any other form of reproduction was introduced. Our moral concepts rest primarily on facts. The moral quality of monogamous marriage depends on its true advantage to the individual and to society. If it were not the best form of marriage for our racial good, it would not be right. All the way up, from the promiscuous horde of savages, with their miscellaneous matings, to the lifelong devotion of romantic love, social life has been evolving a type of sex-union best suited to develop and improve the individual and the race. This is an orderly process, and a pleasant one, involving only such comparative pain and difficulty as always attend the assumption of new processes and the extinction of the old; but accompanied by far more joy than pain.

===Chinese===
历史的道路遍布业已灭绝之种族的化石与依稀残迹——这些种族死于社会学家所谓的内部疾病，而非自然原因。这一点，历来也为观察者所洞悉。人们不难看出，我们自身行为中的某种东西对我们的戕害甚于任何外部困难；但我们一直没有看清的，是我们这种反常行为的自然成因，以及改变它的最便捷途径。

粗略地划分人类困难的主要领域，我们会发现其中一大块在于社会个体成员之间的性关系，另一大块则在于经济关系。概而言之，我们生活中所见的种种烦恼，大多可以追溯到情感或钱袋。我们生活中的另一重祸患——疾病——也常常可以归结到这些原因——归结到经济关系或性关系的某种失调。吃得差，或者繁育得差，或者两者兼备，很大程度上正是我们成为这样一个多病种族的缘由。在这种错误的繁育之中，在人类性关系的这种失调之中，主要的特征是什么？在这部分生活领域中，我们看到社会演化有两条主要的行动路线。其一是作为性结合（sex-union）形式的一夫一妻婚姻（monogamous marriage）的渐进而有序的发展——这种形式最有利于促进个体利益与社会利益。应当明确理解：这是一种自然的发展，是社会进步进程中不可避免之事；并非由人类自立的法律强行造成的人为状态。一夫一妻制见于鸟类和哺乳类动物：它与多偶制（polygamy）、杂交（promiscuity）或任何其他性结合形式一样，同属自然状态；其恒久与完整，是由幼者的需要和种族的利益引入并强化的，正如任何其他生殖形式被引入时一样。我们的道德观念首先立足于事实。一夫一妻婚姻的道德品质，取决于它对个体和社会的真实益处。倘若它不是最有利于我们种族福祉的婚姻形式，它便不是正当的。从杂乱交配的野蛮人游群，一直到浪漫爱情的终身厮守，社会生活一路演化出一种最有利于个体与种族之发展与改进的性结合类型。这是一个有序的进程，也是一个可喜的进程，其中所包含的相对痛苦与艰难，不过是新过程的确立与旧过程的消亡所惯常伴随者；而且沿途的欢愉远多于痛苦。

===Original===
But with the natural process of social advancement has gone an unnatural process—an erratic and morbid action, making the sex-relation of humanity a frightful source of evil. So prominent have been these morbid actions and evil results that hasty thinkers of all ages have assumed that the whole thing was wrong, and that celibacy was the highest virtue. Without the power of complete analysis, without knowledge of the sociological data essential to such analysis, we have sweepingly condemned as a whole what we could easily see was so allied with pain and loss. But, like all natural phenomena, the phenomena of sex may be studied, both the normal and the abnormal, the physiological and the pathological; and we are quite capable of understanding why we are in such evil case, and how we may attain more healthful conditions.

So far, the study of this subject has rested on the assumption that man must be just as we find him, that man behaves just as he chooses, and that, if he does not choose to behave as he does, he can stop. Therefore, when we discovered that human behavior in the sex-relation was productive of evil, we exhorted the human creature to stop so behaving, and have continued so to exhort for many centuries. By law and religion, by education and custom, we have sought to enforce upon the human individual the kind of behavior which our social sense so clearly showed was right.

But always there has remained the morbid action. Whatever the external form of sex-union to which we have given social sanction, however Bible and Koran and Vedas have offered instruction, some hidden cause has operated continuously against the true course of social evolution, to pervert the natural trend toward a higher and more advantageous sex-relation; and to maintain lower forms, and erratic phases, of a most disadvantageous character.

===Chinese===
然而，与社会进步的自然进程相伴同行的，还有一个反常的进程——一种乖张而病态的作用，它使人类的性关系成为可怕的罪恶之源。这些病态作用与恶果如此触目，以至于历代轻率的思想家都断定整件事情统统错了，并认定独身才是最高的德行。由于缺乏完备分析的能力，缺乏此类分析所必需的社会学资料，我们把一眼可见与痛苦和损失如此相伴的东西，一股脑地全盘否定了。然而，与一切自然现象一样，性的现象——无论正常与反常、生理与病理——都是可以研究的；我们完全有能力理解我们何以陷入如此糟糕的境地，也有能力理解我们如何可以达到更为健康的状况。

迄今为止，对这一课题的研究一直建立在这样的假定之上：人必定就是我们所见的这个样子，人的行为完全出于自己的选择，而如果他并不愿如此行事，他尽可以停下来。于是，当我们发现人类在性关系中的行为滋生恶果时，我们便劝诫人类停止这种行为，并且一连许多个世纪劝诫不休。凭着法律与宗教，凭着教育与习俗，我们一直力图把我们的社会意识如此清楚地表明为正当的那种行为，强加到人类个体身上。

但那病态的作用始终存在。无论我们给予何种性结合形式以社会的认可，无论《圣经》、《古兰经》和《吠陀经》如何垂教，总有某种隐秘的原因在持续地对抗社会演化的正途，败坏那趋向更高级、更有利的性关系的自然趋势，并维系着种种具有极端不利性质的更低级形式与乖张阶段。

===Original===
Every other animal works out the kind of sex-union best adapted to the reproduction of his species, and peacefully practises it. We have worked out the kind that is best for us—best for the individuals concerned, for the young resultant, and for society as a whole; but we do not peacefully practise it. So palpable is this fact that we have commonly accepted it, and taken it for granted that this relation must be a continuous source of trouble to humanity. "Marriage is a lottery," is a common saying among us. "The course of true love never did run smooth." And we quote with unction *Punch*’s advice to those about to marry—"Don’t!" That peculiar sub-relation which has dragged along with us all the time that monogamous marriage has been growing to be the accepted form of sex-union—prostitution—we have accepted, and called a "social necessity." We also call it "the social evil." We have tacitly admitted that this relation in the human race must be more or less uncomfortable and wrong, that it is part of our nature to have it so.

Now let us examine the case fairly and calmly, and see whether it is as inscrutable and immutable as hitherto believed. What are the conditions? What are the natural and what the unnatural features of the case? To distinguish these involves a little study of the evolution of the processes of reproduction.

===Chinese===
其他一切动物都演化出了最适宜于其物种繁衍的性结合形式，并平和地实践着它。我们也已经演化出了对我们最好的那种形式——对当事的个体、对由此诞下的幼者、对作为整体的社会都最好；然而我们却未能平和地实践它。这一事实如此昭彰，以至于我们早已普遍默认了它，并理所当然地认为这种关系必定是人类持续烦恼之源。「婚姻是一场彩票」，是我们中间的俗语。「真爱之路从来不会平坦」。我们还煞有介事地引用《笨拙》（*Punch*）杂志给谈婚论嫁者的忠告——「别结！」至于那种奇特的附属关系——在一夫一妻婚姻逐渐成长为公认性结合形式的整个过程中始终与我们相随的卖淫——我们也照单全收，称之为「社会必需」。我们还称之为「社会罪恶」。我们已经默认：人类中的这种关系必定多多少少令人不安、有其不对之处，事情如此乃是我们天性的一部分。

现在，让我们公正而冷静地审视这一情形，看看它是否真如历来所信的那样不可究诘、不可更改。情况究竟如何？其中哪些是自然特征，哪些是反常特征？要辨别这些，需要对生殖诸过程的演化稍作研究。

===Original===
Very early in the development of species it was ascertained by nature’s slow but sure experiments that the establishment of two sexes in separate organisms, and their differentiation, was to the advantage of the species. Therefore, out of the mere protoplasmic masses, the floating cells, the amorphous early forms of life, grew into use the distinction of the sexes—the gradual development of masculine and feminine organs and functions in two distinct organisms. Developed and increased by use, the distinction of sex increased in the evolution of species. As the distinction increased, the attraction increased, until we have in all the higher races two markedly different sexes, strongly drawn together by the attraction of sex, and fulfilling their use in the reproduction of species. These are the natural features of sex-distinction and sex-union, and they are found in the human species as in others. The unnatural feature by which our race holds an unenviable distinction consists mainly in this—a morbid excess in the exercise of this function.

It is this excess, whether in marriage or out, which makes the health and happiness of humanity in this relation so precarious. It is this excess, always easily seen, which law and religion have mainly striven to check. Excessive sex-indulgence is the distinctive feature of humanity in this relation.

To define "excess" in this connection is not difficult. All natural functions that require our conscious cooperation for their fulfilment are urged upon our notice by an imperative desire. We do not have to desire to breathe or to digest or to circulate the blood, because that is done without our volition; but we do have to desire to eat and drink, because the stomach cannot obtain its supplies without in some way spurring the whole organism to secure them. So hunger is given us as an essential factor in our process of nutrition. In the same manner sex-attraction is an essential factor in the fulfilment of our processes of reproduction. In a normal condition the amount of hunger we feel is exactly proportioned to the amount of food we need. It tells us when to eat and when to stop. In some diseased conditions "an unnatural appetite" sets in; and we are impelled to eat far beyond the capacity of the stomach to digest, of the body to assimilate. This is an excessive hunger.

===Chinese===
在物种发展的极早阶段，自然以其缓慢却可靠的实验确证：两性分立为两个有机体并彼此分化，于物种有利。于是，从单纯的原生质团块、漂浮的细胞、无定形的早期生命形式中，逐渐生发出两性的区分——雄性与雌性的器官和功能在两个不同有机体中的渐进发展。经使用而发展、经使用而增强，性的区分便随着物种的演化而不断扩大。区分愈大，吸引愈强，直到一切较高等的种族都拥有两个截然有别的性别，被性的吸引强力地牵引到一处，并在物种的繁衍中各尽其用。这些便是性别区分（sex-distinction）与性结合的自然特征，人类物种与其他物种皆然。而我们这个种族所独有的一项并不令人艳羡的反常特征，主要就在于——对这种功能的行使达到了病态的过度。

正是这种过度——无论发生在婚姻之内还是婚姻之外——使人类在这一关系中的健康与幸福如此岌岌可危。正是这种一向显而易见的过度，才是法律与宗教主要竭力加以遏制的对象。过度的性放纵（excessive sex-indulgence）是人类在这一关系中的独特之处。

在此语境下界定「过度」并不困难。一切需要我们有意识配合方能完成的天生功能，都会由一种不容违抗的欲求敦促我们注意。我们无须想去呼吸、去消化、去让血液循环，因为这些无需我们的意志便自行完成；但我们却必须想吃想喝，因为胃若不以某种方式鞭策整个有机体去获取给养，便得不到它的供应。因此，饥饿被赋予我们，作为营养过程中的一个必要因素。同样，性吸引（sex-attraction）是生殖过程得以完成的一个必要因素。在正常状态下，我们所感饥饿的程度与我们所需食物的量恰成比例。它告诉我们何时当吃、何时当止。在某些疾病状态下，会生出「反常的食欲」；我们被驱使着吃得远远超出胃的消化能力、超出身体的吸收能力。这便是过度的饥饿。

===Original===
We, as a race, manifest an excessive sex-attraction, followed by its excessive indulgence, and the inevitable evil consequence. It urges us to a degree of indulgence which bears no relation to the original needs of the organism, and which is even so absurdly exaggerated as to react unfavorably on the incidental gratification involved; an excess which tends to pervert and exhaust desire as well as to injure reproduction.

The human animal manifests an excess in sex-attraction which not only injures the race through its morbid action on the natural processes of reproduction, but which injures the happiness of the individual through its morbid reaction on his own desires.

What is the cause of this excessive sex-attraction in the human species? The immediately acting cause of sex-attraction is sex-distinction. The more widely the sexes are differentiated, the more forcibly they are attracted to each other. The more highly developed becomes the distinction of sex in either organism, the more intense is its attraction for the other. In the human species we find sex-distinction carried to an excessive degree. Sex-distinction in humanity is so marked as to retard and confuse race-distinction, to check individual distinction, seriously to injure the race. Accustomed as we are simply to accept the facts of life as we find them, to consider people as permanent types instead of seeing them and the whole race in continual change according to the action of many forces, it seems strange at first to differentiate between familiar manifestations of sex-distinction, and to say: "This is normal, and should not be disturbed. This is abnormal, and should be removed." But that is precisely what must be done.

===Chinese===
我们作为一个种族，表现出过度的性吸引，继之以过度的放纵，以及不可避免的恶果。它驱使我们沉溺到这样一种程度——与有机体的本原需要全然不相称，甚至夸张到荒谬的地步，以致反过来损害了这一过程中附带的满足；这种过度不仅伤害生殖，还趋向于败坏和耗竭欲望本身。

人类这种动物在性吸引上表现出的过度，不仅通过对生殖自然过程的病态作用伤害种族，而且通过对其自身欲望的病态反作用伤害个体的幸福。

人类物种中这种过度的性吸引，成因何在？性吸引的直接起因是性别区分。两性分化愈甚，彼此吸引愈强。任一有机体的性区分愈发达，它对异性的吸引便愈炽烈。在人类物种中，我们发现性别区分达到了过度的程度。人类的性别区分如此显著，以致阻碍和扰乱了种族区分（race-distinction），遏制了个体区分（individual distinction），严重地伤害了种族。我们素来惯于径直接受生活中所见的种种事实，惯于把人看作一成不变的类型，而不是把人乃至整个种族看作在诸多力量的作用下不断变化的东西，因此起初要对种种司空见惯的性别区分表现加以甄别，并断言「这是正常的，不应搅动；那是反常的，应予革除」，似乎显得奇怪。但这恰恰是必须去做的事。

===Original===
Normal sex-distinction manifests itself in all species in what are called primary and secondary sex-characteristics. The primary are those organs and functions essential to reproduction; the secondary, those modifications of structure and function which subserve the uses of reproduction ultimately, but are not directly essential—such as the horns of the stag, of use in sex-combat; the plumage of the peacock, of use in sex-competition. All the minor characteristics of beard or mane, comb, wattles, spurs, gorgeous color or superior size, which distinguish the male from the female—these are distinctions of sex. These distinctions are of use to the species through reproduction only, the processes of race-preservation. They are not of use in self-preservation. The creature is not profited personally by his mane or crest or tail-feathers: they do not help him get his dinner or kill his enemies.

On the contrary, they react unfavorably upon his personal gains, if, through too great development, they interfere with his activity or render him a conspicuous mark for enemies. Such development would constitute excessive sex-distinction, and this is precisely the condition of the human race. Our distinctions of sex are carried to such a degree as to be disadvantageous to our progress as individuals and as a race. The sexes in our species are differentiated not only enough to perform their primal functions; not only enough to manifest all sufficient secondary sexual characteristics and fulfil their use in giving rise to sufficient sex-attraction; but so much as seriously to interfere with the processes of self-preservation on the one hand; and, more conspicuous still, so much as to react unfavorably upon the very processes of race-preservation which they are meant to serve. Our excessive sex-distinction, manifesting the characteristics of sex to an abnormal degree, has given rise to a degree of attraction which demands a degree of indulgence that directly injures motherhood and fatherhood. We are not better as parents, nor better as people, for our existing degree of sex-distinction, but visibly worse. To what conditions are we to look for the developing cause of these phenomena?

===Chinese===
正常的性别区分在一切物种中都表现为所谓的第一性征与第二性征（primary and secondary sex-characteristics）。第一性征是生殖所必需的那些器官与功能；第二性征则是那些最终服务于生殖之用、却非直接必需的结构与功能上的变异——例如牡鹿的角，用于性的战斗；孔雀的羽衣，用于性的竞争。凡雄性区别于雌性的次要特征——髯须或鬣毛、肉冠、肉垂、距、华丽的色彩或更魁伟的体格——这些都是性的区分。这些区分仅仅通过生殖、即种族保存（race-preservation）的过程，对物种有益；它们对自我保存（self-preservation）并无用处。这造物并不会因它的鬣毛、冠羽或尾羽而于自身有丝毫获益：它们既不能帮它弄到晚餐，也不能帮它杀死敌人。

相反，倘若这些特征因发展过度而妨碍它的活动，或使它成为敌人显眼的靶子，它们便会反过来损害它自身的利益。这样的发展便构成过度的性别区分（excessive sex-distinction），而这恰恰正是人类的处境。我们的性别区分已达到如此程度，以致对我们作为个体、作为种族的进步都颇为不利。我们物种中两性的分化，不仅是足以履行其原初功能的程度；也不仅是足以显示一切充分的第二性征、足以引发充分性吸引的程度；而是达到了一方面严重妨碍自我保存诸过程的程度；而更触目的是，达到了反过来损害它们本应服务的种族保存诸过程本身的程度。我们那把性特征表现到反常地步的过度性别区分，引发了一种程度的吸引，而这一程度的吸引又要求一种程度的放纵，它直接戕害母职（motherhood）与父职（fatherhood）。就我们现有的性别区分程度而言，我们作为父母并未变得更好，作为人也并未变得更好，而是明显更坏了。我们应当到什么样的境况中去寻找这些现象得以发展的成因呢？

===Original===
Let us first examine the balance of forces by which these two great processes, self-preservation and race-preservation, are conducted in the world. Self-preservation involves the expenditure of energy in those acts, and their ensuing modifications of structure and function, which tend to the maintenance of the individual life. Race-preservation involves the expenditure of energy in those acts, and their ensuing modifications of structure and function, which tend to the maintenance of the racial life, even to the complete sacrifice of the individual. This primal distinction should be clearly held in mind. Self-preservation and race-preservation are in no way identical processes, and are often directly opposed. In the line of self-preservation, natural selection, acting on the individual, develops those characteristics which enable it to succeed in "the struggle for existence," increasing by use those organs and functions by which it directly profits. In the line of race-preservation, sexual selection, acting on the individual, develops those characteristics which enable it to succeed in what Drummond has called "the struggle for the existence of others," increasing by use those organs and functions by which its young are to profit, directly or indirectly. The individual has been not only modified to its environment, under natural selection, but modified to its mate, under sexual selection, each sex developing the qualities desired by the other by the simple process of choice, those best sexed being first chosen, and transmitting their sex-development as well as their racial development.

The order mammalia is the resultant of a primary sex-distinction developed by natural selection; but the gorgeous plumage of the peacock’s tail is a secondary sex-distinction developed by sexual selection. If the peacock’s tail were to increase in size and splendor till it shone like the sun and covered an acre—if it tended so to increase, we will say—such excessive sex-distinction would be so inimical to the personal prosperity of that peacock that he would die, and his tail-tendency would perish with him. If the peahen, conversely, whose sex-distinction attracts in the opposite direction, not by being large and splendid, but small and dull—if she should grow so small and dull as to fail to keep herself and her young fed and defended, then she would die; and there would be another check to excessive sex-distinction. In herds of deer and cattle the male is larger and stronger, the female smaller and weaker; but, unless the latter is large and strong enough to keep up with the male in the search for food or the flight from foes, one is taken and the other left, and there is no more of that kind of animal. Differ as they may in sex, they must remain alike in species, equal in race-development, else destruction overtakes them. The force of natural selection, demanding and producing identical race-qualities, acts as a check on sexual selection, with its production of different sex-qualities. As sexes, they perform different functions, and therefore tend to develop differently. As species, they perform the same functions, and therefore tend to develop equally.

===Chinese===
让我们首先考察一下，自我保存与种族保存这两大过程在世界中得以进行时所凭借的力量平衡。自我保存，意味着把能量消耗于那些趋向于维持个体生命的行动，及其随之而来的结构与功能上的改变。种族保存，意味着把能量消耗于那些趋向于维持种族生命的行动，及其随之而来的结构与功能上的改变——甚至不惜完全牺牲个体。这一原初的区分应当牢记于心。自我保存与种族保存绝非同一的过程，而且往往直接对立。在自我保存这条线上，自然选择（natural selection）作用于个体，发展出使它得以在「生存斗争」（the struggle for existence）中取胜的那些特征，并通过使用而增强那些使它直接获益的器官与功能。在种族保存这条线上，性选择（sexual selection）作用于个体，发展出使它得以在德拉蒙德（Drummond）所谓的「他者的生存斗争」（the struggle for the existence of others）中取胜的那些特征，并通过使用而增强那些使它的幼者得以直接或间接受益的器官与功能。个体不仅在自然选择之下被改变得适应于它的环境，而且在性选择之下被改变得适应于它的配偶，每一性别都通过简单的选择过程发展出对方所期许的品质——性征最彰者最先中选，并把自己的性发展与种族发展一并传给后代。

哺乳纲（mammalia）乃是自然选择所发展的第一性区分的结果；而孔雀尾上华美的羽衣，则是性选择所发展的第二性区分。倘若孔雀的尾巴不断增大、愈加华美，直到亮如太阳、铺满一英亩之地——我们不妨说，倘若它趋向于如此增大——这样的过度性别区分将对那只孔雀的个体昌盛如此有害，以至于它会死去，它的尾巴倾向也将随之一同消亡。反过来说，雌孔雀的性别区分朝着相反方向施展其吸引——不是靠巨大与华丽，而是靠渺小与暗淡——倘若她变得如此之小、如此暗淡，以致无力让自己和幼雏得到食物与护卫，那么她便会死去；这便又多了一重对过度性别区分的遏制。在鹿群牛群中，雄性更大更强，雌性更小更弱；但除非雌性足够大、足够强，能在觅食或避敌时跟上雄性，否则便会一方被掠、一方被弃，这种动物从此绝迹。两性尽可以在性上有所不同，却必须在物种上保持相似，在种族发展上保持均等，否则毁灭便会降临。自然选择的力量要求并产生相同的种族品质，从而对性选择及其所产生的相异的性品质构成遏制。作为性别，它们执行不同的功能，因而趋向于不同的发育；作为物种，它们执行相同的功能，因而趋向于均等的发育。

===Original===
And as sex-functions are only used occasionally, and race-functions are used all the time—as they mate but yearly or tri-monthly, but eat daily and hourly—the processes of obtaining food or of opposing constant enemies act more steadily than the processes of reproduction, and produce greater effect.

We find the order mammalia accordingly producing and suckling its young in the same manner through a wide variety of species which obtain their living in a different manner. The calf and colt and cub and kitten are produced by the same process; but the cow and horse, the bear and cat, are produced by different processes. And, though cow and bull, mare and stallion, differ as to sex, they are alike in species; and the likeness in species is greater than the difference in sex. Cow, mare, and cat are all females of the order mammalia, and so far alike; but how much more different they are than similar!

Natural selection develops race. Sexual selection develops sex. Sex-development is one throughout its varied forms, tending only to reproduce what is. But race-development rises ever in higher and higher manifestation of energy. As sexes, we share our distinction with the animal kingdom almost to the beginning of life, and with the vegetable world as well. As races, we differ in ascending degree; and the human race stands highest in the scale of life so far.

===Chinese===
况且，性功能只是偶尔使用，而种族功能则时时刻刻都在使用——它们一年或一季才交配一次，却日日时时都要进食——觅食的过程或抵御常在之敌的过程，比生殖的过程作用得更稳定，产生的效果也更大。

于是我们发现，哺乳纲的各种物种虽以不同方式谋生，却以相同的方式生育和哺乳其幼崽。牛犊、马驹、幼熊、小猫都由同一过程所诞生；而牛和马、熊和猫，却由不同的过程所造就。而且，尽管母牛与公牛、母马与公马在性上有别，它们在物种上却是相似的；物种上的相似大于性别上的差异。母牛、母马和母猫都是哺乳纲的雌性，就这一点而言它们相似；然而它们之间的差异又何远大于相似！

自然选择发展种族。性选择发展性别。性发展在其纷繁形式中始终如一，只是趋于复制既存者。而种族发展则不断在愈来愈高的能量表现中上升。作为性别，我们与动物界——几乎直溯生命之始——乃至植物界共享着我们的区分。作为种族，我们彼此的差异沿着上升的阶度递增；而迄今为止，人类种族立于生命阶梯的最高处。

===Original===
When, then, it can be shown that sex-distinction in the human race is so excessive as not only to affect injuriously its own purposes, but to check and pervert the progress of the race, it becomes a matter for most serious consideration. Nothing could be more inevitable, however, under our sexuo-economic relation. By the economic dependence of the human female upon the male, the balance of forces is altered. Natural selection no longer checks the action of sexual selection, but cooperates with it. Where both sexes obtain their food through the same exertions, from the same sources, under the same conditions, both sexes are acted upon alike, and developed alike by their environment. Where the two sexes obtain their food under different conditions, and where that difference consists in one of them being fed by the other, then the feeding sex becomes the environment of the fed. Man, in supporting woman, has become her economic environment. Under natural selection, every creature is modified to its environment, developing perforce the qualities needed to obtain its livelihood under that environment. Man, as the feeder of woman, becomes the strongest modifying force in her economic condition. Under sexual selection the human creature is of course modified to its mate, as with all creatures. When the mate becomes also the master, when economic necessity is added to sex-attraction, we have the two great evolutionary forces acting together to the same end; namely, to develop sex-distinction in the human female. For, in her position of economic dependence in the sex-relation, sex-distinction is with her not only a means of attracting a mate, as with all creatures, but a means of getting her livelihood, as is the case with no other creature under heaven. Because of the economic dependence of the human female on her mate, she is modified to sex to an excessive degree. This excessive modification she transmits to her children; and so is steadily implanted in the human constitution the morbid tendency to excess in this relation, which has acted so universally upon us in all ages, in spite of our best efforts to restrain it. It is not the normal sex-tendency, common to all creatures, but an abnormal sex-tendency, produced and maintained by the abnormal economic relation which makes one sex get its living from the other by the exercise of sex-functions. This is the immediate effect upon individuals of the peculiar sexuo-economic relation which obtains among us.

===Chinese===
因此，当我们能够表明：人类的性别区分已过度到不仅损害其自身目的、而且遏制并败坏种族进步的地步，这便成为一个最须严肃考量的问题。然而，在我们的性-经济关系之下，没有什么比这更顺理成章的了。由于人类女性对男性的经济依附（economic dependence），力量的平衡便被改变了。自然选择不再遏制性选择的作用，而是与之携手协作。在两性以同样的劳作、从同样的来源、在同样的条件下获得食物之处，两性所受的作用相同，被环境造就的方式也相同。而在两性在不同的条件下获得食物、且这种不同在于其中一方由另一方喂养之处，喂养的一方便成了被喂养一方的环境。男性在供养女性的过程中，已经成了她的经济环境。在自然选择之下，每一造物都被改变得适应于它的环境，被迫发展出在该环境下谋生所需的品质。男性作为女性的供养者，成为她的经济状况中最强大的塑造力量。在性选择之下，人类当然也如一切动物一样被改变得适应于自己的配偶。当配偶同时成为主人，当经济上的必需叠加于性吸引之上，我们便有了两大演化力量朝同一目标协同作用；这目标便是：发展人类女性的性别区分。因为，在她于性关系中所处的经济依附地位上，性别区分之于她，不仅是如一切动物那样吸引配偶的手段，更是她谋取生计的手段——普天之下，再无别的造物是这种情形。正是由于人类女性在经济上依附于她的配偶，她才被改变得在性上达到过度的程度。这种过度的改变，她传给了自己的子女；于是一种在这一关系上趋向过度的病态倾向，便被稳稳地植入人类的天性之中——尽管我们竭力克制，它仍在一切时代对我们发挥着普遍的作用。这不是一切动物共有的正常性倾向，而是一种反常的性倾向，由那使一性靠行使性功能从另一性获取生计的反常经济关系所产生并维持。这便是通行的这种独特的性-经济关系，对个体产生的直接效应。

## III / 三

===Original===
In establishing the claim of excessive sex-distinction in the human race, much needs to be said to make clear to the general reader what is meant by the term. To the popular mind, both the coarsely familiar and the overrefined, “sexual” is thought to mean “sensual”; and the charge of excessive sex-distinction seems to be a reproach. This should be at once dismissed, as merely showing ignorance of the terms used. A man does not object to being called “masculine,” nor a woman to being called “feminine.” Yet whatever is masculine or feminine is sexual. To be distinguished by femininity is to be distinguished by sex. To be over-feminine is to be oversexed. To manifest in excess any of the distinctions of sex, primary or secondary, is to be oversexed. Our hypothetical peacock, with his too large and splendid tail, would be oversexed, and no offence to his moral character!

===Chinese===
要确立人类这一种族存在过度的性别区分（excessive sex-distinction）的主张，还需要多说几句，才能使一般读者明白这个术语的含义。在大众的心目中——无论是粗俗随便的一路，还是过分文雅的一路——「性的」（sexual）都被以为等于「肉欲的」（sensual）；于是，过度的性别区分这一指控看来便像是一种责难。这种想法应当立刻打消，因为它只不过暴露了对所用术语的无知。男人并不介意被称为「男性化的」，女人也不介意被称为「女性化的」。然而，凡是男性化的或女性化的东西，都属于性的范畴。以女性气质见别，就是以性别见别。过于女性化，便是过度性化。把任何一种性别区分——第一性的也好，第二性的也好——表现到过度的地步，便是过度性化。我们那假想的孔雀，拖着一条过于硕大华美的尾巴，便是过度性化的，而这丝毫无损于它的道德品格！

===Original===
The primary sex-distinctions in our race as in others consist merely in the essential organs and functions of reproduction. The secondary distinctions, and this is where we are to look for our largest excess﻿—consist in all those differences in organ and function, in look and action, in habit, manner, method, occupation, behavior, which distinguish men from women. In a troop of horses, seen at a distance, the sexes are indistinguishable. In a herd of deer the males are distinguishable because of their antlers. The male lion is distinguished by his mane, the male cat only by a somewhat heavier build. In certain species of insects the male and female differ so widely in appearance that even naturalists have supposed them to belong to separate species. Beyond these distinctions lies that of conduct. Certain psychic attributes are manifested by either sex. The intensity of the maternal passion is a sex-distinction as much as the lion’s mane or the stag’s horns. The belligerence and dominance of the male is a sex-distinction: the modesty and timidity of the female is a sex-distinction. The tendency to “sit” is a sex-distinction of the hen: the tendency to strut is a sex-distinction of the cock. The tendency to fight is a sex-distinction of males in general: the tendency to protect and provide for, is a sex-distinction of females in general.

===Chinese===
同其他物种一样，我们种族的第一性区分（primary sex-distinctions）仅在于生殖所必需的器官与功能。第二性区分——而这里正是我们要寻找最大过度之所在——则在于器官与功能、外貌与行动，以及习惯、举止、方式、职业、行为上一切使男人区别于女人的差异。远远望去的一群马中，两性无从分辨。鹿群中的雄鹿因角而可以辨认。雄狮以鬣毛见别，雄猫仅以略为粗壮的体格见别。在某些昆虫物种中，雌雄外观差异之大，竟使博物学家也曾以为它们分属不同的物种。在这些区分之外，还有行为上的区分。某些心理属性由两性中的这一方或那一方表现出来。母性激情的强度，同狮子的鬣毛或牡鹿的角一样，是一种性别区分。雄性的好斗与支配欲是一种性别区分；雌性的羞怯与胆怯是一种性别区分。「抱窝」的倾向是母鸡的一种性别区分；昂首阔步的倾向是公鸡的一种性别区分。好斗的倾向是一切雄性普遍的性别区分；守护与供养的倾向是一切雌性普遍的性别区分。

===Original===
With the human race, whose chief activities are social, the initial tendency to sex-distinction is carried out in many varied functions. We have differentiated our industries, our responsibilities, our very virtues, along sex lines. It will therefore be clear that the claim of excessive sex-distinction in humanity, and especially in woman, does not carry with it any specific “moral” reproach, though it does in the larger sense prove a decided evil in its effect on human progress.

===Chinese===
人类这一种族的主要活动是社会性的，性别区分的原初倾向便在许多各不相同的功能中得以施展。我们已经沿着性别的界线，分化了我们的产业、我们的责任，乃至我们的种种德性。因此应当明白：人类、尤其是女人存在过度的性别区分这一主张，并不附带任何特定的「道德」责难，尽管就更大的意义而言，它确实证明是危害人类进步的一大恶因。

===Original===
In primary distinctions our excess is not so marked as in the farther and subtler development; yet, even here, we have plain proof of it. Sex-energy in its primal manifestation is exhibited in the male of the human species to a degree far greater than is necessary for the processes of reproduction﻿—enough, indeed, to subvert and injure those processes. The direct injury to reproduction from the excessive indulgence of the male, and the indirect injury through its debilitating effect upon the female, together with the enormous evil to society produced by extramarital indulgence﻿—these are facts quite generally known. We have recognized them for centuries, and sought to check the evil action by law, civil, social, moral. But we have treated it always as a field of voluntary action, not as a condition of morbid development. We have held it as right that man should be so, but wrong that man should do so. Nature does not work in that way. What it is right to be, it is right to do. What it is wrong to do, it is wrong to be. This inordinate demand in the human male is an excessive sex-distinction. In this, in a certain over-coarseness and hardness, a too great belligerence and pride, a too great subservience to the power of sex-attraction, we find the main marks of excessive sex-distinction in men. It has been always checked and offset in them by the healthful activities of racial life. Their energies have been called out and their faculties developed along all the lines of human progress. In the growth of industry, commerce, science, manufacture, government, art, religion, the male of our species has become human, far more than male. Strong as this passion is in him, inordinate as is his indulgence, he is a far more normal animal than the female of his species﻿—far less oversexed. To him this field of special activity is but part of life﻿—an incident. The whole world remains besides. To her it is the world. This has been well stated in the familiar epigram of Madame de Staël﻿—“Love with man is an episode, with woman a history.” It is in woman that we find most fully expressed the excessive sex-distinction of the human species﻿—physical, psychical, social. See first the physical manifestation.

===Chinese===
在第一性区分上，我们的过度不及在更深远、更精微的发展中那样显著；然而即便在此，我们也有明白的证据。性能量（sex-energy）在其原初表现上，于人类雄性身上展示的程度，已远远超出生殖诸过程之所需——确实足以颠覆并损害那些过程。男性过度放纵对生殖的直接伤害，及其经由使女性衰弱而造成的间接伤害，连同婚外放纵给社会带来的巨大邪恶——这些都是相当普遍地为人所知的事实。许多世纪以来，我们一直承认这些事实，并力图凭民事的、社会的、道德的法律来遏制这种恶行。但我们始终把它当作一个自愿行动的领域来对待，而不曾把它当作一种病态发展的状况。我们一向认为：男人是这样，是对的；男人这样做，是错的。自然不以这种方式运行。怎样存在是正当的，怎样做就是正当的；怎样做是不正当的，怎样存在就是不正当的。人类雄性这种无节制的欲求，便是一种过度的性别区分。在此——在某种过分的粗砺与坚硬、过大的好斗与骄傲、对性吸引之力量的过分屈从中——我们看到了男性过度性别区分的主要印记。在男性身上，这种倾向始终被种族生活的健康活动所遏制和抵消。他们的能量被召唤出来，他们的官能沿着人类进步的一切路线得到发展。在产业、商业、科学、制造、政府、艺术、宗教的成长中，我们物种的雄性已经成人，远过于成雄。这激情在他身上纵然强烈，他的放纵纵然无度，比起其物种的雌性，他仍是一个远为正常的动物——远不那么过度性化。对他来说，这块特殊活动的领域只是生活的一部分——一个插曲。此外还有整个世界。而对她，它就是整个世界。斯塔尔夫人（Madame de Staël）那句人所熟知的隽语对此说得很好——「爱情于男人是一段插曲，于女人则是一部历史。」正是在女人身上，我们才发现人类物种过度的性别区分得到了最充分的表现——身体的、心理的、社会的。且先看身体方面的表现。

===Original===
To make clear by an instance the difference between normal and abnormal sex-distinction, look at the relative condition of a wild cow and a “milch cow,” such as we have made. The wild cow is a female. She has healthy calves, and milk enough for them; and that is all the femininity she needs. Otherwise than that she is bovine rather than feminine. She is a light, strong, swift, sinewy creature, able to run, jump, and fight, if necessary. We, for economic uses, have artificially developed the cow’s capacity for producing milk. She has become a walking milk-machine, bred and tended to that express end, her value measured in quarts. The secretion of milk is a maternal function﻿—a sex-function. The cow is oversexed. Turn her loose in natural conditions, and, if she survive the change, she would revert in a very few generations to the plain cow, with her energies used in the general activities of her race, and not all running to milk.

===Chinese===
为用一个实例说明正常性别区分与反常性别区分的差别，且看野生的母牛与我们所造就的「奶牛」二者相对的境况。野生的母牛是一头雌兽。她产下健康的牛犊，并有哺喂它们的足够的奶；这便是她所需的全部雌性特质。除此之外，她与其说是雌性的，不如说是牛性的。她是一个轻捷、强壮、迅疾、筋肉结实的生灵，必要时能奔跑、跳跃、搏斗。而我们出于经济用途，人为地发展了母牛的产奶能力。她成了一台行走的产奶机器，专为这一明确的目的而被繁育和照料，她的价值以夸脱计。乳汁的分泌是一种母性功能——一种性功能。这头母牛便是过度性化的。把她放归自然状态，倘若她经得起这一变化，只消极少的几代，她便会退回到普通的母牛，把能量用于其种族的一般活动，而不尽数化作了奶。

===Original===
Physically, woman belongs to a tall, vigorous, beautiful animal species, capable of great and varied exertion. In every race and time when she has opportunity for racial activity, she develops accordingly, and is no less a woman for being a healthy human creature. In every race and time where she is denied this opportunity﻿—and few, indeed, have been her years of freedom﻿—she has developed in the lines of action to which she was confined; and those were always lines of sex-activity. In consequence the body of woman, speaking in the largest generalization, manifests sex-distinction predominantly.

===Chinese===
在身体上，女人属于一个高大、强健、美丽的动物物种，能够付出巨大而多样的劳作。在任何民族、任何时代，只要她有机会从事种族活动，她便相应地得到发展，而且并不因做一个健康的人而稍减其为女人。而在任何民族、任何时代，只要她被剥夺了这种机会——而她自由自在的岁月，确实寥寥无几——她便在被禁锢其中的那些行动路线上得到发展；而那些路线向来是性活动的路线。其结果是，就最笼统的概括而言，女人的身体所显现的，以性别区分为主。

===Original===
Woman’s femininity﻿—and “the eternal feminine” means simply the eternal sexual﻿—is more apparent in proportion to her humanity than the femininity of other animals in proportion to their caninity or felinity or equinity. “A feminine hand” or “a feminine foot” is distinguishable anywhere. We do not hear of “a feminine paw” or “a feminine hoof.” A hand is an organ of prehension, a foot an organ of locomotion: they are not secondary sexual characteristics. The comparative smallness and feebleness of woman is a sex-distinction. We have carried it to such an excess that women are commonly known as “the weaker sex.” There is no such glaring difference between male and female in other advanced species. In the long migrations of birds, in the ceaseless motion of the grazing herds that used to swing up and down over the continent each year, in the wild, steep journeys of the breeding salmon, nothing is heard of the weaker sex. And among the higher carnivora, where longer maintenance of the young brings their condition nearer ours, the hunter dreads the attack of the female more than that of the male. The disproportionate weakness is an excessive sex-distinction. Its injurious effect may be broadly shown in the Oriental nations, where the female in curtained harems is confined most exclusively to sex-functions and denied most fully the exercise of race-functions. In such peoples the weakness, the tendency to small bones and adipose tissue of the oversexed female, is transmitted to the male, with a retarding effect on the development of the race. Conversely, in early Germanic tribes the comparatively free and humanly developed women﻿—tall, strong, and brave﻿—transmitted to their sons a greater proportion of human power and much less of morbid sex-tendency.

===Chinese===
女人的雌性特质——而「永恒女性」（the eternal feminine）指的无非是永恒的性——相对于她的人性而言，比其他动物的雌性特质相对于它们的犬性、猫性或马性更为显眼。一只「女性化的手」或一只「女性化的脚」，在哪儿都能辨认出来。我们却从未听说过「女性化的爪子」或「女性化的蹄子」。手是抓握的器官，脚是运动的器官：它们并不是第二性征。女人那种相对的瘦小与孱弱，是一种性别区分。我们把它推行到如此过度的地步，以致女人普遍被称为「较弱的性别」。在其他高级物种中，雌雄之间并没有这般刺眼的差别。在鸟类漫长的迁徙中，在昔日每年往复于大陆之上的游牧兽群不息的移动中，在溯流产卵的鲑鱼那狂野险陡的旅程中，从未听说过什么较弱的性别。而在较高等的食肉动物中——在那里，对幼崽较长时间的抚育使其境况更接近于我们——猎人畏惧雌兽的攻击甚于雄兽。这种不相称的虚弱，便是一种过度的性别区分。它的有害影响在东方诸民族中可以概见：在那里，帘幕深锁的后宫中的女性，被最彻底地禁锢于性功能，又被最完全地剥夺了种族功能的行使。在这样的民族中，过度性化的雌性那种虚弱、那种细小骨骼与脂肪组织的倾向，会传给雄性，对种族的发展产生迟滞作用。反过来，在早期的日耳曼部落中，相对自由、得到人性化发展的妇女——高大、强壮、勇敢——传给儿子们的人的力量比例更大，病态的性倾向则少得多。

===Original===
The degree of feebleness and clumsiness common to women, the comparative inability to stand, walk, run, jump, climb, and perform other race-functions common to both sexes, is an excessive sex-distinction; and the ensuing transmission of this relative feebleness to their children, boys and girls alike, retards human development. Strong, free, active women, the sturdy, field-working peasant, the burden-bearing savage, are no less good mothers for their human strength. But our civilized “feminine delicacy,” which appears somewhat less delicate when recognized as an expression of sexuality in excess﻿—makes us no better mothers, but worse. The relative weakness of women is a sex-distinction. It is apparent in her to a degree that injures motherhood, that injures wifehood, that injures the individual. The sex-usefulness and the human usefulness of women, their general duty to their kind, are greatly injured by this degree of distinction. In every way the oversexed condition of the human female reacts unfavorably upon herself, her husband, her children, and the race.

===Chinese===
妇女普遍存在的那种虚弱与笨拙的程度，那种在站立、行走、奔跑、跳跃、攀爬以及从事两性共有的其他种族功能上的相对无能，是一种过度的性别区分；而这种相对的虚弱随后传给她们的子女——男孩女孩概莫能外——便迟滞了人类的发展。强壮、自由、活跃的妇女——健壮的、在田间劳作的农妇，负重的部落妇女——并不因她们为人的力量而稍减其为好母亲。但我们文明社会中那种「女性的娇弱」——一旦被认出乃是性征过度的一种表现，它便显得不那么娇弱了——并不会使我们成为更好的母亲，反而更坏。妇女这种相对的虚弱是一种性别区分。它在女人身上显著到这样的程度：戕害母职，戕害妻职，戕害个体。妇女的性效用与人的效用，她们对同类的普遍义务，都被这一程度的区分严重损害。人类雌性这种过度性化的状态，以一切方式反过来损害着她自身、她的丈夫、她的子女和种族。

===Original===
In its psychic manifestation this intense sex-distinction is equally apparent. The primal instinct of sex-attraction has developed under social forces into a conscious passion of enormous power, a deep and lifelong devotion, overwhelming in its force. This is excessive in both sexes, but more so in women than in men﻿—not so commonly in its simple physical form, but in the unreasoning intensity of emotion that refuses all guidance, and drives those possessed by it to risk every other good for this one end. It is not at first sight easy, and it may seem an irreverent and thankless task, to discriminate here between what is good in the “master passion” and what is evil, and especially to claim for one sex more of this feeling than for the other; but such discrimination can be made.

===Chinese===
在心理表现上，这种强烈的性别区分同样显而易见。性吸引（sex-attraction）的原初本能在社会力量的作用下，发展成一种威力惊人的有意识的激情，一种深沉而终身的眷恋，其力量势不可当。这种激情在两性身上都有过度，但在女人身上甚于男人——倒不常以其单纯的肉体形式出现，而是表现为那种拒纳一切引导的、无理智的情感强度，它驱使被它攫住的人为这唯一的目的而拿其他一切善去冒险。在此分辨「主宰激情」（master passion）之中何者为善、何者为恶，尤其还要宣称这种情感在某一性别身上多于另一性别，乍看并非易事，甚或显得是一桩不敬而不讨好的差事；但这样的分辨是可以做出的。

===Original===
