It is good for the individual and for the race to have developed such a degree of passionate and permanent love as shall best promote the happiness of individuals and the reproduction of species. It is not good for the race or for the individual that this feeling should have become so intense as to override all other human faculties, to make a mock of the accumulated wisdom of the ages, the stored power of the will; to drive the individual﻿—against his own plain conviction﻿—into a union sure to result in evil, or to hold the individual helpless in such an evil union, when made.

===Chinese===
发展出那样一种程度的炽热而恒久的爱，使其最能促进个体的幸福与物种的繁衍，这对个体和种族都是好事。这种情感竟变得如此强烈，以致压倒其他一切人类官能，嘲弄世代积累的智慧与意志蓄积的力量，以致驱使个体——违逆其明白的确信——走入一桩必生祸患的结合，或在一桩这样的祸患结合既成之后，使个体无助地困守其中，这对种族或对个体都不是好事。

===Original===
Such is the condition of humanity, involving most evil results to its offspring and to its own happiness. And, while in men the immediate dominating force of the passion may be more conspicuous, it is in women that it holds more universal sway. For the man has other powers and faculties in full use, whereby to break loose from the force of this; and the woman, specially modified to sex and denied racial activity, pours her whole life into her love, and, if injured here, she is injured irretrievably. With him it is frequently light and transient, and, when most intense, often most transient. With her it is a deep, all-absorbing force, under the action of which she will renounce all that life offers, take any risk, face any hardships, bear any pain. It is maintained in her in the face of a lifetime of neglect and abuse. The common instance of the police court trials﻿—the woman cruelly abused who will not testify against her husband﻿—shows this. This devotion, carried to such a degree as to lead to the mismating of individuals with its personal and social injury, is an excessive sex-distinction.

===Chinese===
人类的处境便是如此，给其后代、也给其自身的幸福带来极坏的后果。而且，虽然激情那种直接的支配力量在男人身上或许更为触目，但持有更普遍统治的，却是在女人身上。因为男人还有其他的能力与官能在充分使用之中，可以借以挣脱这种力量；而女人被特别地改变得偏于性、又被剥夺了种族活动，便把整个生命倾注进她的爱情，一旦在此受创，便受损得无可挽回。在他身上，这激情常常是轻浅而短暂的，而且往往最炽烈时也最短暂。在她身上，它却是一股深沉的、吞没一切的力量，在它的作用下，她肯舍弃人生所能给予的一切，肯冒任何风险，肯面对任何艰辛，肯忍受任何痛苦。纵使遭逢一生的冷落与摧残，这感情在她身上仍维系不坠。治安法庭庭审中常见的例证——那个惨遭虐待却不肯指证丈夫的女人——正表明了这一点。这种眷恋达到如此程度，以致把个体引向错误的婚配，并带来个人的与社会的损害，便是一种过度的性别区分。

===Original===
But it is in our common social relations that the predominance of sex-distinction in women is made most manifest. The fact that, speaking broadly, women have, from the very beginning, been spoken of expressively enough as “the sex,” demonstrates clearly that this is the main impression which they have made upon observers and recorders. Here one need attempt no farther proof than to turn the mind of the reader to an unbroken record of facts and feelings perfectly patent to everyone, but not hitherto looked at as other than perfectly natural and right. So utterly has the status of woman been accepted as a sexual one that it has remained for the woman’s movement of the nineteenth century to devote much contention to the claim that women are persons! That women are persons as well as females﻿—an unheard of proposition!

===Chinese===
但是，正是在我们普遍的社会关系中，性别区分在女人身上的支配地位才表现得最为明显。大体而论，女人从一开始便被相当传神地径称为「性」（the sex），这一事实清楚地表明：这正是她们给观察者与记录者留下的主要印象。在此，除了请读者把心思转向那份连绵不断的、对人人而言都再明白不过的事实与感受的记录——只是迄今为止，人们从未把它看作别的，而只看作完全自然而正当——之外，无须再试图举出更多的证明。女人的地位被如此彻底地当作一种性的地位来接受，以致直到十九世纪的妇女运动，还须为「女人是人」这一主张耗费大量争辩！女人不仅是雌性，而且是人——一个闻所未闻的命题！

===Original===
In a *Handbook of Proverbs of All Nations*, a collection comprising many thousands, these facts are to be observed: first, that the proverbs concerning women are an insignificant minority compared to those concerning men; second, that the proverbs concerning women almost invariably apply to them in general﻿—to the sex. Those concerning men qualify, limit, describe, specialize. It is “a lazy man,” “a violent man,” “a man in his cups.” Qualities and actions are predicated of man individually, and not as a sex, unless he is flatly contrasted with woman, as in “A man of straw is worth a woman of gold,” “Men are deeds, women are words,” or “Man, woman, and the devil are the three degrees of comparison.” But of woman it is always and only “a woman,” meaning simply a female, and recognizing no personal distinction: “As much pity to see a woman weep as to see a goose go barefoot.” “He that hath an eel by the tail and a woman by her word hath a slippery handle.” “A woman, a spaniel, and a walnut-tree﻿—the more you beat ’em, the better they be.” Occasionally a distinction is made between “a fair woman” and “a black woman”; and Solomon’s “virtuous woman,” who commanded such a high price, is familiar to us all. But in common thought it is simply “a woman” always. The boast of the profligate that he knows “the sex,” so recently expressed by a new poet﻿—“The things you will learn from the Yellow and Brown, they’ll ’elp you an’ ’eap with the White”; the complaint of the angry rejected that “all women are just alike!”﻿—the consensus of public opinion of all time goes to show that the characteristics common to the sex have predominated over the characteristics distinctive of the individual﻿—a marked excess in sex-distinction.

===Chinese===
在一部收录数千条谚语的《各国谚语手册》（*Handbook of Proverbs of All Nations*）中，可以看到这样一些事实：其一，与关于男人的谚语相比，关于女人的谚语只占微不足道的少数；其二，关于女人的谚语几乎毫无例外地把她们当作一个整体来讲——当作「性」。而关于男人的谚语则加以限定、约束、描摹、专门化。说的是「一个懒惰的男人」「一个粗暴的男人」「一个酗酒的男人」。品质与行为是被断言于男人这个个体的，而不是把他当作一个性别，除非把他与女人截然对举，如「一个稻草男人抵得上一个金子女人」「男人是行动，女人是言辞」，或「男人、女人和魔鬼是三个比较级」。而关于女人，则永远只是、仅仅是「一个女人」，意思无非一个雌性，不承认任何个人的差别：「见女人哭泣之可悯，犹如见鹅赤脚走路。」「手攥着鳗鱼尾巴又攥住女人言语的人，握着的是滑溜的把柄。」「女人、猎犬和胡桃树——越打越好。」偶尔也会在「白皙的女人」与「黝黑的女人」之间作一区分；所罗门那位身价如此之高的「贤德的妇人」，我们也都熟悉。但在通常的观念里，永远只是「一个女人」。放荡之徒夸口说他深谙「性」——一位新诗人新近如此表达：「你从黄种与棕种女人那里将学到的东西，会『帮』上你，也会同白种女人的经验『堆』作一堆」；求爱遭拒的愤懑者则抱怨「天下女人都一个样！」——古往今来舆论的一致之见表明：这一性别共有的特征压倒了个体独具的特征——性别区分上的显著过度。

===Original===
From the time our children are born, we use every means known to accentuate sex-distinction in both boy and girl; and the reason that the boy is not so hopelessly marked by it as the girl is that he has the whole field of human expression open to him besides. In our steady insistence on proclaiming sex-distinction we have grown to consider most human attributes as masculine attributes, for the simple reason that they were allowed to men and forbidden to women.

===Chinese===
从我们的孩子出生之时起，我们就使用一切已知的手段来强化男孩和女孩身上的性别区分；男孩之所以不似女孩那样被它无可救药地打上烙印，原因在于他此外还有整个人类表现的广阔天地向他敞开。在我们对宣示性别区分的一贯坚持中，我们已渐渐把大多数人类属性都视为男性属性，理由很简单：它们被允许给男人，而被禁止给女人。

===Original===
A clear and definite understanding of the difference between race-attributes and sex-attributes should be established. Life consists of action. The action of a living thing is along two main lines﻿—self-preservation and race-preservation. The processes that keep the individual alive, from the involuntary action of his internal organs to the voluntary action of his external organs﻿—every act, from breathing to hunting his food, which contributes to the maintenance of the individual life﻿—these are the processes of self-preservation. Whatever activities tend to keep the race alive, to reproduce the individual, from the involuntary action of the internal organs to the voluntary action of the external organs; every act from the development of germ-cells to the taking care of children, which contributes to the maintenance of the racial life﻿—these are the processes of race-preservation. In race-preservation, male and female have distinctive organs, distinctive functions, distinctive lines of action. In self-preservation, male and female have the same organs, the same functions, the same lines of action. In the human species our processes of race-preservation have reached a certain degree of elaboration; but our processes of self-preservation have gone farther, much farther.

===Chinese===
应当对种族属性（race-attributes）与性属性（sex-attributes）之间的差别建立清晰而明确的理解。生命在于行动。生物的行动沿两条主线进行——自我保存（self-preservation）与种族保存（race-preservation）。维持个体存活的那些过程，从内脏器官的不随意动作到外部器官的随意动作——从呼吸到猎取食物，凡有助于维持个体生命的每一行为——这些都是自我保存的过程。凡趋向于使种族存活、使个体繁衍的活动，从内脏器官的不随意动作到外部器官的随意动作；从生殖细胞的发育到照料孩子，凡有助于维持种族生命的每一行为——这些都是种族保存的过程。在种族保存上，雄性与雌性各有独特的器官、独特的功能、独特的行动路线。在自我保存上，雄性与雌性拥有相同的器官、相同的功能、相同的行动路线。在人类物种中，我们的种族保存诸过程已达到某种精巧的程度；但我们的自我保存诸过程走得更远，远得多。

===Original===
All the varied activities of economic production and distribution, all our arts and industries, crafts and trades, all our growth in science, discovery, government, religion﻿—these are along the line of self-preservation: these are, or should be, common to both sexes. To teach, to rule, to make, to decorate, to distribute﻿—these are not sex-functions: they are race-functions. Yet so inordinate is the sex-distinction of the human race that the whole field of human progress has been considered a masculine prerogative. What could more absolutely prove the excessive sex-distinction of the human race? That this difference should surge over all its natural boundaries and blazon itself across every act of life, so that every step of the human creature is marked “male” or “female,”﻿—surely, this is enough to show our oversexed condition.

===Chinese===
经济生产与分配的种种活动，我们的一切技艺与产业、手艺与行当，我们在科学、发现、政府、宗教上的全部发展——这些都沿着自我保存的路线：这些活动是、或者说应当是两性共有的。教书、治理、制作、装饰、分配——这些都不是性功能：它们是种族功能。然而人类种族的性别区分竟如此无度，以至于人类进步的整个领域都被当作男性的特权。还有什么能比这更彻底地证明人类种族过度的性别区分呢？这一差异竟漫过它的一切天然界限，招摇于人生的每一行为之上，使人类生灵的每一步都被标上「雄」或「雌」——诚然，这足以表明我们过度性化的状态了。

===Original===
Little by little, very slowly, and with most unjust and cruel opposition, at cost of all life holds most dear, it is being gradually established by many martyrdoms that human work is woman’s as well as man’s. Harriet Martineau must conceal her writing under her sewing when callers came, because “to sew” was a feminine verb, and “to write” a masculine one. Mary Somerville must struggle to hide her work from even relatives, because mathematics was a “masculine” pursuit. Sex has been made to dominate the whole human world﻿—all the main avenues of life marked “male,” and the female left to be a female, and nothing else.

===Chinese===
一点一点地、极其缓慢地、顶着最不公正而残酷的反对，以人生最珍视的一切为代价，经由许许多多的殉道，人的工作既是男人的、也是女人的这一事实正逐渐得到确立。哈丽雅特·马蒂诺（Harriet Martineau）在客人来访时必须把她的写作藏到针线活底下，因为「缝纫」是一个女性化的动词，而「写作」是一个男性化的动词。玛丽·萨默维尔（Mary Somerville）必须竭力把她的工作瞒过甚至连亲人，因为数学是一种「男性化的」追求。性被弄成了支配整个人类世界的东西——人生的一切主要大道都被标上「雄」，而雌性则被撇在一边，只许做一头雌性，此外什么也不是。

===Original===
But while with the male the things he fondly imagined to be “masculine” were merely human, and very good for him, with the female the few things marked “feminine” were feminine, indeed; and her ceaseless reiterance of one short song, however sweet, has given it a conspicuous monotony. In garments whose main purpose is unmistakably to announce her sex; with a tendency to ornament which marks exuberance of sex-energy, with a body so modified to sex as to be grievously deprived of its natural activities; with a manner and behavior wholly attuned to sex-advantage, and frequently most disadvantageous to any human gain; with a field of action most rigidly confined to sex-relations; with her overcharged sensibility, her prominent modesty, her “eternal femininity,”﻿—the female of genus *homo* is undeniably oversexed.

===Chinese===
然而，在男人那里，那些被他天真地想象为「男性化的」东西其实不过是人的东西，而且于他大有好处；在女人这里，那少数被标为「女性化的」东西却确乎是雌性的；她把一支短短的歌吟唱个不停，无论多么甜美，也已使它带上一种刺眼的单调。衣裳的主要用途明白无误是宣示她的性别；一种标志着性能量过剩泛滥的装饰倾向；一个被改变得如此偏于性、以致被悲惨地剥夺了种种自然活动的身体；一种完全调谐于性之利益的举止与行为，而且往往对任何为人的收益最为不利；一片被最严苛地禁锢于性关系之内的行动天地；再加上她那过度充盈的敏感、她那突出的羞怯、她那「永恒女性」——人属（genus *homo*）的雌性无可否认地是过度性化的。

===Original===
This excessive distinction shows itself again in a marked precocity of development. Our little children, our very babies, show signs of it when the young of other creatures are serenely asexual in general appearance and habit. We eagerly note this precocity. We are proud of it. We carefully encourage it by precept and example, taking pains to develop the sex-instinct in little children, and think no harm. One of the first things we force upon the child’s dawning consciousness is the fact that he is a boy or that she is a girl, and that, therefore, each must regard everything from a different point of view. They must be dressed differently, not on account of their personal needs, which are exactly similar at this period, but so that neither they, nor anyone beholding them, may for a moment forget the distinction of sex.

===Chinese===
这种过度的区分再一次显现在显著的发展早熟上。我们幼小的孩子、我们小小的婴儿已显出这种迹象，而此时其他生物的幼崽在外观与习性上还是一派安然的雌雄莫辨。我们热切地留意这种早熟。我们引以为豪。我们言传身教、悉心鼓励，着意在小孩子身上发展性的本能，并且不以为有何妨害。我们强加给孩子初萌意识的事情，最早的就有这一件：他是男孩，或她是女孩；因此，两人必须各自从不同的观点看待一切事物。他们必须穿得不同，这不是出于他们个人的需要——这个时期两者的需要毫无二致——而是为了使他们自己、也使任何看到他们的人，一刻也不忘记性别的区分。

===Original===
Our peculiar inversion of the usual habit of species, in which the male carries ornament and the female is dark and plain, is not so much a proof of excess indeed, as a proof of the peculiar reversal of our position in the matter of sex-selection. With the other species the males compete in ornament, and the females select. With us the females compete in ornament, and the males select. If this theory of sex-ornament is disregarded, and we prefer rather to see in masculine decoration merely a form of exuberant sex-energy, expending itself in nonproductive excess, then, indeed, the fact that with us the females manifest such a display of gorgeous adornment is another sign of excessive sex-distinction. In either case the forcing upon girl-children of an elaborate ornamentation which interferes with their physical activity and unconscious freedom, and fosters a premature sex-consciousness, is as clear and menacing a proof of our condition as could be mentioned. That the girl-child should be so dressed as to require a difference in care and behavior, resting wholly on the fact that she is a girl﻿—a fact not otherwise present to her thought at that age﻿—is a precocious insistence upon sex-distinction, most unwholesome in its results. Boys and girls are expected, also, to behave differently to each other, and to people in general﻿—a behavior to be briefly described in two words. To the boy we say, “Do”; to the girl, “Don’t.” The little boy must “take care” of the little girl, even if she is larger than he is. “Why?” he asks. Because he is a boy. Because of sex. Surely, if she is the stronger, she ought to take care of him, especially as the protective instinct is purely feminine in a normal race. It is not long before the boy learns his lesson. He is a boy, going to be a man; and that means all. “I thank the Lord that I was not born a woman,” runs the Hebrew prayer. She is a girl, “only a girl,” “nothing but a girl,” and going to be a woman﻿—only a woman. Boys are encouraged from the beginning to show the feelings supposed to be proper to their sex. When our infant son bangs about, roars, and smashes things, we say proudly that he is “a regular boy!” When our infant daughter coquettes with visitors, or wails in maternal agony because her brother has broken her doll, whose sawdust remains she nurses with piteous care, we say proudly that “she is a perfect little mother already!” What business has a little girl with the instincts of maternity? No more than the little boy should have with the instincts of paternity. They are sex-instincts, and should not appear till the period of adolescence. The most normal girl is the “tomboy,”﻿—whose numbers increase among us in these wiser days﻿—a healthy young creature, who is human through and through, not feminine till it is time to be. The most normal boy has calmness and gentleness as well as vigor and courage. He is a human creature as well as a male creature, and not aggressively masculine till it is time to be. Childhood is not the period for these marked manifestations of sex. That we exhibit them, that we admire and encourage them, shows our oversexed condition.

===Chinese===
我们把物种的惯常习性来了个奇特的倒转——惯常是雄性披挂装饰、雌性暗淡朴素——与其说这是过度的证明，不如说这是我们在性选择（sex-selection）问题上位置奇特颠倒的证明。在其他物种中，雄性以装饰相竞，雌性施行挑选。在我们这里，雌性以装饰相竞，雄性施行挑选。如果不理会这种性装饰的理论，而宁愿在雄性的装扮中仅仅看出性能量的一种形式、一种以无所生产的过度发泄自身的形式，那么，在我们这里雌性竟展现出如此华丽盛装这一事实，便又是过度的性别区分的一个表征。无论取哪一种解释，把一种妨碍女孩身体活动与浑然不觉的自由、又助长早熟性意识的繁复装饰强加给女孩们，都是对我们这种状态所能举出的再清楚、再咄咄逼人不过的证明。女孩竟要穿得需要在照料与举止上都区别对待，而这区别全然立足于她是女孩这一事实——一个在她那个年纪原本不会进入她心头的事实——这是一种对性别区分的超前坚持，其后果极不健康。男孩与女孩还被期望彼此相处有别、对众人也有别——这种行为可以简括成两个词。对男孩，我们说「去做」；对女孩，我们说「别做」。小男孩必须「照顾」小女孩，哪怕她比他高大。「为什么？」他问。因为他是男孩。因为性别。诚然，倘若她更强壮，理当由她来照顾他，何况在一个正常的种族里，保护的本能纯属雌性。用不了多久，男孩便学会了这一课。他是男孩，将来要做男人；而这就意味着一切。「我感谢主，幸而没有生为女人」，希伯来人的祈祷如是说。她是女孩，「只是个女孩」「无非是个女孩」，将来要做女人——只是个女人。男孩从一开始便受到鼓励，去表现那些被认为合乎其性别的情感。当我们的小儿子四处乱撞、大声吼叫、摔打东西时，我们自豪地说他「真是个地道的男孩」！当我们的小女儿向客人撒娇作态，或因哥哥弄坏了她的布娃娃而发出母亲般的痛苦哀哭、又满怀楚楚可怜的细心看护那残剩的锯末时，我们自豪地说她「已经是个十足的小母亲了」！一个小女孩与生育的本能有什么相干？一如小男孩与为父的本能不相干那样，半点也没有。它们是性的本能，不到青春期就不该出现。最正常的女孩是「假小子」——在这些更明智的日子里，她们在我们中间日益增多——一个健康的幼小生灵，彻头彻尾是人的，不到该女性化的时候便不女性化。最正常的男孩既有活力与勇气，也有沉静与温柔。他既是雄性的生灵，也是人的生灵，不到该男性化的时候便不咄咄逼人地男性化。童年不是这些显著的性表现的时期。我们既展示它们，又赞赏并鼓励它们，这正表明了我们过度性化的状态。

## IV / 四

===Original===
Having seen the disproportionate degree of sex-distinction in humanity and its greater manifestation in the female than in the male, and having seen also the unique position of the human female as an economic dependant on the male of her species, it is not difficult to establish a relation between these two facts. The general law acting to produce this condition of exaggerated sex-development was briefly referred to in the second chapter. It is as follows: the natural tendency of any function to increase in power by use causes sex-activity to increase under the action of sexual selection. This tendency is checked in most species by the force of natural selection, which diverts the energies into other channels and develops race-activities. Where the female finds her economic environment in the male, and her economic advantage is directly conditioned upon the sex-relation, the force of natural selection is added to the force of sexual selection, and both together operate to develop sex-activity. In any animal species, free from any other condition, such a relation would have inevitably developed sex to an inordinate degree, as may be readily seen in the comparatively similar cases of those insects where the female, losing economic activity and modified entirely to sex, becomes a mere egg-sac, an organism with no powers of self-preservation, only those of race-preservation. With these insects the only race-problem is to maintain and reproduce the species, and such a condition is not necessarily evil; but with a race like ours, whose development as human creatures is but comparatively begun, it is evil because of its check to individual and racial progress. There are other purposes before us besides mere maintenance and reproduction.

===Chinese===
既已看到人类的性别区分（sex-distinction）已达到失衡的程度，且其在女性身上的显现甚于男性；又已看到人类女性作为本物种雄性的经济依附者（economic dependant）所处的独特地位，那么，在这两个事实之间确立一种关系便非难事。造成这种夸张的性发展（sex-development）状况的普遍法则，已在第二章中简略提及。其内容如下：任何功能都因使用而趋于增强，这一自然倾向使性活动（sex-activity）在性选择（sexual selection）的作用下不断增长。而在大多数物种中，这一倾向受到自然选择（natural selection）之力的遏制，后者把能量导入其他渠道，发展出种种种族活动。在雌性于雄性身上找到自己的经济环境（economic environment）、其经济利益直接以性关系（sex-relation）为条件之处，自然选择之力便叠加于性选择之力之上，二者协同作用以发展性活动。在任何一个不受其他条件约束的动物物种中，这样的关系必然会把性发展到无度的地步——这一点可以在那些情况大体相近的昆虫身上顺而得见：它们的雌性丧失了经济活动能力，被完全改变得专适于性，沦为一个纯粹的卵囊，一种全无自我保存之力、唯有种族保存之力的有机体。对这些昆虫而言，唯一的种族问题便是维持并繁殖物种，这样的状况未必是恶；但对一个像我们这样、其作为人的发展才刚刚起步的种族而言，它是恶的，因为它遏制了个体与种族的进步。在我们面前，除了单纯的维持与繁殖之外，尚有别的目的。

===Original===
It should be clear to anyone accustomed to the working of biological laws that all the tendencies of a living organism are progressive in their development, and are held in check by the interaction of their several forces. Each living form, with its dominant characteristics, represents a balance of power, a sort of compromise. The size of earth’s primeval monsters was limited by the tensile strength of their material. Sea monsters can be bigger, because the medium in which they move offers more support. Birds must be smaller for the opposite reason. The cow requires many stomachs of a liberal size, because her food is of low nutritive value; and she must eat large quantities to keep her machine going. The size of arboreal animals, such as monkeys or squirrels, is limited by the nature of their habitat: creatures that live in trees cannot be so big as creatures that live on the ground. Every quality of every creature is relative to its condition, and tends to increase or decrease accordingly; and each quality tends to increase in proportion to its use, and to decrease in proportion to its disuse. Primitive man and his female were animals, like other animals. They were strong, fierce, lively beasts; and she was as nimble and ferocious as he, save for the added belligerence of the males in their sex-competition. In this competition, he, like the other male creatures, fought savagely with his hairy rivals; and she, like the other female creatures, complacently viewed their struggles, and mated with the victor. At other times she ran about in the forest, and helped herself to what there was to eat as freely as he did.

===Chinese===
凡惯于体会生物学法则之运行的人，都应当看清：生命有机体的一切倾向在其发展中都是递进的，又受诸种力量相互作用的制约而保持平衡。每一种生命形态连同其主导特征，都代表着一种力量的平衡，一种折中。地球上远古巨兽的体型，受其躯体材料的抗拉强度所限。海中巨兽可以更大，因为它们赖以活动的介质提供更多的支撑。鸟类则必须更小，缘由恰恰相反。母牛需要好几个宽大的胃，因为它的食物营养价值低；它必须大量进食，才能让它的机体运转不停。树栖动物如猴类或松鼠的体型，受其栖居地性质的制约：栖于树上的动物，不可能像栖于地上的动物那样大。每一造物的每一品质皆相对于其处境，并随之趋增或趋减；而每一品质又随其使用而趋增，随其废弃而趋减。原始人及其雌性是动物，同其他动物一样。他们是强壮、凶猛、生气勃勃的兽类；她同他一样敏捷而凶悍，只是雄性在性竞争（sex-competition）中平添了几分好斗。在这种竞争中，他像其他雄性动物一样，与其毛茸茸的对手凶猛相搏；她则像其他雌性动物一样，心满意足地旁观他们的争斗，与胜者交配。在其余的时日，她在林间四处奔走，同他一样自由地取食于林间所有可吃之物。

===Original===
There seems to have come a time when it occurred to the dawning intelligence of this amiable savage that it was cheaper and easier to fight a little female, and have it done with, than to fight a big male every time. So he instituted the custom of enslaving the female; and she, losing freedom, could no longer get her own food nor that of her young. The mother ape, with her maternal function well fulfilled, flees leaping through the forest﻿—plucks her fruit and nuts, keeps up with the movement of the tribe, her young one on her back or held in one strong arm. But the mother woman, enslaved, could not do this. Then man, the father, found that slavery had its obligations: he must care for what he forbade to care for itself, else it died on his hands. So he slowly and reluctantly shouldered the duties of his new position. He began to feed her, and not only that, but to express in his own person the thwarted uses of maternity: he had to feed the children, too. It seems a simple arrangement. When we have thought of it at all, we have thought of it with admiration. The naturalist defends it on the ground of advantage to the species through the freeing of the mother from all other cares and confining her unreservedly to the duties of maternity. The poet and novelist, the painter and sculptor, the priest and teacher, have all extolled this lovely relation. It remains for the sociologist, from a biological point of view, to note its effects on the constitution of the human race, both in the individual and in society.

===Chinese===
似乎曾有那么一个时刻，这位性情温厚的野蛮人，其初醒的智识忽然想到：打一顿小小的雌性便一了百了，比起每次都去同大个子雄性搏斗，要更便宜也更省事。于是他开创了奴役雌性的习俗；她既失了自由，便再不能为自己弄到食物，也不能为幼崽弄到食物。母猿充分履行着生育的机能（function of maternity），跳跃着穿行林间——采摘自己的果实与坚果，跟上部落的迁徙，幼崽或伏在她背上，或被她一只强劲的手臂抱着。而被奴役的母亲女人却做不到这些。于是男人、这位父亲发现，奴役自有其义务：他必须照料那被他禁止自我照料者，否则它便死在他手里。于是他缓慢而不情愿地担起了新位置的职责。他开始喂养她；而且不仅如此，还要以自己的亲身来表达被挫抑的生育机能之用途：他不得不连带喂养孩子们。这看来是一种简单的安排。我们每当想起它，总是怀着赞美。博物学家为它辩护，理由是这于物种有利：母亲由此摆脱一切其他操劳，被毫无保留地限于生育（maternity）的职责。诗人与小说家、画家与雕塑家、祭司与教师，无不颂扬这可爱的关系。剩下的，便是由社会学家从生物学的观点出发，记下它对人类种族的构成在个体与社会两个层面的效应。

===Original===
When man began to feed and defend woman, she ceased proportionately to feed and defend herself. When he stood between her and her physical environment, she ceased proportionately to feel the influence of that environment and respond to it. When he became her immediate and all-important environment, she began proportionately to respond to this new influence and to be modified accordingly. In a free state, speed was of as great advantage to the female as to the male, both in enabling her to catch prey and in preventing her from being caught by enemies; but, in her new condition, speed was a disadvantage. She was not allowed to do the catching, and it profited her to be caught by her new master. Free creatures, getting their own food and maintaining their own lives, develop an active capacity for attaining their ends. Parasitic creatures, whose living is obtained by the exertions of others, develop powers of absorption and of tenacity﻿—the powers by which they profit most. The human female was cut off from the direct action of natural selection, that mighty force which heretofore had acted on male and female alike with inexorable and beneficial effect, developing strength, developing skill, developing endurance, developing courage﻿—in a word, developing species. She now met the influence of natural selection acting indirectly through the male, and developing, of course, the faculties required to secure and obtain a hold on him. Needless to state that these faculties were those of sex-attraction, the one power that has made him cheerfully maintain, in what luxury he could, the being in whom he delighted. For many, many centuries she had no other hold, no other assurance of being fed. The young girl had a prospective value, and was maintained for what should follow; but the old woman, in more primitive times, had but a poor hold on life. She who could best please her lord was the favorite slave or favorite wife, and she obtained the best economic conditions.

===Chinese===
当男人开始供养并护卫女人，她便相应地不再供养并护卫自己。当他立于她与她的物质环境之间，她便相应地不再感受那环境的影响并对其作出回应。当他成为她切近而至关重要的环境，她便开始相应地回应这一新的影响，并因之被改变。在自由状态下，速度之于雌性，同之于雄性一样大有益处：既使她得以捕捉猎物，又使她免遭天敌捕食；但在她的新处境中，速度却成了不利。她不被允许去捕捉，而被她的新主人捕获反倒于她有利。自由的造物自取食物、自维生计，发展出达成目的的能动能力；寄生的造物，其生计得自他人的劳作，发展出的则是吸收之力与把持之力——它们借以获益最多的那些力量。人类女性就这样被切断了自然选择的直接作用——那股伟力此前一视同仁地作用于雌雄两性，以不可抗拒而有益的效应，发展着力量，发展着技巧，发展着耐力，发展着勇气——一言以蔽之，发展着物种。如今她所遭遇的，是经由男性间接施展的自然选择；它所发展的，自然便是赢得并抓紧男性所需的种种才能。无须说，这些才能便是性吸引（sex-attraction）的才能——正是这唯一的力量，使男人心甘情愿地在力所能及的奢华里，供养这个令他心悦的存在。许多许多个世纪里，她再无别的凭借，再无别的受养保证。年轻女子尚有一种预期的价值，为日后的用途而得到供养；而老妇，在更原始的时代，对生活的把握却可怜得很。最能取悦其主的她，便是得宠的奴隶或得宠的妻子，从而获得最好的经济境况。

===Original===
With the growth of civilization, we have gradually crystallized into law the visible necessity for feeding the helpless female; and even old women are maintained by their male relatives with a comfortable assurance. But to this day﻿—save, indeed, for the increasing army of women wage-earners, who are changing the face of the world by their steady advance toward economic independence﻿—the personal profit of women bears but too close a relation to their power to win and hold the other sex. From the odalisque with the most bracelets to the débutante with the most bouquets, the relation still holds good﻿—woman’s economic profit comes through the power of sex-attraction.

===Chinese===
随着文明的成长，我们把供养无助女性这一显而易见的必需，逐渐凝定为法律；就连老妇，如今也由男性亲眷供养着，且带有一份安适的保障。但直到今天——确实，除去那支日益壮大的挣工资的妇女大军，她们正以朝向经济独立的稳步前进而改变着世界的面貌——妇女的个人收益，与其赢得并留住异性的能力，关系仍嫌过于密切。从腕上镯环最多的姬妾（odalisque），到手中花束最多的初入社交界的少女（débutante），这一关系依然成立——女人的经济收益，经由性吸引的力量而来。

===Original===
When we confront this fact boldly and plainly in the open market of vice, we are sick with horror. When we see the same economic relation made permanent, established by law, sanctioned and sanctified by religion, covered with flowers and incense and all accumulated sentiment, we think it innocent, lovely, and right. The transient trade we think evil. The bargain for life we think good. But the biological effect remains the same. In both cases the female gets her food from the male by virtue of her sex-relationship to him. In both cases, perhaps even more in marriage because of its perfect acceptance of the situation, the female of genus *homo*, still living under natural law, is inexorably modified to sex in an increasing degree.

===Chinese===
当我们在罪恶的公开市场上大胆而直白地直面这一事实时，我们因恐怖而作呕。而当我们看到同一种经济关系被永久化、由法律确立、被宗教核准并祝圣、覆满鲜花与熏香及一切积攒下来的深情时，我们便认为它无邪、可爱而正当。一时的交易，我们认为是恶；终身的买卖，我们认为是善。但生物学上的效应并无二致。在这两种情形中，雌性都凭借她与雄性的性关系，从他那里获得食物。在这两种情形中——在婚姻中或许尤甚，因为它对这一处境全盘接受——人属（genus *homo*）的雌性虽仍生活于自然法则之下，却不可抗拒地被改变得在性上日益加深。

===Original===
Followed in specific detail, the action of the changed environment upon women has been in given instances as follows: In the matter of mere passive surroundings she has been immediately restricted in her range. This one factor has an immense effect on man and animal alike. An absolutely uniform environment, one shape, one size, one color, one sound, would render life, if any life could be, one helpless, changeless thing. As the environment increases and varies, the development of the creature must increase and vary with it; for he acquires knowledge and power, as the material for knowledge and the need for power appear. In migratory species the female is free to acquire the same knowledge as the male by the same means, the same development by the same experiences. The human female has been restricted in range from the earliest beginning. Even among savages, she has a much more restricted knowledge of the land she lives in. She moves with the camp, of course, and follows her primitive industries in its vicinity; but the warpath and the hunt are the man’s. He has a far larger habitat. The life of the female savage is freedom itself, however, compared with the increasing constriction of custom closing in upon the woman, as civilization advanced, like the iron torture chamber of romance. Its culmination is expressed in the proverb: “A woman should leave her home but three times﻿—when she is christened, when she is married, and when she is buried.” Or this: “The woman, the cat, and the chimney should never leave the house.” The absolutely stationary female and the wide-ranging male are distinctly human institutions, after we leave behind us such low forms of life as the gypsy moth, whose female seldom moves more than a few feet from the pupa moth. She has aborted wings, and cannot fly. She waits humbly for the winged male, lays her myriad eggs, and dies﻿—a fine instance of modification to sex.

===Chinese===
若循具体细节追索这改变了的环境对妇女的作用，诸事例如下：在纯粹的被动环境方面，她的活动范围立即受到了限制。单是这一个因素，对人与动物便都有莫大的影响。一个绝对划一的环境——一种形状、一种大小、一种颜色、一种声音——会把生命（倘若还能有生命）变成一件无助而不变的东西。环境愈益扩展、愈益多样，造物的发展也必随之扩展、多样；因为一旦出现知识的材料与力量的需要，他便获得知识与力量。在迁徙性物种中，雌性得以凭同样的方式获得与雄性同样的知识，凭同样的经历获得同样的发展。而人类女性的活动范围，却从最初的起点起便受到限制。即使在野蛮人中，她对自己所居之地的了解也远为有限。她当然随营而徙，在营地附近从事她的原始劳作；但征途与狩猎属于男人。他有着宽广得多的栖息之地。然而，与随着文明推进、如传奇小说中的铁刑室般步步围拢妇女的习俗紧缩相比，野蛮女性的生活已是自由本身。这种紧缩的极致凝结于谚语：「女人一生只应离家三次——受洗时、出嫁时、下葬时。」又如：「女人、猫和烟囱，永不可离家门。」绝对不动的雌性与驰骋四方的雄性，确凿是人类独有的制度——在我们把舞毒蛾（gypsy moth）这类低等生命形态抛诸身后之后。舞毒蛾的雌蛾极少挪到离蛹数步之遥以外。她的翅膀已然退化，不能飞翔。她谦卑地等待有翅的雄蛾，产下她无数的卵，而后死去——一个被改变得适于性的绝佳例证。

===Original===
To reduce so largely the mere area of environment is a great check to race-development; but it is not to be compared in its effects with the reduction in voluntary activity to which the human female has been subjected. Her restricted impression, her confinement to the four walls of the home, have done great execution, of course, in limiting her ideas, her information, her thought-processes, and power of judgment; and in giving a disproportionate prominence and intensity to the few things she knows about; but this is innocent in action compared with her restricted expression, the denial of freedom to act. A living organism is modified far less through the action of external circumstances upon it and its reaction thereto, than through the effect of its own exertions. Skin may be thickened gradually by exposure to the weather; but it is thickened far more quickly by being rubbed against something, as the handle of an oar or of a broom. To be surrounded by beautiful things has much influence upon the human creature: to make beautiful things has more. To live among beautiful surroundings and make ugly things is more directly lowering than to live among ugly surroundings and make beautiful things. What we do modifies us more than what is done to us. The freedom of expression has been more restricted in women than the freedom of impression, if that be possible. Something of the world she lived in she has seen from her barred windows. Some air has come through the purdah’s folds, some knowledge has filtered to her eager ears from the talk of men. Desdemona learned somewhat of Othello. Had she known more, she might have lived longer. But in the ever-growing human impulse to create, the power and will to make, to do, to express one’s new spirit in new forms﻿—here she has been utterly debarred. She might work as she had worked from the beginning﻿—at the primitive labors of the household; but in the inevitable expansion of even those industries to professional levels we have striven to hold her back. To work with her own hands, for nothing, in direct body-service to her own family﻿—this has been permitted﻿—yes, compelled. But to be and do anything further from this she has been forbidden. Her labor has not only been limited in kind, but in degree. Whatever she has been allowed to do must be done in private and alone, the firsthand industries of savage times.

===Chinese===
仅仅把环境的面积缩减至此，已是对种族发展的巨大遏制；但较之人类女性所遭受的自主活动的缩减，其效应犹不足论。她感受之受限、她被禁锢于家宅四壁之内，固然大奏杀伤之功——限制了她的观念、她的见闻、她的思想过程与判断力，并使她所知的寥寥数事获得失衡的显著与强度；但较之她表达之受限、行动自由之被剥夺，这些作用还算是无害的。一个生命有机体因外部环境的作用及其对此的回应而被改变的程度，远不及因自身劳作之效应而被改变的程度。皮肤可因风吹日晒而渐渐增厚；但让它与某物摩擦——如船桨之柄或扫帚之柄——则增厚得快得多。置身于美的事物之中，于人大有影响；亲手造出美的事物，影响更大。生活在美的环境中而制造丑的东西，比生活在丑的环境中而创造美的东西，更直接地使人堕落。我们所做的，比施加于我们的，更深地改变着我们。表达的自由在妇女身上所受的限制，甚于感受的自由——倘若这还有可能的话。她从装有栅栏的窗后，多少看见过一点她所生活的世界。有些空气透过帷帘（purdah）的褶层吹进来，有些知识从男人们的言谈中渗入她热切的耳朵。苔丝狄蒙娜（Desdemona）对奥赛罗（Othello）略有所知。倘若她知道得更多，或许还能活得更久。但在那不断生长的人类创造冲动之中——那制造、行动、以新形式表达自己新精神的意愿与力量——在此她遭到了彻底的禁绝。她可以劳作，像她自始便做的那样——做那些家务的原始劳动；但即便这些劳作不可避免地扩展至专门职业的水平，我们也力图把她拦在后面。用她自己的双手劳作，分文不取，直接以身体侍奉自己的家庭——这是被允许的——不，是被强制的。但凡要在此之外有所成为、有所作为，她便遭到禁止。她的劳动不仅在种类上受限，在程度上亦受限。凡她被允许去做的事，都必须私下地、独自地进行——那些野蛮时代的亲手劳作。

===Original===
Our growth in industry has been not only in kind, but in class. The baker is not in the same industrial grade with the house-cook, though both make bread. To specialize any form of labor is a step up: to organize it is another step. Specialization and organization are the basis of human progress, the organic methods of social life. They have been forbidden to women almost absolutely. The greatest and most beneficent change of this century is the progress of women in these two lines of advance. The effect of this check in industrial development, accompanied as it was by the constant inheritance of increased racial power, has been to intensify the sensations and emotions of women, and to develop great activity in the lines allowed. The nervous energy that up to present memory has impelled women to labor incessantly at something, be it the veriest folly of fancy work, is one mark of this effect.

===Chinese===
我们在产业上的成长，不仅在种类上，而且在等级上。面包师与家中的厨娘虽同为烤面包者，却不处于同一个产业等级。使任何一种劳动专门化，是上升的一步；把它组织起来，又是另一步。专门化与组织化是人类进步的基础，是社会生活的有机方法。它们对妇女几乎是被绝对禁止的。本世纪最伟大、最有益的变革，便是妇女在这两条前进路线上的进展。产业发展上的这一遏制——伴随它的又是不断增强的种族力量的持续遗传——其效应是强化了妇女的感受与情绪，并在被容许的路线上发展出高度的活动。直到今人记忆所及，那种驱使妇女不停地劳作于某事——哪怕是女红细工这等最无聊的营生——的神经能量，正是这一效应的一个标记。

===Original===
In religious development the same deadline has held back the growth of women through all the races and ages. In dim early times she was sharer in the mysteries and rites; but, as religion developed, her place receded, until Paul commanded her to be silent in the churches. And she has been silent until today. Even now, with all the ground gained, we have but the beginnings﻿—he slowly forced and disapproved beginnings﻿—of religious equality for the sexes. In some nations, religion is held to be a masculine attribute exclusively, it being even questioned whether women have souls. An early Christian council settled that important question by vote, fortunately deciding that they had. In a church whose main strength has always been derived from the adherence of women, it would have been an uncomfortable reflection not to have allowed them souls. Ancient family worship ran in the male line. It was the son who kept the sacred grandfathers in due respect, and poured libations to their shades. When the woman married, she changed her ancestors, and had to worship her husband’s progenitors instead of her own. This is why the Hindu and the Chinaman and many others of like stamp must have a son to keep them in countenance﻿—a deep-seated sex-prejudice, coming to slow extinction as women rise in economic importance.

===Chinese===
在宗教发展中，同一道死线在一切种族、一切时代都阻碍着妇女的成长。在朦胧的远古，她尚分享种种奥秘与仪式；但随着宗教的发展，她的位置步步退却，直到保罗（Paul）命令她在教会中缄默。而她直到今天仍在缄默。即使在现在，尽管已有种种进展，我们所有的也只是两性宗教平等的开端——那被缓慢逼成而又备受非议的开端。在一些民族中，宗教被认为纯属男性的属性，甚至有人质疑女人是否有灵魂。早期基督教的一次公会以表决裁定了这个重大问题，所幸判定她们有。在一个其主要力量始终得自妇女归附的教会里，倘若不许她们有灵魂，那将是一个令人难安的念头。古代的家族祭祀沿男性世系而行。是儿子对神圣的列祖列宗保持应有的敬奉，向他们的亡灵奠酒。女子出嫁，便改换了自己的祖先，须转而敬拜丈夫的先人，而非自己的先人。这便是印度人、中国人以及许多同一类型的人为何非有一个儿子不可、以撑持自己的颜面——一种根深蒂固的性别偏见，它将随着妇女经济重要性的上升而缓慢消亡。

===Original===
It is painfully interesting to trace the gradual cumulative effect of these conditions upon women: first, the action of large natural laws, acting on her as they would act on any other animal; then the evolution of social customs and laws (with her position as the active cause), following the direction of mere physical forces, and adding heavily to them; then, with increasing civilization, the unbroken accumulation of precedent, burnt into each generation by the growing force of education, made lovely by art, holy by religion, desirable by habit; and, steadily acting from beneath, the unswerving pressure of economic necessity upon which the whole structure rested. These are strong modifying conditions, indeed.

===Chinese===
追索这些状况加诸妇女身上的渐进累积效应，是一件令人痛苦而又有兴味的事：首先，是重大自然法则的作用，它们作用于她，一如作用于任何其他动物；其次，是社会习俗与法律的演化（以她的地位为动因），它们循着单纯物质力量的方向，又给这些力量大大加码；再次，是随着文明的增进，先例的累积不曾间断，被日益增强的教育之力烙进每一代人，被艺术装点得可爱，被宗教祝圣得神圣，被习惯烘托得可欲；最后，是在下方稳定作用的、毫不动摇的经济必需的压力——整座结构正奠基于其上。这些，确实是强有力的塑造条件。

===Original===
The process would have been even more effective and far less painful but for one important circumstance. Heredity has no Salic law. Each girl child inherits from her father a certain increasing percentage of human development, human power, human tendency; and each boy as well inherits from his mother the increasing percentage of sex-development, sex-power, sex-tendency. The action of heredity has been to equalize what every tendency of environment and education made to differ. This has saved us from such a female as the gypsy moth. It has held up the woman, and held down the man. It has set iron bounds to our absurd effort to make a race with one sex a million years behind the other. But it has added terribly to the pain and difficulty of human life﻿—a difficulty and a pain that should have taught us long since that we were living on wrong lines. Each woman born, re-humanized by the current of race activity carried on by her father and re-womanized by her traditional position, has had to live over again in her own person the same process of restriction, repression, denial; the smothering “no” which crushed down all her human desires to create, to discover, to learn, to express, to advance. Each woman has had, on the other hand, the same single avenue of expression and attainment; the same one way in which alone she might do what she could, get what she might. All other doors were shut, and this one always open; and the whole pressure of advancing humanity was upon her. No wonder that young Daniel in the apocryphal tale proclaimed: “The king is strong! Wine is strong! But women are stronger!”

===Chinese===
倘若没有一项重要的情况，这一过程本会更加有效，也远不那么痛苦。遗传没有萨利克法（Salic law）。每一个女童都从父亲那里继承了一份比例不断增加的人类发展、人类力量、人类倾向；每一个男童也同样从母亲那里继承比例不断增加的性发展、性力量、性倾向。遗传的作用，一直在于把环境与教育的一切倾向所造成的差异加以拉平。正是这一点，把我们从舞毒蛾那样的雌性中拯救出来。它托高了女人，也压低了男人。它给我们那种荒谬的努力——使一个性别落后另一个性别百万年而造出一个种族——设定了铁的界限。但它也使人类生活的痛苦与艰难大为加剧——这一重艰难与痛苦，本应早就教给我们：我们走的是错误的路。每一个出生的女人，既被父亲所承载的种族活动之流重新人化，又被她传统的位置重新女性化，都不得不在自己身上把同一套限制、压抑、否定的过程重新经历一遍；经历那把她的种种人性的欲望——创造的、发现的、学习的、表达的、前进的欲望——尽数压灭的窒息性的「不」。而另一方面，每一个女人又都拥有同一条唯一的表达与达成的途径；同一条她唯一能够尽其所能、取其所得的路。所有其他的门都关着，唯独这一扇永远敞开；而前进中的人类的全部压力，都压在她身上。难怪次经故事里的少年但以理（Daniel）宣称：「王是强有力的！酒是强有力的！但女人更强大！」

===Original===
To the young man confronting life the world lies wide. Such powers as he has he may use, must use. If he chooses wrong at first, he may choose again, and yet again. Not effective or successful in one channel, he may do better in another. The growing, varied needs of all mankind call on him for the varied service in which he finds his growth. What he wants to be, he may strive to be. What he wants to get, he may strive to get. Wealth, power, social distinction, fame﻿—what he wants he can try for.

===Chinese===
对于直面生活的青年男子，世界是一片开阔。他有什么样的能力，便可以使用，也必须使用。倘若起初选错了，他可以再选，一选再选。在这条渠道里不见成效，他可以在另一条里做得更好。全人类不断增长而多样的需要召唤着他，让他在多样化的服务中成就自己的成长。他想成为什么，便可以奋力成为什么。他想得到什么，便可以奋力得到什么。财富、权力、社会声望、名望——凡他想要的，都可以去争取。

===Original===
To the young woman confronting life there is the same world beyond, there are the same human energies and human desires and ambition within. But all that she may wish to have, all that she may wish to do, must come through a single channel and a single choice. Wealth, power, social distinction, fame﻿—not only these, but home and happiness, reputation, ease and pleasure, her bread and butter﻿—all, must come to her through a small gold ring. This is a heavy pressure. It has accumulated behind her through heredity, and continued about her through environment. It has been subtly trained into her through education, till she herself has come to think it a right condition, and pours its influence upon her daughter with increasing impetus. Is it any wonder that women are oversexed? But for the constant inheritance from the more human male, we should have been queen bees, indeed, long before this. But the daughter of the soldier and the sailor, of the artist, the inventor, the great merchant, has inherited in body and brain her share of his development in each generation, and so stayed somewhat human for all her femininity.

===Chinese===
而对于直面生活的青年女子，外面是同一个世界，内心是同样的人类精力、人类欲望与抱负。但她所愿拥有的一切、所愿做成的一切，都必须经由一条单一的渠道、一次单一的选择而来。财富、权力、社会声望、名望——不仅是这些，还有家庭与幸福、名誉、安逸与欢乐、她的衣食生计——这一切，都必须从一枚小小的金戒指中来到她身上。这是沉重的压力。它经遗传在她身后累积，经环境在她周身延续。它经教育被巧妙地训进她体内，直到她自己开始把它认作一种正当的状态，并以愈来愈猛的势头把这种影响倾注到她的女儿身上。女性被过度性化（oversexed），这难道还有什么奇怪的吗？若不是一代代不断承自那更具人性的男性的遗传，我们确实老早就变成蜂王（queen bee）了。但士兵与水手的女儿、艺术家、发明家、大商人的女儿，每一代都在身体与头脑中继承了父亲发展的一份，于是纵然一身女性气质，总算还保住了几分人性。

===Original===
All morbid conditions tend to extinction. One check has always existed to our inordinate sex-development﻿—nature’s ready relief, death. Carried to its furthest excess, the individual has died, the family has become extinct, the nation itself has perished, like Sodom and Gomorrah. Where one function is carried to unnatural excess, others are weakened, and the organism perishes. We are familiar with this in individual cases﻿—at least, the physician is. We can see it somewhat in the history of nations. From younger races, nearer savagery, nearer the healthful equality of pre-human creatures, has come each new start in history. Persia was older than Greece, and its highly differentiated sexuality had produced the inevitable result of enfeebling the racial qualities. The Greek commander stripped the rich robes and jewels from his Persian captives, and showed their unmanly feebleness to his men. “You have such bodies as these to fight for such plunder as this,” he said. In the country, among peasant classes, there is much less sex-distinction than in cities, where wealth enables the women to live in absolute idleness; and even the men manifest the same characteristics. It is from the country and the lower classes that the fresh blood pours into the cities, to be weakened in its turn by the influence of this unnatural distinction until there is none left to replenish the nation.

===Chinese===
一切病态的状况都趋向消亡。对于我们的无度性发展，一直存在着一重遏制——自然随手可得的宽解：死亡。推至极度的放纵时，个体死了，家族绝了嗣，民族本身也覆亡了，如所多玛（Sodom）与蛾摩拉（Gomorrah）那样。当一种功能被推向反常的过度，其他功能便衰弱下去，有机体便毁灭了。这在个体病例中是我们熟知的——至少医生是熟知的。在民族的历史中，我们也能略见一斑。历史上每一次新的起步，都来自更年轻的种族——更接近野蛮、更接近人类出现以前那些造物之健康平等的种族。波斯比希腊古老，其高度分化的性已使种族品质衰弱，产生了必然的结果。希腊将领从他波斯战俘身上剥下华美的长袍与珠宝，向他的士兵展示他们缺乏男子气的孱弱。「你们与之交战的，是这样的身体；你们为之夺取的，是这样的财物。」他说。在乡村、在农民阶层中，性别区分远少于城市；在城市里，财富使妇女得以生活在绝对的闲逸之中，就连男人也显出同样的特征。正是从乡村和下层阶级，新鲜的血液涌入城市，又轮到它被这反常区分的影响所削弱，直到再无新鲜血液来补充这个民族。

===Original===
The inevitable trend of human life is toward higher civilization; but, while that civilization is confined to one sex, it inevitably exaggerates sex-distinction, until the increasing evil of this condition is stronger than all the good of the civilization attained, and the nation falls. Civilization, be it understood, does not consist in the acquisition of luxuries. Social development is an organic development. A civilized State is one in which the citizens live in organic industrial relation. The more full, free, subtle, and easy that relation; the more perfect the differentiation of labor and exchange of product, with their correlative institutions﻿—the more perfect is that civilization. To eat, drink, sleep, and keep warm﻿—these are common to all animals, whether the animal couches in a bed of leaves or one of eiderdown, sleeps in the sun to avoid the wind or builds a furnace-heated house, lies in wait for game or orders a dinner at a hotel. These are but individual animal processes. Whether one lays an egg or a million eggs, whether one bears a cub, a kitten, or a baby, whether one broods its chickens, guards its litter, or tends a nursery full of children, these are but individual animal processes. But to serve each other more and more widely; to live only by such service; to develop special functions, so that we depend for our living on society’s return for services that can be of no direct use to ourselves﻿—this is civilization, our human glory and race-distinction.

===Chinese===
人类生活不可避免的趋向是走向更高的文明；但只要这文明局限于一个性别，它便不可避免地放大性别区分，直到这一状况日益加深的恶，强过所获文明的一切善，民族便倾覆了。须知，文明并不在于奢华之物的获取。社会的发展是一种有机的发展。一个文明的国家，是其公民生活在有机的产业关系之中的国家。这种关系愈充分、愈自由、愈精微、愈顺畅；劳动的分化与产品的交换，连同与之相辅的种种制度，愈完善——那文明便愈完善。吃、喝、睡、取暖——这些是一切动物共有的，无论这动物卧在枝叶铺成的床上还是鸭绒铺成的床上，无论它为避风而睡在阳光下还是建造一所火炉供暖的宅子，无论它潜伏伺猎还是到旅馆点一席晚餐。这些不过是个体的动物过程。产一枚卵还是产一百万枚卵，生一头幼兽、一只小猫还是一个婴孩，孵自己的小鸡、护自己的幼崽还是看护满满一育儿室的孩童——这些也不过是个体的动物过程。但是，愈来愈广泛地彼此服务；唯凭这样的服务而生活；发展出专门的职能，以至于我们的生计须仰赖社会对我们那些于我们自己并无直接用处的服务的回报——这才是文明，是我们人类的光荣与种族区分。

===Original===
All this human progress has been accomplished by men. Women have been left behind, outside, below, having no social relation whatever, merely the sex-relation, whereby they lived. Let us bear in mind that all the tender ties of family are ties of blood, of sex-relationship. A friend, a comrade, a partner﻿—this is a human relative. Father, mother, son, daughter, sister, brother, husband, wife﻿—these are sex-relatives. Blood is thicker than water, we say. True. But ties of blood are not those that ring the world with the succeeding waves of progressive religion, art, science, commerce, education, all that makes us human. Man is the human creature. Woman has been checked, starved, aborted in human growth; and the swelling forces of race-development have been driven back in each generation to work in her through sex-functions alone.

===Chinese===
所有这一切人类的进步，都是由男人完成的。妇女则被留在后面、留在外面、留在下面，没有任何社会关系，只有那赖以为生的性关系。让我们谨记：家庭中一切温柔的纽带，都是血缘的纽带、性亲缘的纽带。朋友、同志、合伙人——这些是人的亲缘。父、母、子、女、姊、妹、兄、弟、夫、妻——这些是性的亲缘。我们说，血浓于水。诚然。但血缘的纽带，并不是那些使进步的宗教、艺术、科学、商业、教育——使一切使我们成其为人的东西——以相继不绝的浪潮传遍世界的纽带。男人才是人的造物。女人在人的成长中被遏制、被饿馁、被摧折；而种族发展那日益涨满的力量，则在一辈又一辈中被驱赶回去，只能经由性功能单独在她身上做工。

===Original===
This is the way in which the sexuo-economic relation has operated in our species, checking race-development in half of us, and stimulating sex-development in both.

===Chinese===
这便是性-经济关系（sexuo-economic relation）在我们物种中发生作用的方式：它在我们一半人身上遏制种族发展，又在两性身上一同刺激性发展。

## IX / 九

===Original===
The main justification for the subjection of women, which is commonly advanced, is the alleged advantage to motherhood resultant from her extreme specialization to the uses of maternity under this condition.

===Chinese===
人们通常提出的、为妇女的屈从辩护的主要理由，是说在这种境况之下，她向着生育（maternity）之用途的极端专门化，会给母职（motherhood）带来所谓的好处。

===Original===
There are two weak points in this position. One is that the advantage to motherhood cannot be proved: the other, that it is not the uses of maternity to which she is specialized, but the uses of sex-indulgence. So far from the economic dependence of women working in the interests of motherhood, it is the steadily acting cause of a pathological maternity and a decreasing birthrate.

===Chinese===
这一立场有两处薄弱。其一，对母职的好处无法被证明；其二，她所专门化的，并不是生育的用途，而是性放纵的用途。妇女的经济依附（economic dependence）非但谈不上有益于母职，反倒是一种病态生育与出生率递减的持续起作用的因由。

===Original===
In simple early times there was a period when women were economically profited by childbearing; when, indeed, that was their sole use, and, failing it, they were entitled to no respect or profit whatever. Such a condition tended to increase the quantity of children, if not the quality. With industrial development and the increasing weight of economic cares upon the shoulders of the man, children come to be looked upon as a burden, and are dreaded instead of desired by the hard-worked father. They subtract from the family income; and the mother, absolutely dependent upon that income and also overworked in her position of unpaid house-servant, is not impelled to court maternity by any economic pressure. In the working classes—to which the great majority of people belong—the woman is by no means "segregated to the uses of maternity." Among the most intelligent and conscientious workingmen today there is a strong feeling against large families, and a consistent effort is made to prevent them.

===Chinese===
在简朴的早期时代，曾有一个时期，妇女靠生育而在经济上得利；那时，生育确实算得上她们唯一的用途，倘若不能尽这用途，她们便无权得到任何尊敬或好处。那样的境况倾向于增加孩子的数量——如果不是质量的话。随着产业的发展，随着经济操劳在男人肩头愈益沉重，孩子开始被看作负担，被劳苦过度的父亲所畏惧，而非所企盼。他们使家庭收入减少；而母亲呢，既绝对地依附于那笔收入，又在她无酬家内佣工的位置上劳苦过度，便没有任何经济压力驱使她去追求生育。在劳动阶级——即绝大多数人所属的阶级——中间，女人绝没有被「隔离专事生育之用」。在今日最有见识、最有良知的工人中间，存在着一股反对大家庭的强烈情绪，并且人们一贯地努力防止大家庭。

===Original===
Lest this be considered as not bearing directly upon the economic position of women, but rather on the general status of the working classes, let us examine the same condition among the wealthy. It is here that the economic dependence of women is carried to its extreme. The daughters and wives of the rich fail to perform even the domestic service expected of the women of poorer families. They are from birth to death absolutely nonproductive in goods or labor of economic value, and consumers of such goods and labor to an extent limited only by the purchasing power of their male relatives. In this condition the economic advantage of the woman, married or unmarried, not merely in food and clothes, but in such social advantage as she desires, lies in her power to attract and hold the devotion of men; and this power is not the power of maternity. On the contrary, maternity, by lowering the personal charms and occupying the time of the mother, fails to bring her the pleasure and profit obtainable by the woman who is not a mother. It is through the sex-relation minus its natural consequence that she profits most; and, therefore, the force of economic advantage acts against maternity instead of toward it.

===Chinese===
为免这被认为并非直接关乎妇女的经济处境，而只是关乎劳动阶级的一般状况，且让我们在富人中间考察同一境况。恰恰是在这里，妇女的经济依附被推到了极端。富人的女儿与妻子，连较贫困家庭的女性所被期待的那份家内仆役，也不必承担。她们从生到死，在具有经济价值的财物或劳动上绝对地无所产出，而对这类财物与劳动的消费，其程度只以其男性亲眷的购买力为限。在这种境况中，女人——无论已婚未婚——在经济上的好处，不只在衣食之上，还在她所想望的种种社会利益之上，皆系于她吸引并维系男人之爱慕的力量；而这力量并不是生育的力量。恰恰相反，生育由于减损母亲的个人魅力、占用母亲的时间，并不能给她带来那些不做母亲的女人所能得到的愉悦与好处。使她获利最丰的，是那抽去了自然后果的性关系（sex-relation）；因此，经济利益的力量所推动的，是逆着生育，而不是向着生育。

===Original===
In the last extreme this is clear to all in the full flower of the sexuo-economic relation—prostitution, than which nothing runs more absolutely counter to the improvement of the race through maternity. Specialization to uses of maternity, as in the queen bee, is one thing. Specialization to uses of sex without maternity is quite another. Yet this popular opinion, that we as a race are greatly benefited by having all our women saved from direct economic activity, and so allowed to concentrate all their energies on the beautiful work of motherhood, remains strong among us.

===Chinese===
在最极端处，这一点人人都看得清楚——它显现在性-经济关系（sexuo-economic relation）盛开的花朵即卖淫上面；没有什么比卖淫更绝对地逆于借生育改良种族了。专门化于生育之用途，如蜂王（queen bee）那样，是一回事；专门化于没有生育的性之用途，则完全是另一回事。然而，那种流行的意见——以为我们作为一个种族大大受益于让全部妇女免于直接的经济活动、从而得以把全部精力集中于母职这桩美好的工作——至今仍在我们中间根深蒂固。

===Original===
In *The Forum* for November, 1888, Lester F. Ward published a paper called "Our Better Halves," in which was clearly shown the biological supremacy of the female sex. This naturally aroused much discussion; and in an answering article, "Woman's Place in Nature" (*The Forum*, May, 1889), Mr. Grant Allen very thoroughly states the general view on this subject. He says of woman: "I believe it to be true that she is very much less the race than man; that she is, indeed, not even half the race at present, but rather a part of it told specially off for the continuance of the species, just as truly as drones or male spiders are parts of their species told off for the performance of male-functions, or as 'rotund' honey ants are individual insects told off to act as living honey jars to the community. She is the sex sacrificed to reproductive necessities."

===Chinese===
在 1888 年 11 月号的《论坛》（*The Forum*）上，莱斯特·F·沃德（Lester F. Ward）发表了一篇题为《我们的较好一半》（“Our Better Halves”）的论文，其中清楚表明了雌性性别在生物学上的优势地位。这自然激起了许多讨论；而在一篇作答的文章《女人在自然中的位置》（“Woman's Place in Nature”）（《论坛》，1889 年 5 月号）中，格兰特·艾伦（Grant Allen）先生十分周全地陈述了这一问题上的通行见解。他这样说到女人：「我相信这是真的：比起男人，她在很大程度上算不得种族本身；确实，她如今甚至算不上种族的一半，而毋宁是种族中被特别拨派出来延续物种的那一部分，正与雄蜂（drone）或雄蜘蛛是被拨派出来履行雄性功能的那一部分同样确实，也与『圆滚的』蜜蚁是被拨派出来充当群体活蜜罐的个别昆虫同样确实。她就是那个为生殖之必然而牺牲的性别。」

===Original===
Since biological facts point to the very gradual introduction and development of the male organism solely as a reproductive necessity, and since women are sacrificed not to reproductive necessities, but to a most unnecessary and injurious degree of sex-indulgence under economic necessity, such a statement as Mr. Grant Allen's has elements of humor. The opinion is held, however, not only by the special students of biology and sociology, but by the general public, and demands most careful attention. Those holding such a view may admit the over-development of sex consequent upon the economic relation between men and women, and the train of evils, individual and social, following that over-development. They may even admit, further, something of the alleged injury to economic evolution. But they will claim in answer that these morbid conditions are essential to human progress, and that the good to humanity through the segregation of the female to the uses of maternity overbalances the evil, great as this is; also, conversely, that the gain to the individual and to society to be obtained by the economic freedom of the female would be more than offset by the loss to the race caused by the removal of our highly specialized motherhood.

===Chinese===
既然生物学的事实表明，雄性机体之被引入与发展，纯乎是作为一种生殖的必要而极为渐进地实现的；既然妇女被牺牲掉，并非为着生殖的必要，而是为着经济必然之下的、一种大可不必而又为害至深的性放纵——那么，格兰特·艾伦先生这样的说法，便带上了几分幽默。然而，持这意见的不仅有生物学与社会学的专门学者，还有一般公众，因而它极须仔细对待。持这种观点的人可以承认：随男女之间的经济关系（economic relation）而来的性的过度发展，以及随那过度发展而来的一连串个体的与社会的邪恶。他们甚至可以进一步承认，所谓对经济演化的伤害也有几分属实。但他们会答辩说：这些病态境况是人类进步所不可或缺的；把雌性隔离专事生育之用而为人类带来的好处，胜得过那恶——纵使那恶再大；反过来，雌性获得经济自由而给个体与社会带来的收益，也远远抵不上取消我们那高度专门化的母职给种族造成的损失。

===Original===
To meet this, it is necessary to show that our highly specialized motherhood is not so advantageous as believed; that it is below rather than above the efficacy of motherhood in other species; that its deficiency is due to the sexuo-economic relation; that the restoration of economic freedom to the female will improve motherhood; and, finally, to indicate in some sort the lines of social and individual development along which this improvement may be "practically" manifested.

===Chinese===
要回应这一点，就必须表明：我们那高度专门化的母职并不像人们所信的那样有利；它非但不在其他物种母职的效力之上，反在其下；它的亏缺应归因于性-经济关系；把经济自由归还给雌性，将改善母职；最后，还须约略指明社会与个体发展所循的路线——沿着这些路线，这一改善可以「实际地」显现出来。

===Original===
In approaching this subject, we need something of special mental preparation. We need to realize that our ideas upon this theme are peculiarly colored by prejudice, that in no other field of thought are we so blinded by our emotions. We have felt more on this subject than on any other, and thought less. We have also felt much on the relation of the sexes; but it has been made a subject of study, of comparison, of speculation. There are differences of feeling on the sex question, but as to motherhood none. Here and there, to be sure, some isolated philosopher, a Plato, a Rousseau, dares advance some thought on this ground; but, on the whole, no theme of commensurate importance has been so little studied. More sacred than religion, more binding than the law, more habitual than methods of eating, we are each and all born into the accepted idea of motherhood and trained in it; and in maturity we hand it down unquestioningly. A man may question the purposes and methods of his God with less danger of outcry against him than if he dare to question the purposes and methods of his mother. This matriolatry is a sentiment so deep-seated, widespread, and long-established as to be dominant in every class of minds. It is so associated with our religious instincts, on the one hand, and our sex-instincts, on the other, both of which we have long been forbidden to discuss—the one being too holy and the other too unholy—that it is well-nigh impossible to think clearly and dispassionately on the subject. It is easy to understand why we are so triple-plated with prejudice in the case.

===Chinese===
接近这个题目时，我们需要某种特别的心理准备。我们需要认识到：我们在这个题目上的观念，格外地为偏见所染色；在任何别的思想领域，我们都不曾像在这里这样被自己的情感蒙蔽。在这个问题上，我们感受得最多，思考得最少。在两性关系上，我们也曾感触良多；但两性关系毕竟被弄成了研究、比较与思辨的对象。在性问题上，人们的感受尚有分歧；而在母职问题上，却全无分歧。诚然，这里那里，也偶有孤立的哲人，一位柏拉图（Plato）、一位卢梭（Rousseau），敢于在这块地面上提出一点思想；但总的来说，没有哪个同等重要的题目受到过如此少的研究。它比宗教更神圣，比法律更有约束力，比进食的方式更习以为常——我们每一个人都生而落入这公认的母职观念之中，受训于其中；到了成年，我们又毫不置疑地把它传下去。一个人质疑他的上帝的目的与方式，所招来众口嚣嚣的危险，还比不上他胆敢质疑他母亲的目的与方式。这种母亲崇拜（matriolatry）是一种根柢如此之深、流传如此之广、确立如此之久的情感，以致在每一类心智中都占支配地位。它一方面同我们的宗教本能、另一方面同我们的性本能纠结得如此之深——而这两者我们长久以来都被禁止讨论：一个太过神圣，一个太过不洁——以至于要对这个问题作清楚而冷静的思考，几乎是不可能的。这就不难理解，我们在这件事上为什么裹着三层偏见的铠甲。

===Original===
The instinct that draws the child to its mother is exactly as old as the instinct that draws the mother to her child; and that dates back to the period when the young first needed care—among the later reptiles, perhaps. This tie has lasted unbroken through the whole line of progression, and is stronger with us than with any other creature, because in our social evolution the parent is of advantage to the child not only through its entire life, but even after death, by our laws of inheritance. So early, so radically important, so long accumulated an animal instinct, added to by social law, is a great force. Besides this, we must reckon with our long period of ancestor worship. This finally changed the hideous concepts of early idolaters into the idea of parental divinity; for, having first made a god of their father, they then made a father of God, and this deep religious feeling has added much to the heavy weight of instinct. Parental government, too, absolute in the patriarchal period, has added further to our devout, blind faith in parenthood until it is *lèse majesté* to question its right fulfilment. Two most interesting developments are to be noted along this line. One is that the height of filial devotion was reached in the patriarchal age; when the father was the sole governor and feeder of the family, and could slay or sell his child at will; and that this relic of ancestor worship has steadily declined with the extension of government, until, in our democracy, with the fullest development of individual liberty and responsibility, is found the lowest degree of filial reverence and submission. Its place is taken, to our great gain, by such familiar, loving intercourse between parent and child as was utterly incompatible with the grovelling attitude of children in earlier times.

===Chinese===
把孩子引向母亲的本能，同把母亲引向孩子的本能，恰是一样古老；而那要追溯到幼崽最初需要照料的时代——或许是在较晚的爬行动物中间。这条纽带在整个进化的路线上不曾间断地延续下来，而且在我们身上比在任何别的造物身上都更强固，因为在我们的社会演化中，亲代之于孩子的益处，不仅贯穿其一生，甚至延续到死后——凭着我们的继承法。一个出现如此之早、如此根本重要、又积聚如此之久，再经社会法律增补的动物本能，是一股巨大的力量。除此之外，我们还须考虑到我们漫长的祖先崇拜时期。它最终把早期偶像崇拜者那些狰狞的观念，化成了亲代神性的观念；因为人们既先把他们的父亲造成了神，随后又把神造成了一位父亲；这一深沉的宗教情感，又为本能的重负添了许多。还有亲长的统治——它在父权制时期是绝对的——也进一步加深了我们对于亲职那种虔诚而盲目的信仰，直到质疑亲职是否得到正当履行，竟成了 *lèse majesté*（大逆不道）。沿着这条线索，有两个最为有趣的发展值得注意。其一，孝敬之极致是在父权时代达到的：那时父亲是全家唯一的治理者与供养者，可以随意杀掉或卖掉自己的孩子；而这一祖先崇拜的遗存，随着政府统治的扩展而持续衰落，直到在我们的民主制度中——随着个人自由与责任的最充分发展——出现了孝敬与顺从的最低程度。取代它的——于我们是大大的增益——是亲代与孩子之间那种亲昵而慈爱的交往；那样的交往，与早年间孩子那种匍匐卑屈的姿态是全然不能相容的。

===Original===
The other is the gradual swing from supreme devotion to the father, "the author of my being," as the child used to consider him, to our modern mother-worship. The dying soldier on the battlefield thinks of his mother, longs for her, not for his father. The traveller and exile dreams of his mother's care, his mother's doughnuts. The pathos of the popular tale today is in bringing the prodigal back to his mother, not to his father. If the original prodigal had a mother, she was probably busy in cooking the fatted calf. If today's prodigal has a father, he is merely engaged in paying for the veal. Our tenderest love, our deepest reverence, our fiercest resentment of insult, all centre about the mother today rather than about the father; and this is a strong proof that the recognition of woman's real power and place in life grow upon us just as our minds grow able to perceive it. Nothing can ever exceed the truth as to the value of the mother. Our instinct is a right one, as all deep-seated social instincts are; but about it has grown up a mass of falsehoods and absurdities such as always tend to confuse and impede the progress of great truths.

===Chinese===
其二，是从对父亲——孩子从前所认的「我生命的创造者」——的至高崇敬，渐渐摆向我们现代的母亲崇拜。战场上垂死的士兵想念的是他的母亲，渴盼的是她，而不是他的父亲。旅人与流亡者梦见的，是母亲的照料、母亲做的炸面圈。今日通俗故事里令人动容之处，是把浪子带回母亲身边，而不是父亲身边。倘若原先那个浪子有母亲，她多半正忙着烹调那头肥牛犊。倘若今天的浪子有父亲，他也只是忙着为那牛肉付账。我们最温柔的爱、我们最深挚的敬意、我们对侮辱最激烈的愤懑，今天全都集于母亲一身，而非父亲一身；而这是一个有力的证明：对女人在生活中的真实力量与位置的承认，正随着我们心智之日渐能够感知它而一同增长。至于母亲的价值，那真理是任何东西都逾越不了的。我们的本能是对的，一如一切根深蒂固的社会本能那样对；但围绕着它，长出了一大团谬误与荒诞，而这类东西总是倾向于淆乱并阻碍伟大真理的进程。

===Original===
As the main agent in reproduction, the mother is most to be venerated on basic physiological grounds. As the main agent in developing love, the great human condition, she is the fountain of all our growth. As the beginner of industry, she is again a source of progress. As the first and final educator, she outwardly molds what she has inwardly made; and, as she is the visible, tangible, lovable, living type of all this, the being in whose person is expressed the very sum of good to the individual, it is no wonder that our strongest, deepest, tenderest feelings cluster about the great word "mother."

===Chinese===
作为生殖中的主要动因，母亲在最根本的生理学理由上最堪崇敬。作为发展爱——人类的伟大境况——的主要动因，她是我们全部生长的源泉。作为产业的开创者，她又是进步的一个源头。作为最初也是最终的教育者，她在身外模塑出她在身内所造就的东西；而她既是这一切可见、可触、可爱、有生命的典型，是在其身上体现着个体之善的总和的存在，那么，我们最强烈、最深挚、最温柔的情感全都簇拥在「母亲」这个伟大的字眼周围，也就不足为怪了。

===Original===
Fully recognizing all this, it yet remains open to us to turn the light of science and the honest labor of thought upon this phase of human life as upon any other; to lay aside our feelings, and use our reason; to discover if even here we are justified in leaving the most important work of individual life to the methods of primitive instinct. Motherhood is but a process of life, and open to study as all processes of life are open. Among unconscious, early forms it fulfils its mission by a simple instinct. In the consciousness and complexity of human life it demands far more numerous and varied forces for its right fulfilment. It is with us a conscious process—a process rife with consequences for good or evil. With this voluntary power come new responsibility and a need for new methods—a need not merely to consider whether or not we will enter upon the duties of maternity, but how best we can fulfil them.

===Chinese===
充分承认了这一切之后，我们仍然可以把科学之光与诚实的思想劳作，像照向任何别的阶段那样，照向人类生活的这一阶段；把情感搁在一边，使用我们的理性；去查明：即便在这里，我们是否有理由把个体生命最重要的工作，交给原始本能的那套方法。母职不过是一个生命过程，同一切生命过程一样对研究敞开。在无意识的早期形态中间，它凭一个简单的本能便完成其使命。在人类生活的自觉与繁复之中，它的正当履行便要求远为众多、远为多样的力量。在我们这里，它是一个自觉的过程——一个充满或善或恶之后果的过程。随着这种自主之力而来的，是新的责任与对新方法的需要——不仅要考虑我们是否要承担母亲职责（maternal duties），更要考虑怎样才能最好地履行它们。

===Original===
Motherhood, like every other natural process, is to be measured by its results. It is good or evil as it serves its purpose. Human motherhood must be judged as it serves its purpose to the human race. Primarily, its purpose is to reproduce the race by reproducing the individual; secondarily, to improve the race by improving the individual. The mere office of reproduction is as well performed by the laying of eggs to be posthumously hatched as by many years of exquisite devotion; but in the improvement of the species we come to other requirements. The functions of motherhood have been evolved as naturally as the functions of nutrition, and each stage of development has brought new duties to the mother. The mother bird must brood her young, the mother cow must suckle them, the mother cat must hunt for them; and, in every varied service which the mother gives, its value is to be measured by its effect upon the young. To perform that which is most good for the young of the species is the measure of right motherhood, and that which is most good for the young is what will help them to a better maturity than that of their parents. To leave in the world a creature better than its parent, this is the purpose of right motherhood.

===Chinese===
母职，同一切别的自然过程一样，须以它的结果来衡量。它或善或恶，视其是否服务于它的目的。人类的母职，必须依它服务于人类种族之目的的情况来评判。首要地，它的目的是经由繁衍个体而繁衍种族；其次，是经由改善个体而改善种族。单是生殖这一职务，靠产下卵来待身后孵出，与靠多年精微的慈爱侍奉，完成得一样好；但一到物种的改善，我们便遇到了别样的要求。母职的诸功能，同营养的诸功能一样是自然演化而来的，而发展的每个阶段都给母亲带来了新的职责。母鸟必须孵育幼雏，母牛必须哺喂幼犊，母猫必须为幼崽捕食；而在母亲给出的每一种各不相同的服务中，其价值都须以它作用于幼者的效果来衡量。做那对物种的幼者最有益之事，便是正当母职的尺度；而对幼者最有益的，就是那能帮助他们达到比其亲代更好的成熟的东西。在世上留下一个比其亲代更好的造物——这就是正当母职的目的。

===Original===
In the human race this purpose is served by two processes: first, by the simple individual function of reproduction, of which all care and nursing are but an extension; and, second, by the complex social function of education. This was primarily a maternal process, and therefore individual; but it has long since become a racial rather than an individual function, and bears no relation to sex or other personal limitation. The young of the human race require for their best development not only the love and care of the mother, but the care and instruction of many besides their mother. So largely is this true that it may be said in extreme terms that it would be better for a child today to be left absolutely without mother or family of any sort, in the city of Boston, for instance, than to be supplied with a large and affectionate family and be planted with them in Darkest Africa.

===Chinese===
在人类种族中，这一目的由两个过程来服务：其一，是生殖这一简单的个体功能，一切照料与哺育不过是它的延伸；其二，是教育这一繁复的社会功能。教育起初本是一个母性的过程，因而是个体的；但早已成为种族的而非个体的功能，与性别或其他个人局限再无关系。人类的幼者，要得到最好的发展，不仅需要母亲的爱与照料，还需要母亲之外的许多人的照料与教导。这一点实在重大，以致可以说得极端些：今天，一个孩子若是被留在波士顿（Boston）城里，绝对地没有母亲、没有任何形式的家，也胜过被配给一个庞大而慈爱的家庭、然后同他们一起被栽到「最黑暗的非洲」（Darkest Africa）去。

===Original===
Human functions are race-functions, social functions; and education is one of them. The duty of the human mother, and the measure of its right or wrong fulfilment, are to be judged along these two main lines, reproduction and education. As we have no species above us with which to compare our motherhood, we must measure by those below us. We must show improvement upon them in this function which we all hold in common.

===Chinese===
人类的功能是种族功能（race-functions）、社会功能；教育便是其中之一。人类母亲的职责，及其履行之正误的尺度，须沿着生殖与教育这两条主线来评判。既然我们之上没有物种可以用来比照我们的母职，我们就必须以我们之下的物种来度量。在这种我们与它们共有的功能上，我们必须显出对它们的改进。

===Original===
Does the human mother succeed better than others of her order, mammalia, in the reproduction of the species? Does she bring forth and rear her young more perfectly than lower mothers? They, being less conscious, act simply under instinct, mating in their season, bringing forth young in their season, nursing, guarding, defending as best they may; and they leave in the world behind them creatures as good, or better, than their mothers. Of wild animals we have few reliable statistics, and of tame ones it is difficult to detach their natural processes from our interference therewith. But in both the simple maintenance of species shows that motherhood at least reproduces fairly well; and in those we breed for our advantage the wonderful possibilities of race-development through this process are made apparent. How do we, with the human brain and the human conscience, rich in the power and wisdom of our dominant race—how do we, as mothers, compare with our forerunners?

===Chinese===
在物种的生殖上，人类的母亲是否比她所属的哺乳纲（mammalia）中的其他母亲更为成功？她是否比更低等的母亲们更完善地生育和养育幼者？它们因自觉更少，只在本能之下行动：届时交配，届时产崽，哺喂、守护、防卫，各尽其力；它们在身后留下的造物，同它们的母亲一样好，或者更好。关于野兽，我们少有可靠的统计；关于驯养的动物，又难以把它们自然的过程同我们的干预分开。但在这两类中，物种的简单存续都表明：母职至少把生殖完成得相当不错；而在那些为我们自己的利益而饲养的动物中，经由这一过程而实现种族发展的种种惊人可能，更是明白可见。那么我们呢——有着人类的头脑与人类的良心、坐拥我们这支配性种族的力量与智慧——我们作为母亲，比之我们的先行者又如何？

===Original===
Human motherhood is more pathological than any other, more morbid, defective, irregular, diseased. Human childhood is similarly pathological. We, as animals, are very inferior animals in this particular. When we take credit to ourselves for the sublime devotion with which we face "the perils of maternity," and boast of "going down to the gates of death" for our children, we should rather take shame to ourselves for bringing these perils upon both mother and child. The gates of death? They are the gates of life to the unborn; and there is no death there save what we, the mothers, by our unnatural lives, have brought upon our own children. Gates of death, indeed, to the thousands of babies late-born, prematurely born, misborn, and stillborn for lack of right motherhood. In the primal physical functions of maternity the human female cannot show that her supposed specialization to these uses has improved her fulfilment of them, rather the opposite. The more freely the human mother mingles in the natural industries of a human creature, as in the case of the savage woman, the peasant woman, the working-woman everywhere who is not overworked, the more rightly she fulfils these functions.

===Chinese===
人类的母职，比任何别的母职都更病态——更加病弱、更有缺损、更无规律、更多疾病。人类的童年同样病态。作为一种动物，我们在这一件事上是非常低劣的动物。当我们因面对「生育的种种危险」时的崇高献身而自居其功、夸耀自己为孩子「直下到死亡的门前」时，我们更该因把这些危险同时加于母亲与孩子而自感羞愧。死亡的门前？对未生者，那是生命之门；那里并没有死亡——除了我们这些母亲以自己反常的生活加之于亲生孩子的那种。死亡之门——对于成千上万因缺乏正当母职而迟生的、早产的、错生的、死产的婴儿，倒是千真万确。在生育的原初身体功能上，人类雌性拿不出证据，表明她那所谓的对这些用途的专门化改进了她对它们的履行——毋宁说恰恰相反。人类的母亲愈是自由地参与一个人这种造物的自然产业——如野蛮人妇女、农家妇女、各地不曾劳苦过度的劳动妇女——她便愈是正当地履行这些功能。

===Original===
The more absolutely woman is segregated to sex-functions only, cut off from all economic use and made wholly dependent on the sex-relation as means of livelihood, the more pathological does her motherhood become. The over-development of sex caused by her economic dependence on the male reacts unfavorably upon her essential duties. She is too female for perfect motherhood! Her excessive specialization in the secondary sexual characteristics is a detrimental element in heredity. Small, weak, soft, ill-proportioned women do not tend to produce large, strong, sturdy, well-made men or women. When Frederic the Great wanted grenadiers of great size, he married big men to big women—not to little ones. The female segregated to the uses of sex alone naturally deteriorates in racial development, and naturally transmits that deterioration to her offspring. The human mother, in the processes of reproduction, shows no gain in efficiency over the lower animals, but rather a loss, and so far presents no evidence to prove that her specialization to sex is of any advantage to her young. The mother of a dead baby or the baby of a dead mother; the sick baby, the crooked baby, the idiot baby; the exhausted, nervous, prematurely aged mother—these are not uncommon among us; and they do not show much progress in our motherhood.

===Chinese===
女人愈是被绝对地隔离于单纯的性功能（sex-functions），被切断一切经济用途，被迫全然以性关系为谋生之资，她的母职就愈发病态。她之经济上依附于雄性所造成的性的过度发展，对她那些根本职责起着不利的作用。她过于女性化了，担当不起完美的母职！她在第二性征上的过度专门化，是遗传中的一个有害要素。瘦小、孱弱、绵软、比例失调的女人，倾向于生不出高大、强壮、结实、身量匀称的男人或女人。当腓特烈大帝（Frederic the Great）想要身材高大的掷弹兵时，他让高大的男人娶高大的女人——而不是娶小个子的。被隔离于单纯性之用途的雌性，自然会在种族发展上退化，也自然会把这退化传给她的后代。人类的母亲在生殖的诸过程中，比起低等动物非但没有效率上的增益，反而有所亏损；而且迄今为止，她拿不出任何证据来证明：她那对性的专门化，于她的幼者有半点好处。死了孩子的母亲，或者死了母亲的婴儿；病弱的婴儿、畸形的婴儿、痴愚的婴儿；精疲力竭、神经衰弱、未老先衰的母亲——这些在我们中间并非罕有；而它们并未显出我们母职的多少进步。

===Original===
Since we cannot justify the human method of maternity in the physical processes of reproduction, can we prove its advantages in the other branch, education? Though the mother be sickly and the child the same, will not her loving care more than make up for it? Will not the tender devotion of the mother, and her unflagging attendance upon the child, render human motherhood sufficiently successful in comparison with that of other species to justify our peculiar method? We must now show that our motherhood, in its usually accepted sense, the "care" of the child (more accurately described as education), is of a superior nature.

===Chinese===
既然我们无法为人类的生育方式在生殖的身体过程中辩护，那么在另一支——教育——上，我们能证明它的优越吗？纵然母亲病弱、孩子也病弱，她那慈爱的照料不也足以弥补一切吗？母亲温柔的献身、她对孩子的须臾不离的侍奉，难道不能使人类的母职与其他物种相比足够成功，从而为我们这套独特的方式辩护吗？现在我们必须表明：我们的母职，在它通常被接受的意义上，即对孩子的「照料」（更准确地说是教育），具有一种更优越的性质。

===Original===
Here, again, we lack the benefit of comparison. No other animal species is required to care for its young so long, to teach it so much. So far as they have it to do, they do it well. The hen with her brood is an accepted model of motherhood in this respect. She not only lays eggs and hatches them, but educates and protects her young so far as it is necessary. But beyond such simple uses as this we have no standard of comparison for educative motherhood. We can only study it among ourselves, comparing the child left motherless with the child mothered, the child with a mother and nothing else with the child whose mother is helped by servants and teachers, the child with what we recognize as a superior mother to the child with an inferior mother. This last distinction, a comparison between mothers, is of great value. We have tacitly formulated a certain vague standard of human motherhood, and loosely apply it, especially in the epithets "natural" and "unnatural" mother.

===Chinese===
在这里，我们同样缺少比较之益。没有任何别的动物物种需要照料幼者如此之久、教导它如此之多。凡它们须做的，它们都做得好。带着一窝小鸡的母鸡，在这一方面是公认的母职典范。她不仅产卵、孵卵，还在必要的范围内教育并保护她的幼雏。但超出这类简单的用途之外，我们就没有比较教育性母职的标准了。我们只能在我们自己中间研究它：把失去母亲的孩子的境况与母亲照管的孩子相比，把只有母亲别无其他的孩子与母亲有佣工和教师辅助的孩子相比，把我们认作优越的母亲的孩子与低劣的母亲的孩子相比。这最后一种区分——母亲与母亲的比较——价值极大。我们已经心照不宣地拟出了某种关于人类母职的模糊标准，并宽泛地运用着它，尤其是在「天然的」母亲与「非天然的」母亲这类称号上。

===Original===
But these terms again show how prone we still are to consider the whole field of maternal action as one of instinct rather than of reason, as a function rather than a service. We do have a standard, however, loose and vague as it is; and even by that standard it is painful to see how many human mothers fail. Ask yourselves honestly how many of the mothers whose action toward their children confronts you in street and shop and car and boat, in hotel and boardinghouse and neighboring yard—how many call forth favorable comment compared with those you judge unfavorably? Consider not the rosy ideal of motherhood you have in your mind, but the coarse, hard facts of motherhood as you see them, and hear them, in daily life.

===Chinese===
但这些称号又表明：我们仍然何等倾向于把母性作为的整个领域，看作本能的领域而非理性的领域，看作一种功能而非一种服务。然而我们确乎有一个标准——不管它多么宽泛模糊；而即便以那个标准来衡量，看到有多少人类母亲不合格，也令人痛心。诚实地问问你们自己：那些在街上、店铺里、车中、船上，在旅馆、寄宿公寓与邻里院落中当着你们的面对待孩子的母亲们——与你们评为不堪的那些相比，能引来称许的有多少？不要想你们心里那玫瑰色的母职理想，要看你们在日常生活中亲眼所见、亲耳所闻的母职那粗粝而坚硬的事实。

===Original===
Motherhood in its fulfilment of educational duty can be measured only by its effects. If we take for a standard the noble men and women whose fine physique and character we so fondly attribute to "a devoted mother," what are we to say of the motherhood which has filled the world with the ignoble men and women, of depraved physique and character? If the good mother makes the good man, how about the bad ones? When we see great men and women, we give credit to their mothers. When we see inferior men and women—and that is a common circumstance—no one presumes to question the motherhood which has produced them. When it comes to congenital criminality, we are beginning to murmur something about "heredity"; and, to meet gross national ignorance, we do demand a better system of education. But no one presumes to suggest that the mothering of mankind could be improved upon; and yet there is where the responsibility really lies. If our human method of reproduction is defective, let the mother answer. She is the main factor in reproduction. If our human method of education is defective, let the mother answer. She is the main factor in education.

===Chinese===
母职对其教育职责的履行，只能以其效果来衡量。倘若我们以那些高贵的男女为标准——他们的健美体魄与品格，我们多么乐于归功于「一位尽心竭力的母亲」——那么，对于那使世界充斥了体魄与品格俱已败坏的卑贱男女的母职，我们又该说什么呢？倘若好的母亲造就好的人，那么坏的母亲又如何呢？当我们看到伟大的男人与女人，我们把功劳记在他们的母亲身上。当我们看到低劣的男人与女人——这可是寻常的情形——却没有人敢去质问造出他们的那种母职。一说到先天的犯罪性，我们开始嗫嚅几句「遗传」；而为了对付全国性的大面积无知，我们也确实要求一种更好的教育制度。但没有人敢提一句：人类的为母之道是可以改进的；而责任恰恰就落在这里。如果我们人类的生殖方式有缺陷，让母亲来作答。她是生殖中的主要因素。如果我们人类的教育方式有缺陷，让母亲来作答。她是教育中的主要因素。

===Original===
To this it is bitterly objected that such a claim omits the father and his responsibility. When the mother of the world is in her right place and doing her full duty, she will have no ground of complaint against the father. In the first place, she will make better men. In the second, she will hold herself socially responsible for the choice of a right father for her children. In the third place, as an economic free agent, she will do half duty in providing for the child. Men who are not equal to good fatherhood under such conditions will have no chance to become fathers, and will die with general pity instead of living with general condemnation. In his position, doing all the world's work, all the father's, and half the mother's, man has made better shift to achieve the impossible than woman has in hers. She has been supposed to have no work or care on earth save as mother. She has really had the work of the mother and that of the world's house service besides. But she has surely had as much time and strength to give to motherhood as man to fatherhood; and not until she can show that the children of the world are as well mothered as they are well fed can she cast on him the blame for our general deficiency.

===Chinese===
对这一点，有人愤愤地反驳说：这样的主张把父亲及其责任撇在了一边。当这世界的母亲处在她正当的位置上、尽着她全部的职责时，她便再没有向父亲抱怨的余地。第一，她将造出更好的男人。第二，她将为孩子择定一位正当的父亲，并在这件事上负起自己的社会责任。第三，作为一个经济上的自由行动者，她将在供养孩子一事上尽一半的职责。在这样的条件下担当不起良好父职的男人，将没有机会成为父亲，并且将在众人的一般怜悯中死去，而不是在众人的一般谴责中活着。男人处在他的位置上，做着全世界的工作、全部的父亲的工作、再加上一半的母亲的工作，比起处在她位置上的女人，还更善于将就着应付那不可能之事。她一直被设想为除了为母之外，在这世上没有任何工作与操劳。而实际上，她既承担着母亲的工作，又承担着世界家务劳役的工作。但可以肯定，她能给予母职的时间与气力，绝不比男人能给予父职的少；而且，除非她能证明这世界的孩子们所受的母亲的照管，已同他们所受的口腹之养一样好，她才能把我们普遍的亏缺归罪于他。

===Original===
There is no personal blame to be laid on either party. The sexuo-economic relation has its inevitable ill-effects on both motherhood and fatherhood. But it is to the mother that the appeal must be made to change this injurious relation. Having the deeper sense of duty to the young, the larger love, she must come to feel how her false position hurts her motherhood, and for her children's sake break away from it. Of man and his fatherhood she can make what she will.

===Chinese===
这不是哪一方的个人罪责。性-经济关系对母职与父职，各有其不可避免的恶果。但要改变这有害的关系，诉求却必须向母亲发出。她既有对幼者更深的义务感、更广大的爱，她就必须渐渐感到她那虚伪的位置如何伤害着她的母职，并为她孩子的缘故挣脱它。至于男人和他的父职——她尽可以随心造就。

===Original===
The duty of the mother is first to produce children as good as or better than herself; to hand down the constitution and character of those behind her the better for her stewardship; to build up and improve the human race through her enormous power as mother; to make better people. This being done, it is then the duty of the mother, the human mother so to educate her children as to complete what bearing and nursing have only begun. She carries the child nine months in her body, two years in her arms, and as long as she lives in her heart and mind. The education of the young is a tremendous factor in human reproduction. A right motherhood should be able to fulfil this great function perfectly.
