不妨顺带指出：「美国症」（Americanitis）的病因之一，便是这种家庭关系中日益加重的神经紧张，它尤其作用于女人。随着她变得愈加个体化，她便愈加苦于早先时代家庭生活那原始而不分化的境况。昔日「为人妻」「为人母」者被认为完全合身的那一切，这位新近专门化了的、同时又是个人人格的妻子与母亲，却觉得笨拙而不合身——她要的是一只手套，到手的却是一只连指手套。家内的照料与劳作依然未经发展，给不了她日益增长的专门化以任何发挥余地。厨娘-保姆-洗衣妇-寝室女仆-女管家-女侍-家庭女教师这一尚未分化的胚胎式组合，尚且甘于「样样都做、样样不精」；而那能把其中一桩事做得尽善尽美、并因此更难把其余诸事全数做好的女人，便遭受着双重的痛苦：做不成她想做的事，又被迫去做她不想做的事。对那精微分化了的现代头脑而言，一天之内十几次从一门活计跳到另一门活计的震荡与冲击，是一种实实在在的伤害，一种神经力量的浪费。随着今日女性更大程度的社会化——对更宽广的联合、更普遍的兴趣、为更大目标服务的更有组织的工作方法的胜任，以及随之而来的渴望——她便愈发沉重地感到那些较原始的家内职责、兴趣与方法所具有的那种强烈个人性的局限。而这痛楚与紧张，必随妇女的进步而增长，直到新的功能力量为自身造就出有机的表达，直到这些落在时代后面的家务劳作，像现代生活其他必需的劳动一样，得到提升与组织。

===Original===
In the meantime, however, the very best and foremost women suffer most; and a heavy check is placed on social progress by this difficulty in enlarging old conditions to suit new powers. It should still be remembered it is not the essential relations of wife and mother which are thus injurious, but the industrial conditions born of the economic dependence of the wife and mother, and hitherto supposed to be part of her functions. The change we are making does not in any way militate against the true relations of the family, marriage, and parentage, but only against those sub-relations belonging to an earlier period and now in process of extinction. The family as an entity, an economic and social unit, does not hold as it did. The ties between brother and sister, cousins and relatives generally, are gradually lessening their hold, and giving way under pressure of new forces which tend toward better things.

===Chinese===
然而在这期间，受苦最深的恰是那些最优秀、最前列的女性；而旧境况难于扩充以适应新力量，也给社会进步设下了沉重的滞碍。仍应记住的是：如此有害的并不是妻子与母亲那些本质性的关系，而是由妻子与母亲的经济依附（economic dependence）所生、并至今被认作其职能之一部分的那些产业境况。我们正在进行的改变，丝毫不违逆家庭、婚姻与亲职的真关系，只违逆那些属于更早时期、如今正处在消亡过程中的次级关系。家庭作为一个实体、一个经济与社会的单位，已不像从前那样维系得住。兄弟与姊妹之间、堂表亲与诸亲眷之间的种种纽带，正渐渐放松其维系，并在那趋向于更善之物的诸新力量的压力下让路。

===Original===
The change is more perceptible among women than among men, because of the longer survival of more primitive phases of family life in them. One of its most noticeable features is the demand in women not only for their own money, but for their own work for the sake of personal expression. Those who object to women’s working on the ground that they should not compete with men or be forced to struggle for existence look only at work as a means of earning money. They should remember that human labor is an exercise of faculty, without which we should cease to be human; that to do and to make not only gives deep pleasure, but is indispensable to healthy growth. Few girls today fail to manifest some signs of this desire for individual expression. It is not only in the classes who are forced to it: even among the rich we find this same stirring of normal race-energy. To carve in wood, to hammer brass, to do “art dressmaking,” to raise mushrooms in the cellar﻿—our girls are all wanting to do something individually. It is a most healthy state, and marks the development of race-distinction in women with a corresponding lowering of sex-distinction to its normal place.

===Chinese===
这一变化在女性中比在男性中更可察觉，因为家庭生活较原始的诸阶段在她们身上存留得更久。它最显著的特征之一，是女性不仅要求自己的钱，而且要求自己的工作，以求个人的表达。那些以女人不该与男人竞争、不该被迫为生存而挣扎为由反对女人工作的人，只把工作看作挣钱的方法。他们应当记得：人的劳动是官能的运用，没有它我们便不复为人；去做、去创造，不仅给予深切的快乐，而且为健康的生长所不可或缺。今日的姑娘，很少有不显出这种个人表达之渴望的某种迹象的。这并不只发生在被迫如此的诸阶级身上：即便在富人中间，我们也发现这同一种正常的种族能量（race-energy）的萌动。雕木、锤铜、做「艺术裁缝」、在地窖里种蘑菇——我们的姑娘人人都想亲手做点什么。这是一种最健康的状态，它标志着女性身上种族区分（race-distinction）的发展，伴随着性别区分（sex-distinction）相应地降回其正常的位置。

===Original===
In body and brain, wherever she touches life, woman is changing gloriously from the mere creature of sex, all her race-functions held in abeyance, to the fully developed human being, none the less true woman for being more truly human. What alarms and displeases us in seeing these things is our funny misconception that race-functions are masculine. Much effort is wasted in showing that women will become “unsexed” and “masculine” by assuming these human duties. We are told that a slight sex-distinction is characteristic of infancy and old age, and that the assumption of opposite traits by either sex shows either a decadent or an undeveloped condition. The young of any race are less marked by sex-distinction; and in old age the distinguishing traits are sometimes exchanged, as in the crowing of old hens and in the growing of the beard on old women. And we are therefore assured that the endeavor of women to perform these masculine economic functions marks a decadent civilization, and is greatly to be deprecated. There would be some reason in this objection if the common racial activities of humanity, into which women are now so eagerly entering, were masculine functions. But they are not. There is no more sublimated expression of our morbid ideas of sex-distinction than in this complacent claiming of all human life-processes as sex-functions of the male. “Masculine” and “feminine” are only to be predicated of reproductive functions﻿—processes of race-preservation. The processes of self-preservation are racial, peculiar to the species, but common to either sex.

===Chinese===
在身体与头脑之中，在她触及生命的每一处，女性正辉煌地从单纯的性之造物——她全部的种族功能（race-functions）皆被悬置——转变为充分发展的人；她更加真实地成其为人，却丝毫不因此减其为真实的女人。看到这些事情时使我们惊慌而不快的，是我们那可笑的误解：以为种族功能是男性的。人们费尽气力去证明，女人一旦承担起这些人类的职责，就会变得「失去性别」「男性化」。有人告诉我们：轻微的性别区分是幼年与老年的特征，而任一性别若表现出相反的特质，便表明一种颓废的或未发展的状态。任何种族的幼者，性别区分都较不明显；而到了老年，两性的区分特质有时会互换，如老母鸡打鸣、老妇人长须。于是我们又得到保证说：女性履行这些男性经济功能的努力，标志着一个颓废的文明，是大当贬斥之事。倘若女性如今如此热切地进入的人类的那些共同种族活动，当真是男性功能，这反对倒也有些道理。但它们不是。把我们病态的性别区分观念表达得更为升华的，莫过于这种自满的声称：把人的一切生命过程都认作男性的性功能（sex-functions）。「男性的」与「女性的」，只能用来述说生殖的功能——种族保存（race-preservation）的诸过程。自我保存（self-preservation）的诸过程则是种族性的，为物种所特有，却为两性所共有。

===Original===
If it could be shown that the women of today were growing beards, were changing as to pelvic bones, were developing bass voices, or that in their new activities they were manifesting the destructive energy, the brutal combative instinct, or the intense sex-vanity of the male, then there would be cause for alarm. But the one thing that has been shown in what study we have been able to make of women in industry is that they are women still, and this seems to be a surprise to many worthy souls. A female horse is no less female than a female starfish, but she has more functions. She can do more things, is a more highly specialized organism, has more intelligence, and, with it all, is even more feminine in her more elaborate and farther-reaching processes of reproduction. So the “new woman” will be no less female than the “old” woman, though she has more functions, can do more things, is a more highly specialized organism, has more intelligence. She will be, with it all, more feminine, in that she will develop far more efficient processes of caring for the young of the human race than our present wasteful and grievous method, by which we lose fifty percent of them, like a codfish. The average married pair, says the scientific dictator, in all sobriety, should have four children merely to preserve our present population, two to replace themselves and two to die﻿—a pleasant method this, and redounding greatly to the credit of our motherhood.

===Chinese===
倘若能够表明，今日的女性正在长出胡须，骨盆正在发生变化，正在长出男低音般的嗓音，或者在新活动中显出男人那种破坏性的能量、残忍的好斗本能或强烈的性虚荣，那倒真有理由惊慌。但在我们所能作的关于产业中女性的研究中，业已表明的一点是：她们仍然是女人——而这在许多可敬的人看来竟像是件意外。母马之为雌性，并不逊于母海星，但她有更多的功能。她能做更多的事，是一个更高度专门化的有机体，有更高的智力；而与此同时，在她那更精巧、更远及的生殖过程中，她甚至更具雌性。同样，「新女性」之为雌性，也不会逊于「旧」女性，尽管她有更多的功能，能做更多的事，是一个更高度专门化的有机体，有更高的智力。与此同时，她还将更具女性气质，因为她将发展出远为高效的方法来照料人类的幼者，胜过我们如今这种浪费而悲惨的方法——我们靠那方法失去一半的孩子，像鳕鱼一样。科学的独断者一本正经地说：一对平常的已婚夫妇，单为维持我们现有的人口，就应当生下四个孩子——两个接替他们自己，两个用来死去——这方法着实愉快，也大大为我们母职（motherhood）增光。

===Original===
The rapid extension of function in the modern woman has nothing to do with any exchange of masculine and feminine traits: it is simply an advance in human development of traits common to both sexes, and is wholly good in its results. No one who looks at the life about us can fail to see the alteration going on. It is a pity that we so fail to estimate its value. On the other hand, the growth and kindling intensity of the social consciousness among us all is as conspicuous a feature of modern life as the change in woman’s position, and closely allied therewith.

===Chinese===
现代女性功能的迅速扩展，与男性特质和女性特质的任何交换都毫无关系：它只是两性共有的特质在人类发展中的一次前进，其结果是完全好的。凡是看一看我们周围生活的人，都不会看不到正在发生的改变。可惜我们偏偏未能估量它的价值。另一方面，我们所有人身上社会意识（social consciousness）的增长与其炽烈的燃烧，也同妇女地位的改变一样，是现代生活一个显著的特征，并且与后者紧密相连。

===Original===
Never before have people cared so much about other people. From its first expression in greater kindliness and helpfulness toward individual human beings to its last expression in the vague, blind, groping movements toward international justice and law, the heart of the world is alive and stirring today. The whole social body is affected with sudden shudders of feeling over some world calamity or world rejoicing. When the message of *Uncle Tom’s Cabin* ran from heart to heart around the world, kindling a streak of fire, the fire of human love and sympathy which is latent in us all and longing always for some avenue of common expression, it proved that in every civilized land of our time the people are of one mind on some subjects. Nothing could have so spread and so awakened a response in the Periclean, the Augustan, or even the Elizabethan age; for humanity was not then so far socialized and so far individualized as to be capable of such a general feeling.

===Chinese===
人们从未像今天这样深切地关怀他人。从它在对待个体之人更大的仁慈与扶助上的最初表达，到它在朝向国际正义与国际法的朦胧、盲目而摸索的举动上的最后表达，世界的心今天活着，而且在激荡。整个社会躯体，会因某场世界性的灾难或世界性的欢庆而突然一阵战栗。当《汤姆叔叔的小屋》（*Uncle Tom’s Cabin*）的讯息在世界各地心心相传，点燃一线火焰——那潜藏在我们所有人身上、始终渴望着某种共同表达途径的人的爱与同情的火焰——它便证明了：在我们这个时代的每一片文明土地上，人民在某些主题上是同一颗心。这样一场传布、这样一场唤醒的回应，在伯里克利时代、奥古斯都时代、乃至伊丽莎白时代，都绝无可能；因为那时的人类，无论社会化还是个体化，都还远未能具备这样一种普遍的情感。

===Original===
Invention and the discoveries of science are steadily unifying the world today. The statement is frequently advanced that the minds of the men of Greece or of the great thinkers of the Middle Ages were stronger and larger than the minds of the men of today. Perhaps they were. So were the bodies of the megatherium and the ichthyosaurus stronger and larger than the bodies of the animals of today. Yet they were lower in the scale of organic evolution. The ability of the individual is not so much the criterion of social progress as that organic relation of individuals which makes the progress of each available to all. Emerson has done more for America than Plato could do for Greece. Indeed, Plato has done more for America than he could do for Greece, because the printing press and the public school have made thought more freely and easily transmissible.

===Chinese===
发明与科学的种种发现，正在今日稳步地统一世界。常有人提出这样的说法：希腊人的头脑，或中世纪伟大思想家们的头脑，比今日之人的头脑更强、更大。也许是吧。大地懒（megatherium）与鱼龙（ichthyosaurus）的躯体，也曾比今日动物的躯体更强、更大。然而它们在有机演化的阶梯上更低。个体的能力，与其说是社会进步的判准，不如说那判准是个体之间的有机联系——它使每个人的进步为所有人可得。爱默生（Emerson）为美国所做的，多过柏拉图（Plato）能为希腊所做的。的确，柏拉图为美国所做的，也多过他能为希腊所做的，因为印刷机与公立学校已使思想的传递更自由、更轻易。

===Original===
Human progress lies in the perfecting of the social organization, and it is here that the changes of our day are most marked. Whereas, in more primitive societies, injuries were only felt by the individual as they affected his own body or direct personal interests, and later his own nation or church, today there is a growing sensitiveness to social injuries, even to other nations. The civilized world has suffered in Armenia’s agony, even though the machinery of social expression is yet unable fully to carry out the social feeling or the social will. Function comes before organ always; and the human heart and mind, which are the social heart and mind, must feel and think long before the social body can act in full expression.

===Chinese===
人类的进步在于社会组织的完善，而正是在这里，我们时代的变化最为显著。在较原始的社会里，伤害唯有在其触及个体自己的身体或直接的个人利益时才被他感觉到，后来又扩及他自己的国家或教会；而今天，人们对社会的伤害——甚至对他国的伤害——正变得愈来愈敏感。文明世界曾在亚美尼亚（Armenia）的苦难中一同受苦，纵然社会表达的机器还未能充分实现社会的情感或社会的意志。总是功能先于器官；而人的心与头脑——它们正是社会的心与头脑——必须先长久地感受与思考，然后社会躯体才能以充分的表达去行动。

===Original===
Social sympathy and thought are growing more intense and active every day. In our cumbrous efforts at international arbitration, in the halfhearted alliances and agreements between great peoples, in the linking of humanity together across ocean and mountain and desert plain by steam and electricity, in the establishment of such world-functions as the international postal service﻿—in these, externally, our social unity has begun to act. In the more familiar field of personal life, who has not seen how unceasingly many of us are occupied in the interests of the community, even to the injury of our own? The rising manifestations of social interest among women were covered with ridicule at first, through such characters as Mrs. Jellyby or Mrs. Pardiggle, although a few women who were so great and so identified with religion and philanthropy as to command respect, women like the saintly Elizabeth Fry, Florence Nightingale, and Clara Barton, escaped. But both belong to the same age, are part of the same phenomena. Today there is hardly a woman of intelligence in all America, to say nothing of other countries, who is not definitely and actively concerned in some social interest, who does not recognize some duty besides those incident to her own blood relationship.

===Chinese===
社会的同情与思想，正一天天变得更强烈、更活跃。在我们对国际仲裁的笨拙努力中，在诸伟大民族之间半心半意的联盟与协议中，在以蒸汽与电把人类跨过海洋、高山与荒漠平原连结起来的进程中，在诸如国际邮政这样的世界性职能的确立中——在这些方面，我们的社会统一已开始外在地运作。在更切近的个人生活领域，谁没有看到，我们中间有多少人在没完没了地为社群的利益操劳，甚至不惜损伤自己的利益？女性社会兴趣的种种兴起，起初曾被讥笑所淹没——通过杰利比太太（Mrs. Jellyby）或帕迪格尔太太（Mrs. Pardiggle）这样的人物；尽管有少数女性因其伟大、因其与宗教和慈善事业融为一体而赢得敬重——如圣洁的伊丽莎白·弗莱（Elizabeth Fry）、弗洛伦斯·南丁格尔（Florence Nightingale）与克拉拉·巴顿（Clara Barton）——幸免于此。但二者同属一个时代，是同一现象的两个部分。今天，在整个美国——更不必说别的国家——几乎找不到一位有智识的女性，不切实而积极地关切某项社会利益，不认她的血缘关系所附带者之外的某种义务。

===Original===
The woman’s club movement is one of the most important sociological phenomena of the century﻿—indeed, of all centuries﻿—marking as it does the first timid steps toward social organization of these so long unsocialized members of our race. Social life is absolutely conditioned upon organization. The military organizations which promote peace, the industrial organizations which maintain life, and all the educational, religious, and charitable organizations which serve our higher needs constitute the essential factors of that social activity in which, as individuals, we live and grow; and it is plain, therefore, that while women had no part in these organizations they had no part in social life. Their main relation to society was an individual one, an animal one, a sexual one. They produced the people of whom society was made, but they were not society. Of course, they were indispensable in this capacity; but one might as well call food a part of society because people could not exist without eating as to call women a social factor because people could not exist without being born. Women have made the people who made the world, and will always continue so to do. But they have heretofore had a most insignificant part in the world their sons have made.

===Chinese===
妇女俱乐部运动是本世纪——实则是历来一切世纪——最重要的社会学现象之一，因为它标志着我们种族中这些如此长久未获社会化的成员，正迈出走向社会组织的第一批怯生生的步子。社会生活绝对地以组织为条件。维护和平的军事组织，维持生活的产业组织，以及服务我们更高需要的一切教育、宗教与慈善组织，构成了那社会活动的本质要素——我们作为个体在其中生活并成长；因此很明白：只要妇女在这些组织中没有份，她们在社会生活中便没有份。她们与社会的主要关系是个人的关系、动物的关系、性的关系。她们生产了构成社会的人们，但她们并不是社会。当然，她们在这一身份上是不可或缺的；但若因人不出生便不能存在，便说女人是一个社会要素，那就好比因人不吃饭便不能存在，便说食物是社会的一部分。女人造出了缔造这个世界的人，并将永远如此。但在她们的儿子们缔造的这个世界上，她们迄今为止所占的份额却微乎其微。

===Original===
The only form of organization possible to women was for long the celibate religious community. This has always been dear to them; and, as today many avoid undesired marriage for the sake of “independence,” so in earlier times many fled from undesired marriage to the communal independence of the convent. The fondness of women for the church has been based, not only on religious feeling, but on the force of the human longing for coordinate interest and activities; and only here could this be gratified. In the church at least they could be together. They could feel in common and act in common﻿—the deepest human joy. As the church has widened its activities, it has found everywhere in women its most valuable and eager workers. To labor together, together to raise funds for a common end, for a new building or a new minister, for local charities or for foreign missions﻿—but to labor together, and for other needs than those of the family relation﻿—this has always met glad response from the struggling human soul in woman. When it became possible to work together for other than religious ends﻿—when large social service was made possible to women, as in our sanitary commission during the last war﻿—women everywhere rose to meet the need. The rise and spread of that greatest of women’s organizations, the Woman’s Christian Temperance Union, has shown anew how ready is the heart of woman to answer the demands of other than personal relations.

===Chinese===
长期以来，女性唯一可能的组织形式，是独身的宗教共同体。这种形式一向为她们所珍爱；正如今天许多人为「独立」而回避不愿要的婚姻，早先也有许多人逃避不愿要的婚姻，逃入女修道院那共同体的独立。女性对教会的偏爱，从来不只基于宗教情感，还基于人对协同的兴趣与活动的那种渴望之力；而唯有在这里，这渴望才能得到满足。至少在教会里，她们能够相聚。她们能够共同地感受、共同地行动——这是最深的人的快乐。随着教会拓宽其活动，它在各处都发现女性是它最可贵、最热切的劳动者。一同劳作，一同为一个共同的目标筹款——为一座新建筑或一位新牧师，为地方慈善或海外传教——但要紧的是一同劳作，且为家庭关系之外的诸种需要而劳作——这一向得到女人身上那挣扎着的人的灵魂的欣然回应。当为宗教之外的目的而共同劳作成为可能时——当大规模的社会服务对女性成为可能时，如上次战争期间我们的卫生委员会——各地的女性都奋起应对需要。而那个最伟大的妇女组织——基督教妇女禁酒联合会（Woman’s Christian Temperance Union）——的兴起与传布，再一次表明：女人的心何等乐于应答个人关系之外的种种要求。

===Original===
And now the whole country is budding into women’s clubs. The clubs are uniting and federating by towns, States, nations: there are even world organizations. The sense of human unity is growing daily among women. Not to see it is impossible. Not to watch with pleasure and admiration this new growth in social life, this sudden and enormous reinforcement of our best forces from the very springs of life, only shows how blind we are to true human advantage, how besotted in our fondness for sex-distinction in excess.

===Chinese===
而今整个国家正含苞绽出妇女俱乐部。俱乐部正按城镇、按州、按国家联合起来、结成联合会：甚至已有世界性的组织。人类的统一感正在女性中间与日俱增。看不到它是不可能的。面对社会生活中这株新芽——我们最良善的力量从生命本源处突然获得的这笔巨大增援——若不能怀着欣悦与赞叹注视它，只能表明我们对真正的人类利益何其盲目，表明我们偏爱过度的性别区分，已偏爱到何等痴迷的地步。

===Original===
One of the most valuable features of this vast line of progress is the new heroism it is pouring into life. The crumbling and flattening of ambitions and ideals under pressure of our modern business life is a patent fact. We are growing to surrender taste and conscience and honor itself to the demands of business success, prostituting the noblest talents to the most ignoble uses with that last excuse of cowardice﻿—“A man must live.” Into this phase of life comes a new spirit﻿—the spirit of such women as Elizabeth Cady Stanton and Susan B. Anthony; of Dr. Elizabeth Blackwell and her splendid sisterhood; of all the women who have battled and suffered for half a century, forcing their way, with sacrifices never to be told, into the field of freedom so long denied them﻿—not for themselves alone, but for one another. We have loudly cried out at the injury to the home and family which are supposed to follow such a course. We have unsparingly ridiculed the unattractive and unfeminine among these vanguard workers. But few have thought what manner of spirit it must take to leave the dear old easy paths so long trodden by so many feet, and go to hew out new ones alone. The nature of the effort involved and the nature of the opposition incurred conduced to lessen the soft charms and graces of the ultra-feminine state; but the women who follow and climb swiftly up the steps which these great leaders so laboriously built may do the new work in the new places, and still keep much of what these strenuous heroes had to lose.

===Chinese===
这条宏大进步路线最可贵的特征之一，是它正倾注入生活的新英雄气。在现代商业生活的压力之下，抱负与理想的碎裂坍平，是一桩显豁的事实。我们正日渐把趣味、良心乃至荣誉本身拱手让与商业成功的要求，以怯懦的最后托辞——「人总得活下去」——把最高贵的才干卖到最不齿的用途里去。就在生命的这一阶段中，一种新的精神到来了——伊丽莎白·卡迪·斯坦顿（Elizabeth Cady Stanton）与苏珊·B·安东尼（Susan B. Anthony）这些女性的精神；伊丽莎白·布莱克韦尔医生（Dr. Elizabeth Blackwell）及其出色姊妹之群的精神；所有那些奋战并受苦了半个世纪、以无从诉说的牺牲强行挤入那被长久拒绝于她们的自由之地的女人的精神——不为她们自己，而为彼此。我们曾大声疾呼，痛惜据说会随这样一条道路而来的对家庭与家族的伤害。我们曾毫不留情地讥笑这些先锋战士中那些不迷人、不像女人的。却很少有人想过：离开那么多脚久已踏惯的、亲切的旧坦途，独自去砍凿新路，这需要何等样的精神。这番努力的性质与所招阻力的性质，难免减损那超女性状态的柔媚与风致；但那些追随其后、迅捷攀上这些伟大领袖如此辛劳筑起的台阶的女人们，将能在新的地方做新的工作，同时仍保有这些刚毅的英雄们不得不失去的许多东西。

===Original===
It is not being a doctor that makes a woman unwomanly, but the treatment which the first women medical students and physicians received was such as to make even men unmanly. That time is largely past. The gates are nearly all open, at least in some places; and the racial activities of women are free to develop as rapidly as the nature of the case will allow. The main struggle now is with the distorted nature of the creature herself. Grand as are the women who embody at whatever cost the highest spirit of the age, there still remains to us the heavy legacy of the years behind﻿—the innumerable weak and little women, with the aspirations of an affectionate guinea pig. The soul of woman must speak through the long accumulations of her intensified sex-nature, through the uncertain impulses of a starved and thwarted class. She must recognize that she is handicapped. She must understand her difficulty, and meet it bravely and firmly.

===Chinese===
使女人不像女人的，并不是行医这件事本身；而是最初那些女医学生与女医生所受的对待——那种对待足以使连男人都不像男人。那个时代大体已经过去。大门几乎全都敞开了，至少在有些地方是如此；女性的种种种族活动已能随事理所能允许的程度自由地快速发展。如今主要的斗争，是同这造物自身被扭曲的本性进行的。那些不惜一切代价体现着时代最高精神的女性固然伟大，但我们身上仍压着身后岁月的重负——那无数软弱而渺小的女人，怀着一只多情豚鼠般的心愿。女人的灵魂，必须穿过她那被强化了的性本性的重重积压、穿过一个受饿受挫的阶级的摇摆不定的冲动，才能发出声音。她必须承认自己负重而行。她必须理解自己的困难，并勇敢而坚定地迎向它。

===Original===
But this is a matter for personal volition, for subjective consciousness. The thing to see and to rejoice in is that, with and without their conscious volition, with or without the approval and assistance of men, in spite of that crowning imbecility of history﻿—the banded opposition of some women to the advance of the others﻿—the female of our race is making sure and rapid progress in human development.

===Chinese===
但这是属于个人意志、属于主观意识的事。要看清并且要为之欢欣的是：无论她们有无自觉的意志，无论有无男人的赞许与援助，也尽管有历史那登峰造极的愚妄——一部分女性结伙反对其余女性的前进——我们种族的雌性，正在人类的发展中取得确实而迅速的进步。

## X / 十

===Original===
Although the superior maternity of the human female is so difficult to prove, so open to heavy charges of inadequacy, so erratic and pathological, there remain intact our devout belief in it, our reverence, our unshaken conviction that it is the one perfect thing. The facts as to our carelessness and ignorance in the fulfilment of this function are undeniable: the rate of infant mortality and children’s diseases—those classed by physicians as “preventable diseases,” namely—these mortal errors and failures confront us everywhere; but we ignore them all, or attribute them to any and every reason save deficient motherhood.

===Chinese===
尽管人类雌性这优越的生育机能（maternity）如此难以证明，如此容易招致难孚其任的严重指控，如此反复无常而病态，我们对它的虔诚信仰、我们的崇敬、我们那认定它便是唯一完美之物的坚定信念，却依旧完好无损。我们在履行这一机能时的粗疏与无知，事实俱在、无可否认：婴儿死亡率与儿童疾病——即医生们归为「可预防疾病」的那一类——这些致命的错误与失败在我们面前比比皆是；可我们对它们一概置若罔闻，或者把缘故归到这样那样的任何一点上，唯独不归到母职（motherhood）的欠缺上。

===Original===
One of the most frequent excuses, among those who have gone far enough to admit that excuse is needed, is that the father is to blame for these conditions. His vices, it is alleged, weaken the constitution of the race. His failure to provide prevents the mother from giving the proper care. He is held responsible for what evil we see in our children; and still we worship the mother for the physical process of bearing a child—now considered an act of heroism—and for the “devotion” with which she clings to it afterward, irrespective of the wisdom or effectiveness of this devotion. A healthy and independent motherhood would no more think of taking credit to itself for the right fulfilment of its natural functions than would a cat for bringing forth her kittens or a sheep her lambs. The common fact that the women of the lower social grades bear more children and bear them more easily than the women of higher classes ought to give pause to this ridiculous assumption, but it does not. The more women weaken themselves and their offspring, and imperil their very lives by anti-maternal habits, the more difficulty, danger, and expense are associated with this natural process, the more do women solemnly take credit to themselves and receive it from others for the glorious self-sacrifice with which they risk their lives (and their babies’ lives!) for the preservation of humanity. As to the father and his share in the evil results, nothing that he has ever done or can do removes from motherhood its primal responsibility.

===Chinese===
在那些已经走得足够远、肯承认需要找些借口的人中间，最常见的借口之一，便是这些境况应归咎于父亲。据说他的种种恶习削弱了种族的体质。他供养的失职，使母亲无法给予恰当的照料。我们在孩子身上见到什么恶果，责任都落在他身上；而我们依然崇拜母亲——为她生养孩子的那个身体过程（如今被看作一桩英勇之举），为她此后把孩子紧抱不放的「奉献」，全不顾这奉献明智与否、有效与否。一个健康而独立的母职，绝不会想到要为正常履行自己的自然功能而居功，正如猫不会为生下小猫、母羊不会为产下羔羊而居功一样。较低社会阶层的妇女比较高阶层的妇女生养更多、也更容易生养，这一常见的事实本该叫这荒谬的臆断踌躇止步，可它并没有。女人们越是因反母性的习惯而削弱自己与后代、危害自己的性命，这一自然过程越是伴随着困难、危险与花费，女人们便越是郑重其事地为自己邀功、也从别人那里领受称誉——为着那光辉的自我牺牲，她们为此拿性命（还有她们婴儿的性命！）去冒险，以求人类的保存。至于父亲、以及他在这些恶果中的那份干系，他无论做过什么、能做什么，都卸不去母职那原初的责任。

===Original===
Suppose the female of some other species, ignoring her racial duty of right selection, should mate with mangy, toothless cripples—if there were such among her kind—and so produce weak, malformed young, and help exterminate her race. Should she then blame him for the result? An entire sex, sacredly set apart for maternal functions so superior as to justify their lack of economic usefulness, should in the course of ages have learned how to select proper fathers. If the only way in which the human mother can feed and guard her children is through another person, a provider and protector on whom their lives and safety must depend, what natural, social, or moral excuse has she for not choosing a good one?

===Chinese===
试想某个别的物种的雌性，无视自己正当择配的种族职责，去与生疥癣的、没了牙的跛残者交配——倘若她的同类中真有这等角色——从而产下孱弱畸形的幼崽，帮着灭绝自己的种族。难道她还能反过来为这结果责怪对方吗？整整一个性别，被神圣地专划给那号称优越得足以开脱其经济无用的母性功能，本该在漫长的岁月里学会如何挑选称职的父亲。倘若人类母亲喂养并护卫自己孩子的唯一途径，是经由另一个人——一个她们的性命与安全必须仰赖的供养者与保护者——那么，她不挑一个好的，在自然上、社会上、道德上还有什么借口可言？

===Original===
But how can a young girl know a good prospective father, we ask. That she is not so educated as to know proves her unfitness for her great task. That she does not think or care proves her dishonorable indifference to her great duty. She can in no way shirk the responsibility for criminal carelessness in choosing a father for her children, unless indeed there were no choice—no good men left on earth. Moreover, we are not obliged to leave this crucial choice in the hands of young girls. Motherhood is the work of grown women, not of half-children; and, when we honestly care as much for motherhood as we pretend, we shall train the woman for her duty, not the girl for her guileless maneuvers to secure a husband. We talk about the noble duties of the mother, but our maidens are educated for economically successful marriage.

===Chinese===
可是，我们不禁要问，一个年轻姑娘又怎么认得出一个好的未来父亲呢？她所受的教育竟不足以使她知道，这本身就证明她不配担当那伟大的任务。她不曾想到、也不曾在意，这本身就证明她对那伟大的职责怀有不光彩的漠然。在为自己的孩子挑选父亲这件事上犯了玩忽大意的罪过，这份责任她无论如何推卸不掉——除非当真已无可选择，地球上再没有好人剩下。再者，我们也并非非得把这生死攸关的选择留在年轻姑娘们手里不可。母职是成年妇女的事业，不是半大孩子的营生；等到我们当真像口口声声表露的那样关心母职，我们就会去训练妇女担当她的职责，而不是训练姑娘用她那不设心机的伎俩去猎取丈夫。我们高谈母亲的崇高职责，我们的少女们受的教育却是为着经济上划算的婚姻。

===Original===
Leaving this field of maternal duty through sex-selection, there remains the far larger ground to which the popular mind flees in triumph: that the later work of the mother proves the success of our racial division of labor on sex-lines, that in the care of the child, the education of the child, the beautiful life of the home and family, it is shown how well our system works. This is the last stronghold. Solidly intrenched herein sits popular thought, safe in the sacred precincts of the home. “Every man’s home is his castle,” is the common saying. The windows are shut to keep out the air. The curtains are down to keep out the light. The doors are barred to keep out the stranger. Within are the hearth fire and its gentle priestess, the initial combination of human life—the family in the home.

===Chinese===
撇下经由性选择而行的这一片母职职责的园地，还剩下一片远为宽广的地面，大众的心智正得胜地遁逃而去：母亲的后期工作证明了我们按性别界线划分种族劳动的分工之成功；在孩子的照料中、孩子的教育中、家与家庭的美好生活之中，显示出我们的制度运转得多么良好。这是最后一座堡垒。大众的思想便安坐其中，深沟高垒，稳踞于家的神圣境域之内。「每个人的家都是他的城堡」，这是人们常说的话。窗户关着，把空气挡在外面。帘子垂着，把光挡在外面。门闩上着，把生人挡在外面。里面是炉火和它温柔的女祭司——人类生活最初的那一组合——家宅之中的家庭。

===Original===
Our thrones have been emptied, and turned into mere chairs for passing presidents. Our churches have been opened to the light of modern life, and the odor of sanctity has been freshened with sweet sunny air. We can see room for change in these old sanctuaries, but none in the sanctuary of the home. And this temple, with its rights, is so closely interwound with the services of subject woman, its altar so demands her ceaseless sacrifices, that we find it impossible to conceive of any other basis of human living. We are chilled to the heart’s core by the fear of losing any of these ancient and hallowed associations. Without this blessed background of all our memories and foreground of all our hopes, life seems empty indeed. In homes we were all born. In homes we all die or hope to die. In homes we all live or want to live. For homes we all labor, in them or out of them. The home is the centre and circumference, the start and the finish, of most of our lives. We love it with a love older than the human race. We reverence it with the blind obeisance of those crouching centuries when its cult began. We cling to it with the tenacity of every inmost, oldest instinct of our animal natures, and with the enthusiasm of every latest word in the unbroken chant of adoration which we have sung to it since first we learned to praise.

===Chinese===
我们的王座已被搬空，变成了供过往总统们坐的寻常椅子。我们的教堂已向现代生活的光线敞开，圣洁的气息也因甜美而洒满阳光的空气变得清新。在这些古老的圣所里，我们看得出还有变动的余地；而在家的圣所里，我们看不出任何余地。而这座殿堂，连同它的种种权利，又与处于臣属地位的妇女的服侍如此紧密地缠绕在一起，它的祭坛如此需要她不停的献祭，以至于我们发觉，再无法设想人类生活还有什么别的基础。一想到要失去这些古老而神圣的情谊中的任何一种，我们便从心底深处感到寒意。没有这一片蒙福的背景——我们全部记忆的背景、我们全部希望的远景——生活便当真空虚。我们全都生于家宅。我们全都死于家宅，或希望死于家宅。我们全都活在家宅中，或想活在家宅中。我们全都为家宅而劳作，或在其内，或在其外。家是我们大多数人生活的中心与周界、起点与终点。我们爱它，用一种比人类种族更古老的爱。我们崇敬它，用那些蜷伏的世纪——当对它的崇拜发端之时——的盲目叩拜。我们紧抓住它不放，怀着我们动物天性中一切最深藏、最古老的本能所共有的执拗，也怀着那自我们学会赞美之初便唱个不绝的崇拜之歌里每一个最新字眼的热忱。

===Original===
And since we hold that our home life, just as we have it, is the best thing on earth, and that our home life plainly demands one whole woman at the least to each home, and usually more, it follows that anything which offers to change the position of woman threatens to “undermine the home,” “strikes at the root of the family,” and we will none of it. If, in honest endeavor to keep up to the modern standard of free thought and free speech, we do listen—turning from our idol for a moment, and saying to the daring iconoclast, “Come, show us anything better!”—with what unlimited derision do we greet his proposed substitute! Yet everywhere about us today this inner tower, this castle keep of vanishing tradition, is becoming more difficult to defend or even to keep in repair. We buttress it anew with every generation; we love its very cracks and crumbling corners; we hang and drape it with endless decorations; we hide the looming dangers overhead with fresh clouds of incense; and we demand of the would-be repairers and rebuilders that they prove to us the desirability of their wild plans before they lift a hammer. But, when they show their plans, we laugh them to scorn.

===Chinese===
而既然我们认定，我们的家居生活，就照现在这个样子，便是世上最好的东西；既然我们的家居生活分明要求每家至少搭上整整一个女人，通常还不止一个；那么，凡是提议改变妇女位置的东西，便都威胁着要「动摇家宅的根基」、要「直击家庭之根」，我们一概拒之门外。倘若我们出于跟上现代自由思想与自由言论之标准的诚恳努力，果真肯听一听——暂时从我们的偶像转过脸去，对那大胆的偶像破坏者说：「来，拿出更好的给我们看看！」——我们对他所提的替代方案，又是报以何等无边的嘲弄啊！然而今天，在我们四周，这座内塔，这座正在消逝的传统之城堡主楼，已越来越难于守卫、甚至难于修缮了。我们每一代人都为它重新加上扶壁；我们连它的裂缝和剥落的墙角都爱；我们为它挂上、披上无穷无尽的装饰；我们用新起的香云把头顶上隐隐逼近的危险遮掩起来；我们还要求那些有意修缮与重建的人，先向我们证明他们那狂想方案之可取，然后才许他们抡起锤子。可是，当他们拿出方案来时，我们却报以嘲笑。

===Original===
It is a difficult case to meet. To call attention to existing conditions and to establish the relation between them and existing phenomena is one thing. To point out how a change of condition will produce new phenomena, and how these phenomena will benefit us, is quite another. Yet this is the task that is always involved in the conscious progress of the human race. While that progress was unconscious, it was enough that certain individuals and classes gradually entered into new relations in process of social evolution, and that they forced their conditions upon the reluctant conservatives who failed so to evolve.

===Chinese===
这是一个难于应付的局面。唤起人们对既存境况的注意，并确立这些境况与既存现象之间的关系，是一回事。指出境况的改变将会产生怎样的新现象、这些现象又将怎样造福于我们，则是完全另一回事。然而，这正是人类的自觉进步永远裹挟在身的任务。在那进步还是无意识的年代，只要某些个人与阶级在社会演化进程中渐渐进入新的关系、并把他们的境况强加给那些未能如此演化的不情愿的保守者，也就够了。

===Original===
In the quite recent passage from the feudal to the monarchical system, no time was wasted in the endeavor to persuade and convince the headstrong barons of their national duty. The growing power of the king struggled with and survived the lessening power of the barons—that was all. Had a book been written then to urge the change, it could have proved clearly enough the evils of the feudal system; but, when it tried to portray the glories of national peace and power under a single monarch, it would have had small weight. National peace and power, which had been hitherto nonexistent, would have failed to appeal to the sturdy lords of the soil, whose only idea of peace and power was to sit down and rest on their prostrate neighbors. Had their strength run in the line of argument, they would have scouted the “should be’s” and “will be’s” of the author, and defied him to prove that the new condition would be developed by the new processes; and, indeed, he would have found it hard.

===Chinese===
在晚近由封建制向君主制过渡的那一段，没有一分钟被浪费在劝说顽梗的男爵们认识自己民族义务的努力上。日益增长的王权与日益衰减的男爵权相争而存——如此而已。倘若当时有人写了一本书来鼓吹这变革，它本可以把封建制的种种邪恶证明得够清楚；可当它试图描绘单一君主之下民族的和平与强盛的荣耀时，它便没有多少分量了。民族的和平与强盛，此前从未存在过，无法打动那些壮硕的领主——他们对和平与强盛的全部理解，就是坐下来，把身子靠在被他们打趴下的邻人身上歇息。倘若他们的气力也长于论辩之道，他们定会对作者的「应当如何」与「将会如何」嗤之以鼻，并激他拿出证明：新的境况当真会由新的过程发展出来；而这，他确实也会觉得难办。

===Original===
So today, in questioning the economic status of woman and her position in the home and in the family, it is far easier to prove present evil than future good. Yet this is what is most exactingly demanded. It is required of the advocate of social reform not only that he convince the contented followers of the present system of its wrong, but that he prove to their satisfaction the superiority of some other system. This, in the nature of the case, is impossible. When people are contented, you cannot make them feel that what is is wrong, or that something else might be better. Even the discontented are far more willing to refer their troubles to some personal factor than to admit that their condition as a whole inevitably produces the general trouble in which they share. Even if convinced that a change of condition will remove the source of injury, they, like the fox with the swarm of flies, fear to be disturbed, lest their last state be worse than their first. In the face of this inevitable difficulty, however, the task must be undertaken.

===Chinese===
所以今天，在质疑妇女的经济地位（economic status）以及她在家宅与家庭中的位置时，证明当下的恶，远比证明未来的善容易。然而人们最苛刻索要的，恰是后者。人们要求社会改革的倡导者不仅要说服现制度中心满意足的追随者，使他们认识其错；还要使他们心满意足地信服某种别的制度更为优越。而这一点，就事情的性质而言，是不可能的。当人们心满意足时，你无法使他们感到现存的就是错的，或别的什么也许更好。连心怀不满的人，也更愿意把他们的苦恼归之于某个个人的因素，而不肯承认他们整体的境况必然造成他们所分担的普遍祸患。即便被说服相信境况的改变将除掉祸患的根源，他们也像那身缠一群苍蝇的狐狸，害怕被惊扰，唯恐后来的境况比先前的更糟。然而，面对这无可避免的困难，这任务仍必须承担起来。

===Original===
Two things let us premise and agree upon before starting. First, that the duty of human life is progress, development; that we are here, not merely to live, but to grow—not to be content with lean savagery or fat barbarism or sordid semi-civilization, but to toil on through the centuries, and build up the ever-nobler forms of life toward which social evolution tends. If this is not believed, if any hold that to keep alive and reproduce the species is the limit of our human duty, then they need look no farther here. That aim can be attained, and has been attained, for irrefutable centuries, through many forms of sex-relation and of economic relation. Human beings have lived and brought up children as good as their parents in free promiscuity and laziness, in forced polygamy and slavery, in willing polyandry and industry, and in monogamy *plus* prostitution and manufactures. Just to live and bear children does not prove the relative superiority of any system, either in sex or economics. But, when we believe that life means progress, then each succeeding form of sex-relation or economic relation is to be measured by its effect on that progress.

===Chinese===
出发之前，让我们先立下两条前提并取得一致。第一，人生的职责是进步、是发展；我们在此，不只是为了活着，更是为了生长——不是安于贫瘠的蛮荒、膏腴的野蛮或卑污的半开化，而是劳作不休地穿越诸世纪，建立起社会演化所趋向的那种日益高贵的生命形式。倘若有人不相信这一点，认为苟全性命、繁衍物种便是人类义务的极限，那么他们在这里就无须再往下看了。那个目标能够达到，而且不容置疑地曾在许多个世纪里达到过——凭着许多种形式的性关系（sex-relation）与经济关系（economic relation）。人类曾在自由的乱交与懒惰之中、在强制的多妻制与奴役之中、在自愿的多夫制与勤勉之中、也在一夫一妻制*外加*卖淫与制造业之中，活了下来，也养大了不逊于父母的儿女。仅仅活着并生养儿女，并不能证明任何制度——无论性方面的还是经济方面的——具有相对的优越性。但是，当我们相信人生意味着进步，那么性关系或经济关系的每一种后续形式，便都要以它对那进步的作用来衡量。

===Original===
It may be necessary here to agree on a definition of human progress. According to the general law of organic evolution, it may be defined as follows: such progress in the individual and in his social relations as shall maintain him in health and happiness and increase the organic development of society.

===Chinese===
这里或许有必要就人类进步的定义取得一致。依照有机演化的一般法则，它可以定义如下：个体及其社会关系上的这样一种进步——它能使个体保持于健康与幸福之中，并增进社会的有机发展。

===Original===
If we accept such a definition of human progress, if we agree that progress is the duty of society, and that all social institutions are to be measured by it, we may proceed to our second premise. This is not to be ranked with the first in importance: it should be too commonly understood and accepted to be dragged into such a prominent position. But it is not commonly understood and accepted. In fact, it is misunderstood and denied to so general a degree that no apology is needed for insisting on it here.

===Chinese===
倘若我们接受这样一个人类进步的定义，倘若我们同意进步是社会的职责、一切社会制度都应以此衡量，我们便可以进入第二条前提。这一条在重要性上本不该与第一条并列：它本该是人人明白、人人接受的事，不该被拖到这样显眼的位置上来。可它偏偏不是人人明白、人人接受。事实上，它被误解、被否认的范围如此之广，以至于在这里坚持它，无须任何歉意。

===Original===
The second premise is this: our enjoyment of a thing does not prove that it is right. Even our love, admiration, and reverence for a thing does not prove that it is right; and, even from an evolutionary point of view, our belief that a thing is “natural” does not prove that it is right. A thing may be right in one stage of evolution which becomes wrong in another. For instance, promiscuity is “natural”; the human animal, like many others, is quite easily inclined thereto. Monogamy is proven right by social evolution: it is the best way to carry on the human race in social relation; but it is not yet as “natural” as could be desired.

===Chinese===
第二条前提是：我们对一件事的享用，并不能证明它是对的。甚至我们对一件事的爱、赞美与崇敬，也不能证明它是对的；而且，即便从演化的观点看，我们相信一件事是「自然的」，也不能证明它是对的。一件事在演化的某个阶段是对的，到了另一个阶段可能就成为错的。譬如，乱交是「自然的」；人类这动物，同许多别的动物一样，颇容易倾向于此。一夫一妻制已被社会演化证明为对：它是在社会关系中延续人类种族的最好方式；但它还不像人们所希望的那样「自然」。

===Original===
So, to return to our second premise, which is admittedly rather a large one, to show that any custom or status of ours is “natural” and enjoyable does not prove that it is right. It does not of course prevent its being right. Right things may be enjoyed, may be loved, admired, and reverenced, may even be “natural”; but so may wrong things. Even that subhuman faculty called instinct is only a true guide to conduct when the conditions are present which originally developed that instinct. The instinct that makes a modern house-dog turn around three times before he lies down is not worthy of much admiration today, though it served its purpose on the grassy plains and in the leafy hollows where it was formed. If these two premises are granted, that the duty of human life is progress, and that a given condition is not necessarily right because we like it, we may go on.

===Chinese===
于是，回到我们那第二条前提——这诚然是相当宽泛的一条——证明我们的某种习俗或某种地位是「自然的」、是令人愉快的，并不能证明它是对的。这当然也不妨碍它是对的。对的东西可以被享用，可以被爱、被赞美、被崇敬，甚至可以是「自然的」；但错的东西同样可以。就连被称作本能的那种低于人的官能，也只有当当初形成那本能的条件仍然存在时，才堪为行为的可靠向导。使一条现代家犬在躺下之前转上三圈的那份本能，今天并不值得多少赞赏，虽然在它最初形成的那些青草平原上、那些绿叶洼地里，它也曾尽过自己的职分。倘若这两条前提都得到承认——人生的职责是进步，以及一件事并不因为我们喜欢它就必然是对的——我们就可以继续下去了。

===Original===
Is our present method of home life, based on the economic dependence of woman in the sex-relation, the best calculated to maintain the individual in health and happiness, and develop in him the higher social faculties? The individual is not maintained in health and happiness—that is visible to all; and how little he is developed in social relation is shown in the jarring irregularity and wastefulness of our present economic system.

===Chinese===
我们现行的家居生活方式——奠基于性关系之中妇女的经济依附（economic dependence）之上——是不是最宜于使个体保持健康与幸福、并在他身上发展出更高的社会官能的那种方式呢？个体并没有被保持在健康与幸福之中——这是人人可见的；而他在社会关系中又是何等少地得到发展，则显现在我们现行经济制度那刺耳的无序与浪费之中。

===Original===
Economic independence for women necessarily involves a change in the home and family relation. But, if that change is for the advantage of individual and race, we need not fear it. It does not involve a change in the marriage relation except in withdrawing the element of economic dependence, nor in the relation of mother to child save to improve it. But it does involve the exercise of human faculty in women, in social service and exchange rather than in domestic service solely. This will of course require the introduction of some other form of living than that which now obtains. It will render impossible the present method of feeding the world by means of millions of private servants, and bringing up children by the same hand.

===Chinese===
妇女的经济独立（economic independence）必然牵动家宅与家庭关系的改变。但倘若那改变有利于个体与种族，我们便无须畏惧它。它并不牵动婚姻关系的改变——除了抽去经济依附这一成分；也不牵动母亲与孩子的关系——除了使之改善。但它确实牵动妇女身上人类官能的运用：在社会服务与交换之中，而非仅仅在家内仆役（domestic service）之中。这当然需要引入某种不同于现行的生活方式。它将使现在这种靠千百万私家仆役来供养世界、又用同一双手来养育孩子的办法，再无可能维持。

===Original===
It is a melancholy fact that the vast majority of our children are reared and trained by domestic servants—generally their mothers, to be sure, but domestic servants by trade. To become a producer, a factor in the economic activities of the world, must perforce interfere with woman’s present status as a private servant. House mistress she may still be, in the sense of owning and ordering her home, but housekeeper or house-servant she may not be—and be anything else. Her position as mother will alter, too. Mother in the sense of bearer and rearer of noble children she will be, as the closest and dearest, the one most honored and best loved; but mother in the sense of exclusive individual nursery-maid and nursery-governess she may not be—and be anything else.

===Chinese===
一个可悲的事实是：我们的孩子，绝大多数是由家内仆役养育和训练的——诚然，多半是他们的母亲，但那是以此为业的家内仆役。成为一个生产者、成为世界经济活动中的一个因素，就必然要妨碍妇女现在这种私家仆役的地位。她仍可以是家宅的女主人——就拥有并支配自己的家宅而言；但她不能再做女管家（housekeeper）或家内佣工（house-servant）——同时又做别的什么。她作为母亲的地位也将改变。作为高贵儿女的生育者与养育者的母亲，她仍将是——那最亲近、最亲爱的，最受尊崇、也最被热爱的一个；但作为独占的、个体的育儿女仆与保育女教师的母亲，她便不能再是——同时又做别的什么。

===Original===
It is precisely here that the world calls a halt. Nothing, it says, can be better than our homes with their fair priestesses. Nothing can be better for children than the hourly care of their own mothers. It is the position of the feudal baron over again. We can perhaps be made to see the evils of existing conditions: we cannot be made to see any possibility of improving on them. Nevertheless, it may be tried.

===Chinese===
恰恰是在这里，世界喊出了止步。它说，没有什么能比我们的家宅与家宅里那些美丽的女祭司更好。没有什么能比孩子们自己母亲时时刻刻的照料对他们更好。这又是封建男爵的那副老位置。或许还能教我们看见既存境况的种种邪恶：却怎么也教我们看不见对它们加以改进的任何可能。尽管如此，还是不妨一试。

===Original===
Let us deliberately set ourselves to imagine, by sheer muscular effort as it were, a better kind of motherhood than that of the private nursery governess, a better way to feed and clean and clothe the world than by the private house servant.

===Chinese===
让我们特意打定主意去想象——几乎是凭纯粹的筋肉之力——一种比私人保育女教师更好的母职，一种比用私家家内佣工来喂养、清洁、衣着这个世界更好的办法。

===Original===
Here is felt the need of our second premise; for we enjoy things as they are (that is, some of us do, sometimes, and the rest of us think that we do). We love, admire, and reverence them; and it is “natural” to have them so. If it can be shown that human progress is better served by other methods, then other methods will be proven right; and we must grow to enjoy and honor them as fast as we can, and in due course of time we shall find them natural. If it can be shown that our babies would be better off if part of their time was passed in other care than their mothers’, then such other care would be right; and it would be the duty of motherhood to provide it. If it can be shown that we could all be better provided for in our personal needs of nutrition, cleanliness, warmth, shelter, privacy, by some other method than that which requires the labor of one woman or more to each family, then it would be the duty of womanhood to find such method and to practise it.

===Chinese===
在这里便感到了第二条前提的必要；因为我们享用着事物的现状（这就是说，我们中有些人有时确乎享用，其余的人则自以为享用）。我们爱它们、赞美它们、崇敬它们；而它们如此这般，也是「自然的」。倘若能够表明，别种方法能更好地服务于人类进步，那么别种方法便将被证明为对；我们就必须尽快学会享用它们、尊崇它们，而假以时日，我们自会发现它们是自然的。倘若能够表明，我们的婴孩把一部分时间交给母亲以外的照料、处境会更好，那么这样的其他照料便是对的；提供这种照料，便是母职的职责。倘若能够表明，我们每个人在营养、清洁、温暖、居所、私密这些个人需要上，都可以由某种别的方法得到更好的照应，而无须像现在这样要求每个家庭投入一个或更多女人的劳作，那么找到这样的方法并践行它，便是做女人（womanhood）的职责。

===Original===
Perhaps it is worthwhile to examine the nature of our feeling toward that social institution called “the family,” and the probable effect upon it of the change in woman’s economic status.

===Chinese===
或许值得考察一下我们对那称为「家庭」的社会制度怀有怎样的情感、其本性如何，以及妇女经济地位的改变可能对它产生的影响。

===Original===
Marriage and “the family” are two institutions, not one, as is commonly supposed. We confuse the natural result of marriage in children, common to all forms of sex-union, with the family—a purely social phenomenon. Marriage is a form of sex-union recognized and sanctioned by society. It is a relation between two or more persons, according to the custom of the country, and involves mutual obligations. Although made by us an economic relation, it is not essentially so, and will exist in much higher fulfilment after the economic phase is outgrown.

===Chinese===
婚姻与「家庭」是两种制度，并非如通常所想的那样是一种。我们把婚姻在儿女身上的自然结果——一切性结合（sex-union）形式所共有的那种结果——同家庭混为一谈，而家庭纯粹是一种社会现象。婚姻是社会所承认并核准的一种性结合形式。它是一种介于两人或多人之间的关系——依各国的习俗而定——并牵涉相互的义务。它虽被我们弄成了一种经济关系，本质上却并非如此；待经济阶段被超越之后，它将以远为完满的方式存在下去。

===Original===
The family is a social group, an entity, a little state. It holds an important place in the evolution of society quite aside from its connection with marriage. There was a time when the family was the highest form of social relation—indeed, the only form of social relation—when to the minds of pastoral, patriarchal tribes there was no conception so large as “my country,” no State, no nation. There was only a great land spotted with families, each family its own little world, of which Grandpa was priest and king. The family was a social unit. Its interests were common to its members, and inimical to those of other families. It moved over the earth, following its food supply, and fighting occasionally with stranger families for the grass or water on which it depended. Indissoluble common interests are what make organic union, and those interests long rested on blood relationship.

===Chinese===
家庭是一个社会群体、一个实体、一个小小的国家。在社会演化中，它占有一个重要的位置，而这一位置与它同婚姻的联系无干。曾有一个时代，家庭是社会关系的最高形式——事实上，是唯一的形式——那时，在游牧的、父权制的部落心目中，没有什么概念大得过「我的国家」，没有国家，没有民族。只有一片点缀着一个个家庭的大地，每个家庭自成一个小世界，老祖父便是这世界的祭司兼国王。家庭是一个社会单位。它的利益为其成员所共享，而与其他家庭的利益相敌。它在大地上迁徙，追随着它的食物来源，偶尔也同陌生的家庭为它所倚赖的草地或水源而战。不可分解的共同利益造就有机的联合，而那些利益长久以来便寄托于血缘关系之上。

===Original===
While the human individual was best fed and guarded by the family, and so required the prompt, correlative action of all the members of that family, naturally the family must have a head; and that form of government known as the patriarchal was produced. The natural family relation, as seen in parents and young of other species, or in ourselves in later forms, involves no such governmental development: that is a feature of the family as a social entity alone.

===Chinese===
当人类个体还最妥善地由家庭供养并护卫、因而需要那一家庭全体成员及时而协同的行动时，家庭自然而然必须有一个头目；于是便产生了那种被称作父权制的统治形式。自然的家庭关系——如见于其他物种的亲代与幼崽之间，或见于我们自己较晚的形式之中——并不牵涉这样的统治发展：那是家庭作为社会实体才独有的特征。

===Original===
One of the essentials of the patriarchal family life was polygamy, and not only polygamy, but open concubinage, and a woman slavery which was almost the same thing. The highest period of the family as a social institution was a very low period for marriage as a social institution—a period, in fact, when marriage was but partially evolved from the early promiscuity of the primitive savage. The family seems indeed to be a gradually disappearing survival of the still looser unit of the horde, which again is more closely allied to the band or pack of gregarious carnivora than to an organic social relation. A loose, promiscuous group of animals is not a tribe; and the most primitive savage groups seem to have been no more than this.

===Chinese===
父权制家庭生活的要项之一便是多妻制，而且不止是多妻制，还有公然的纳妾，以及一种几乎与之无异的妇女奴役。家庭作为社会制度的鼎盛时期，恰是婚姻作为社会制度的极低时期——事实上，那是一个婚姻才从原始野蛮人的早期乱交中局部演化出来的时期。家庭看来确乎是那更为松散的单位——游群（horde）——的一种渐次消逝的遗存；而游群呢，又与其说近于一种有机的社会关系，不如说更近于群居食肉兽的帮与群。一群松散而乱交的动物并不是部落；而最原始的野蛮人群体，看来也不过如此。

===Original===
The tribe in its true form follows the family—is a natural extension of it, and derives its essential ties from the same relationship. These social forms, too, are closely related to economic conditions. The horde was the hunting unit; the family, and later the tribe, the pastoral unit. Agriculture and its resultant, commerce and manufacture, gradually weaken these crude blood ties, and establish the social relationship which constitutes the State. Before the pastoral era the family held no important position, and since that era it has gradually declined. With social progress we find human relations resting less and less on a personal and sex basis, and more and more on general economic independence. As individuals have become more highly specialized, they have made possible a higher form of marriage.

===Chinese===
真正形式的部落接在家庭之后——它是家庭的一种自然扩展，其根本的纽带也出自同一种关系。这些社会形态，同样与经济条件紧密相关。游群是狩猎的单位；家庭，以及后来的部落，则是游牧的单位。农业及其结果——商业与制造业——渐渐削弱了这些粗糙的血缘纽带，建立起构成国家的那种社会关系。在游牧时代之前，家庭并不占重要位置；自那个时代以来，它又渐渐衰落。随着社会的进步，我们发现人际关系愈来愈少地奠基于个人的与性的基础，愈来愈多地奠基于普遍的经济独立。随着个体变得愈益高度专门化，一种更高的婚姻形式也随之成为可能。

===Original===
The family is a decreasing survival of the earliest grouping known to man. Marriage is an increasing development of high social life, not fully evolved. So far from being identical with the family, it improves and strengthens in inverse ratio to the family, as is easily seen by the broad contrast between the marriage relations of Jacob and the unquenchable demand for lifelong single mating that grows in our hearts today. There was no conception of marriage as a personal union for life of two well-matched individuals during the patriarchal era. Wives were valued merely for childbearing. The family needed numbers of its own blood, especially males; and the man-child was the price of favor to women then. It was but a few degrees beyond the horde, not yet become a tribe in the full sense. Its bonds of union were of the loosest—merely common paternity, with a miscellaneous maternity of inimical interests. Such a basis forever forbade any high individualization, and high individualization with its demands for a higher marriage forbids any numerical importance to the family. Marriage has risen and developed in social importance as the family has sunk and decreased.

===Chinese===
家庭是人类已知最早的那种群聚形式日渐衰减的遗存。婚姻则是高尚社会生活日益增长的发展，尚未完全演化成形。它非但与家庭并不相同，反而与家庭成反比地改善着、增强着——这一点，只要把雅各（Jacob）的婚姻关系同今日我们心中那不可遏抑的终身单配的要求两相对照，便一目了然。在父权制时代，没有把婚姻看作两个匹配的个体之间终身个人结合的观念。妻子之所以受重视，仅仅是为了生育。家庭需要自家血缘的数目，尤其是男性；那时，生下男孩便是妇女换取宠幸的代价。那时代不过超出游群几度而已，尚未在完全的意义上成为部落。它的联合纽带松散之至——不过是一个共同的父系，加上利害彼此敌对、驳杂不一的母系罢了。这样的基础永远禁止了任何高度的个体化（individualization）；而高度的个体化连同它对更高婚姻的要求，又禁止了家庭在数目上的重要地位。家庭愈是沉落、愈是缩减，婚姻便愈是在社会重要性上兴起并发展。

===Original===
It is most interesting to note that, under the comparatively similar conditions of the settlement of Utah, the numerical strength and easily handled common interests of many people under one head, which distinguish the polygamous family, were found useful factors in that great pioneering enterprise. In the further development of society a relation of individuals more fluent, subtle, and extensive was needed. The family as a social unit makes a ponderous body of somewhat irreconcilable constituents, requiring a sort of military rule to make it work at all; and it is only useful while the ends to be attained are of a simple nature, and allow of the slowest accomplishment. It is easy to see the family extending to the tribe by its own physical increase; and, similarly, the father hardening into the chief, under the necessities of larger growth. Then, as the steadily enlarging forces of national unity make the chief an outgrown name and the tribe an outgrown form, the family dwindles to a monogamic basis, as the higher needs of the sex-relation become differentiated from the more primitive economic necessities of the family.

===Chinese===
最耐人寻味的是：在犹他（Utah）拓殖那些相对相似的条件之下，多妻制家庭所独具的那种人数之众、以及一头统领之下许多人的共同利益之易于打理，竟被证明是那桩伟大拓荒事业中大有用的因素。在社会进一步的发展中，需要一种更流畅、更精微、更宽广的个体间关系。家庭作为一个社会单位，构成一个由颇难调和的成分组成的笨重躯体，非要一种近乎军法的统治才能使它勉强运转；而且，只有当所要达到的目的性质简单、容得下最迟缓的完成时，它才派得上用场。不难看出，家庭如何凭自身的繁衍扩展为部落；同样，也不难看出，父亲如何在更大规模的必然之下硬化为首领。随后，当民族统一的力量稳步扩张，使首领成为一个过时的名号、部落成为一种过时的形式，家庭便萎缩到一夫一妻的基础上——因为性关系的更高需要，已同家庭那些较原始的经济必然区分开来。

===Original===
And now, further, when our still developing social needs call for an ever-increasing delicacy and freedom in the interservice and common service of individuals, we find that even what economic unity remains to the family is being rapidly eliminated. As the economic relation becomes rudimentary and disappears, the sex-relation asserts itself more purely; and the demand in the world today for a higher and nobler sex-union is as sharply defined as the growing objection to the existing economic union. Strange as it may seem to us, so long accustomed to confound the two it is precisely the outgrown relics of a previously valuable family relation which so painfully retard the higher development of the monogamic marriage relation.

===Chinese===
而现在，更进一步，当我们仍在发展着的社会需要，要求个体之间的互济服务与共同服务有愈益精微的自由时，我们发觉，连家庭所残留的那一点经济统一，也正被迅速清除。随着经济关系变得只剩残迹、归于消失，性关系便愈纯粹地申明自身；今日世界上对一种更高尚、更崇高的性结合的要求，其轮廓之鲜明，一如对现存经济结合日益增长的反对。尽管在我们这些久已惯于将二者混为一谈的人看来似乎奇怪，但恰恰是那曾经宝贵的家庭关系留下的过时残余，如此痛苦地阻碍着一夫一妻制婚姻关系的更高发展。

===Original===
Each generation of young men and women comes to the formation of sex-union with higher and higher demands for a true marriage, with ever-growing needs for companionship. Each generation of men and women need and ask more of each other. A woman is no longer content and grateful to have “a kind husband”: a man is no longer content with a patient Griselda; and, as all men and women, in marrying, revert to the economic status of the earlier family, they come under conditions which steadily tend to lower the standard of their mutual love, and make of the average marriage only a sort of compromise, borne with varying ease or difficulty according to the good breeding and loving-kindness of the parties concerned. This is not necessarily, to their conscious knowledge, an “unhappy marriage.” It is as happy as those they see about them, as happy perhaps as we resignedly expect “on earth”; and in heaven we do not expect marriages. But it is not what they looked forward to when they were young.

===Chinese===
一代又一代的青年男女走向性结合的缔结，带着对真正婚姻愈来愈高的要求，带着对伴侣之谊永不停止增长的需要。一代又一代的男女，彼此需要得更多，也彼此索求得更多。女人再不会因有了「一个体贴的丈夫」而知足感激；男人再不会满足于一个耐心的格丽泽尔达（Griselda）；而由于所有男女一结婚便退回到早期家庭的经济地位，他们便落入了一种境况——这境况稳稳地趋于压低他们相互爱情的标准，把寻常的婚姻弄成不过是一种妥协，其忍受之难易，全看当事人的教养与慈爱如何。这倒未必——就他们自己的自觉而言——是一桩「不快的婚姻」。它同他们四周看到的那些一样快乐，或许也同我们逆来顺受地在「尘世」上期待的一样快乐；而在天堂里，我们是不指望有婚姻的。但它终究不是他们年轻时所盼望的那一种。

===Original===
When two young people love each other, in the long hours which are never long enough for them to be together in, do they dwell in ecstatic forecast on the duties of housekeeping? They do not. They dwell on the pleasure of having a home, in which they can be “at last alone”; on the opportunity of enjoying each other’s society; and, always, on what they will *do* together. To act with those we love—to walk together, work together, read together, paint, write, sing, anything you please, so that it be together—that is what love looks forward to.

===Chinese===
当两个年轻人彼此相爱，在那对他们而言永远不够长、供他们厮守的长时里，他们可曾在狂喜的憧憬中细细盘算过操持家务的种种职责吗？没有。他们盘算的是拥有一个家的快乐——在那里他们可以「终于独处」；是彼此相伴的机会；而且，永远盘算着他们将一同*做*什么。同所爱的人一起行动——一起散步、一起劳作、一起读书，一起作画、写作、歌唱，随你什么，只要是一起——那才是爱情所前瞻的东西。

===Original===
Human love, as it rises to an ever higher grade, looks more and more for such companionship. But the economic status of marriage rudely breaks in upon love’s young dream. On the economic side, apart from all the sweetness and truth of the sex-relation, the woman in marrying becomes the house-servant, or at least the housekeeper, of the man. Of the world we may say that the intimate personal necessities of the human animal are ministered to by woman. Married lovers do not work together. They may, if they have time, rest together: they may, if they can, play together; but they do not make beds and sweep and cook together, and they do not go down town to the office together. They are economically on entirely different social planes, and these constitute a bar to any higher, truer union than such as we see about us. Marriage is not perfect unless it is between class equals. There is no equality in class between those who do their share in the world’s work in the largest, newest, highest ways and those who do theirs in the smallest, oldest, lowest ways.

===Chinese===
人的爱随着它升到愈来愈高的等级，愈来愈指望这样的伴侣之谊。但婚姻的经济地位粗暴地闯入了爱情这场青春的梦。在经济的一面——撇开性关系全部的甘美与真诚——女人一结婚，便成了男人的家内佣工，至少也是他的女管家。就这个世界而言，我们可以说：人类动物那些亲密的个人需要，是由女人来照应的。结了婚的恋人并不一同劳作。他们若有余暇，或许可以一同休息；他们若能够，或许可以一同游乐；但他们不一同铺床、扫地、做饭，也不一同进城到办公室去。他们在经济上处于全然不同的社会层面，而这些层面便构成一道栅栏，把任何比我们四围所见更高、更真实的结合都拦在外面。婚姻若要完满，除非它缔结于阶级相等的人之间。以最宏大、最新、最高的方式在世界工作中尽其一份之力的人，与以最狭小、最旧、最低的方式尽其一份之力的人之间，是没有阶级的平等可言的。

===Original===
Granting squarely that it is the business of women to make the home life of the world true, healthful, and beautiful, the economically dependent woman does not do this, and never can. The economically independent woman can and will. As the family is by no means identical with marriage, so is the home by no means identical with either.

===Chinese===
痛痛快快地承认吧——使世界的家居生活真实、健康而美丽，本是妇女的本分——可经济上依附的女人做不到这一点，而且永远做不到。经济独立的女人做得到，也将会做到。正如家庭绝不等同于婚姻一样，家也绝不等同于这二者中的任何一个。

===Original===
A home is a permanent dwelling-place, whether for one, two, forty, or a thousand, for a pair, a flock, or a swarm. The hive is the home of the bees as literally and absolutely as the nest is the home of mating birds in their season. Home and the love of it may dwindle to the one chamber of the bachelor or spread to the span of a continent, when the returning traveller sees land and calls it “home.” There is no sweeter word, there is no dearer fact, no feeling closer to the human heart than this.

===Chinese===
家是一个长久的居所，无论它容的是一个人、两个人、四十个人还是一千个人，是一对、一群还是一窝。蜂巢是蜜蜂的家，正如鸟巢是应季交配的鸟儿的家一样，是字面而绝对地成立的。家与对家的爱，可以缩到独身者的一间斗室，也可以展到一个大陆的幅员——当归来的旅人望见陆地，唤它作「家」。再没有更甜的字眼，再没有更亲的事实，再没有比这更贴近人心的情感了。

===Original===
On close analysis, what are the bases of our feelings in this connection? and what are their supporting facts? Far down below humanity, where “the foxes have holes, and the birds of the air have nests,” there begins the deep home feeling. Maternal instinct seeks a place to shelter the defenceless young, while the mother goes abroad to search for food. The first sharp impressions of infancy are associated with the sheltering walls of home, be it the swinging cradle in the branches, the soft dark hollow in the trunk of a tree, or the cave with its hidden lair. A place to be safe in; a place to be warm and dry in; a place to eat in peace and sleep in quiet; a place whose close, familiar limits rest the nerves from the continuous hail of impressions in the changing world outside: the same place over and over—the restful repetition, rousing no keen response, but healing and soothing each weary sense—that “feels like home.” All this from our first consciousness. All this for millions and millions of years. No wonder we love it.

===Chinese===
细加分析，我们在这层关联上的情感，其根基是什么？其支撑的事实又是什么？在远低于人类的深处——在那里「狐狸有洞，天空的飞鸟有窝」——深深的家之感便开始了。母性本能要寻一个地方庇护无告的幼小，好让母亲外出觅食。婴孩时期最初那些鲜明的印象，全都同家的蔽护之墙联结在一起——无论那是枝头摇曳的摇篮、树干里柔软幽暗的洞窝，还是藏着巢穴的山洞。一个可以安身的地方；一个温暖干燥的地方；一个可以安然进食、静静安睡的地方；一个以其紧窄而熟稔的界限，让神经从外面变幻世界里接连不断的印象之雹中得到歇息的地方：同一个地方，一遍又一遍——那安适的重复，激不起敏锐的反应，却治愈并抚平每一处疲惫的感官——那便是「有家的感觉」。这一切自我们最初的意识便开始了。这一切绵延了千百万又千百万年。难怪我们爱它。

===Original===
Then comes the gradual addition of tenderer associations, family ties of the earliest. Then, still primitive, but not yet outgrown, the groping religious sentiment of early ancestor-worship, adding sanctity to safety, and driving deep our sentiment for home. It was the place in which to pray, to keep alight the sacred fire, and pour libations to departed grandfathers. Following this, the slow-dying era of paternal government gave a new sense of honor to the place of comfort and the place of prayer. It became the seat of government also—the palace and the throne. Upon this deep foundation we have built a towering superstructure of habit, custom, law; and in it dwell together every deepest, oldest, closest, and tenderest emotion of the human individual. No wonder we are blind and deaf to any suggested improvement in our lordly pleasure-house.

===Chinese===
接着，渐渐添上了更温柔的联想——最早的家庭纽带。再接着，是那仍然原始、却尚未褪去的早期祖先崇拜中摸索的宗教情感，它给安全加上了神圣，把我们对于家的情感深深打入地底。那是一个可以祈祷的地方，一个让圣火长明不熄的地方，一个向故去的祖父们奠酒的地方。随后，父权统治那迟迟不肯断气的时代，又给这安适之所、这祈祷之所添上了一种新的荣耀感。它还成了统治的所在——宫殿与王座。在这深厚的地基之上，我们建起了习惯、风俗、法律那高耸的上层建筑；人类个体每一种最深、最古、最切、最柔的情感，都一同栖居其中。难怪对我们这座堂皇的行乐之殿，任何改建的建议我们都充耳不闻、视而不见。

===Original===
But look farther. Without contradicting any word of the above, it is equally true that the highest emotions of humanity arise and live outside the home and apart from it. While religion stayed at home, in dogma and ceremony, in spirit and expression, it was a low and narrow religion. It could never rise till it found a new spirit and a new expression in human life outside the home, until it found a common place of worship, a ceremonial and a morality on a human basis, not a family basis. Science, art, government, education, industry—the home is the cradle of them all, and their grave, if they stay in it. Only as we live, think, feel, and work outside the home, do we become humanly developed, civilized, socialized.

===Chinese===
但再看远一点。丝毫不与上述任何一句话相抵触，下面这一点同样为真：人类最高的诸种情感，是在家宅之外、离开家宅而生、而活的。当宗教还待在家里——在教条与仪轨之中，在精神与表达之中——它是一种低下而狭隘的宗教。直到它在家庭之外的人类生活中找到一种新的精神与新的表达，直到它找到一个共同的礼拜之所、一套以人类为基础而非以家庭为基础的仪轨与道德，它才得以上升。科学、艺术、统治、教育、产业——家是它们共同的摇篮，也是它们共同的坟墓，倘若它们一直待在里面。唯有当我们在家宅之外生活、思考、感受并劳作，我们才得到属人的发展，才文明化、社会化。

===Original===
The exquisite development of modern home life is made possible only as an accompaniment and result of modern social life. If the reverse were true, as is popularly supposed, all nations that have homes would continue to evolve a noble civilization. But they do not. On the contrary, those nations in which home and family worship most prevail, as in China, present a melancholy proof of the result of the domestic virtues without the social. A noble home life is the product of a noble social life. The home does not produce the virtues needed in society. But society does produce the virtues needed in such homes as we desire today. The members of the freest, most highly civilized and individualized nations, make the most delightful members of the home and family. The members of the closest and most highly venerated homes do not necessarily make the most delightful members of society.

===Chinese===
现代家居生活那精微的发展，唯有作为现代社会生活的伴随与结果，方为可能。倘若真相恰如流俗所想的那样颠倒过来，那么一切有家宅的民族都将不断演化出高贵的文明来。可事实并非如此。恰恰相反，那些家宅与家庭崇拜最盛行的民族——如在中国——正提供了一个可悲的证明：只有家内之德而无社会之德，会结出怎样的果。高贵的家居生活是高贵的社会生活的产物。家宅并不产出社会所需要的诸德；而社会却确乎产出我们今日所向往的那种家宅所需要的诸德。最自由、最文明、最个体化的民族，其成员恰是家宅与家庭中最可爱的成员；而最紧密、最受尊崇的家宅中的成员，却未必就是社会中最可爱的成员。

===Original===
In social evolution as in all evolution the tendency is from “indefinite, incoherent homogeneity to definite, coherent heterogeneity”; and the home, in its rigid maintenance of a permanent homogeneity, constitutes a definite limit to social progress. What we need is not less home, but more; not a lessening of the love of human beings for a home, but its extension through new and more effective expression. And, above all, we need the complete disentanglement in our thoughts of the varied and often radically opposed interests and industries so long supposed to be component parts of the home and family.

===Chinese===
社会演化同一切演化一样，其趋势是「从不定的、不连贯的同质，走向定的、连贯的异质」（from indefinite, incoherent homogeneity to definite, coherent heterogeneity）；而家宅，由于它僵死地维持着一种恒常的同质，便构成了社会进步的一道确定的界限。我们需要的不是更少的家，而是更多；不是减少人类对家的爱，而是经由新的、更有效的表达去扩展它。而首要的是，我们需要在思想中把那些五花八门、而且往往根本相敌的利益与产业，从那长久以来被当作家宅与家庭之组成部分的捆绑中，彻底地解开来。

===Original===
The change in the economic position of woman from dependence to independence must bring with it a rearrangement of these home interests and industries, to our great gain.

===Chinese===
妇女经济位置这一由依附到独立的转变，必然随之带来这些家宅利益与家宅产业的一次重整——而我们将由此获益良多。

## XI / 十一

===Original===
As a natural consequence of our division of labor on sex-lines, giving to woman the home and to man the world in which to work, we have come to have a dense prejudice in favor of the essential womanliness of the home duties, as opposed to the essential manliness of every other kind of work. We have assumed that the preparation and serving of food and the removal of dirt, the nutritive and excretive processes of the family, are feminine functions; and we have also assumed that these processes must go on in what we call the home, which is the external expression of the family. In the home the human individual is fed, cleaned, warmed, and generally cared for, while not engaged in working in the world.

===Chinese===
作为我们按性别界线划分劳动分工的自然结果——这分工把家宅给了女人，把供人劳作的世界给了男人——我们渐渐养成了一种根深蒂固的偏见：家务职责本质上属于女人气，其他一切工作本质上都属于男人气。我们认定，食物的备办与奉上、污垢的清除——家庭那营养与排泄的诸过程——是女性的功能（feminine functions）；我们还认定，这些过程必须在所谓的家宅之中进行，而家宅正是家庭的外在表达。在家宅之中，人类个体于不在世界上劳作的当口，被喂养、被清洁、被供暖，并受到方方面面的照料。

===Original===
Human nutrition is a long process. There’s many a ship ’twixt the cup and the lip, to paraphrase an old proverb. Food is produced by the human race collectively﻿—not by individuals for their own consumption, but by interrelated groups of individuals, all over the world, for the world’s consumption. This collectively produced food circulates over the earth’s surface through elaborate processes of transportation, exchange, and preparation, before it reaches the mouths of the consumers; and the final processes of selection and preparation are in the hands of woman. She is the final purchaser: she is the final handler in that process of human nutrition known as cooking, which is a sort of extra-organic digestion proven advantageous to our species. This department of human digestion has become a sex-function, supposed to pertain to women by nature.

===Chinese===
人类的营养是一个漫长的过程。套改一句古谚来说：杯到嘴边，还隔着许多条船。食物是由人类集体生产的——不是由个人为自己消费而生产，而是由全世界相互关联的一群群个人，为世界的消费而生产。这些集体生产出来的食物，经过运输、交换与备办的繁复过程，在大地表面流转，方才抵达消费者的口中；而最后的挑选与备办诸过程，握在女人手里。她是最终的购买者：她是人类营养那名为烹饪的过程里最后的经手人——烹饪乃是一种业已证明对我们物种有益的体外消化（extra-organic digestion）。人类消化的这个部门，就这样变成了一种性功能（sex-function），被认定天然属于女人。

===Original===
If it is to the advantage of the human race that its food supply should be thus handled by a special sex, this advantage should be shown in superior health and purity of habit. But no such advantage is visible. In spite of all our power and skill in the production and preparation of food we remain “the sickest beast alive” in the matter of eating. Our impotent outcries against adulteration prove that part of the trouble is in the food products as offered for purchase, the pathetic reiteration of our numerous cookbooks proves that part of the trouble is in the preparation of those products, and the futile exhortations of physicians and mothers prove that part of the trouble is in our morbid tastes and appetites. It would really seem as if the human race after all its long centuries had not learned how to prepare good food, nor how to cook it, nor how to eat it﻿—which is painfully true.

===Chinese===
倘若人类的食物供应交由一个专门的性别如此经手，于人类种族有利，那么这利益本应显现在更优越的健康与更纯净的习惯上。可是不见任何这样的好处。尽管我们在食物的生产与备办上拥有全部的力量与技艺，在吃这件事上，我们仍旧是「现存病得最重的野兽」。我们对掺假那无力的怒声，证明麻烦有一部分出在出售的食品本身；我们那数量可观的食谱一遍遍可怜的重复，证明麻烦有一部分出在这些食品的备办上；医生与母亲们徒劳的谆谆劝诫，又证明麻烦有一部分出在我们病态的口味与食欲上。这真叫人觉得，人类种族历经这许多个漫长世纪，竟还没有学会怎样备办好食物，怎样烹调它，怎样吃它——而这正是痛切的事实。

===Original===
This great function of human nutrition is confounded with the sex-relation, and is considered a sex-function: it is in the helpless hands of that amiable but abortive agent, the economically dependent women; and the essential incapacity of such an agent is not hard to show. In her position as private house-steward she is the last purchaser of the food of the world, and here we reach the governing factor in our incredible adulteration of food products.

===Chinese===
人类营养这一重大机能被同性关系（sex-relation）混为一谈，被看作一种性功能：它握在那可亲却难成事的代理人——经济上依附的妇女——那无告的手中；而这样一个代理人在本质上的无能，是不难指明的。她以私家家宅总管（house-steward）的身份，做着全世界食物的最后一手购买者；于是我们便触到了食品掺假那令人难以置信的现状背后的支配性因素。

===Original===
All kinds of deceit and imposition in human service are due to that desire to get without giving, which, as has been shown in previous chapters, is largely due to the training of women as nonproductive consumers. But the particular form of deceit and imposition practised by a given dealer is governed by the intelligence and power of the buyer. The dilution and adulteration of food products is a particularly easy path to profit, because the ultimate purchaser has almost no power and very little intelligence. The individual housewife must buy at short intervals and in small quantities. This operates to her pecuniary disadvantage, as is well known; but its effect on the quality of her purchases is not so commonly observed. Not unless she becomes the head of a wealthy household, and so purchases in quantity for family, servants, and guests, is her trade of sufficient value to have force in the market. The dealer who sells to a hundred poor women can and does sell a much lower quality of food than he who sells an equal amount to one purchaser. Therefore, the home, as a food agency, holds an essentially and permanently unfavorable position as a purchaser; and it is thereby the principal factor in maintaining the low standard of food products against which we struggle with the cumbrous machinery of legislation.

===Chinese===
人类服务中形形色色的欺骗与蒙混，都出自那不劳而获的欲望——正如前几章已表明的，这欲望又在很大程度上归因于把妇女训练成非生产性的消费者。但某个商贩施行欺骗与蒙混的特定形式，则取决于购买者的智力与力量。食品的掺水与掺假是一条格外好走的牟利之路，因为那最终的购买者几乎全无力量，智力也少得可怜。单个的家庭主妇必须隔三差五、零敲碎打地购买。这对她钱财上的不利是人所共知的；可它对她所购货品品质的影响，却较少被人留意。除非她成了一户富贵人家的主妇，为家庭、仆役与宾客大批采买，否则她那点生意便不足以在市场上形成分量。把同样多的货卖给一百个穷女人的商贩，能够、而且确乎在售卖比把等量货品卖给一个买主的人低劣得多的食品。因此，家宅作为一个食品供应机构，在购买者的位置上占着一种本质而恒久的不利地位；也正因如此，它成了维持食品低标准的首要因素——我们正是拖着立法那笨重的机器，同这低标准搏斗。

===Original===
Most housekeepers will innocently prove their ignorance of these matters by denying that the standard of food products is so low. Let such offended ladies but examine the statutes and ordinances of their own cities﻿—of any civilized city﻿—and see how the bread, the milk, the meat, the fruit, are under a steady legislative inspection which endeavors to protect the ignorance and helplessness of the individual purchaser. If the private housekeeper had the technical intelligence as purchaser which is needed to discriminate in the selection of foods, if she were prepared to test her milk, to detect the foreign substance in her coffee and spices, rightly to estimate the quality of her meat and the age of her fruit and vegetables, she would then be able at least to protest against her supply, and to seek, as far as time, distance, and funds allowed, a better market. This technical intelligence, however, is only to be obtained by special study and experience; and its attainment only involves added misery and difficulty to the private purchaser, unless accompanied by the power to enforce what the intelligence demands.

===Chinese===
大多数女管家（housekeeper）们会以否认食品标准竟如此低劣的方式，天真地证明她们对这些事情的无知。且让这些被冒犯的太太们去查一查她们自己城市的法令与条例——随便哪座文明城市的都行——看看面包、牛奶、肉食、水果，处在怎样一种持续的立法监督之下：那监督所竭力维护的，正是个体购买者的无知与无告。倘若私家的女管家具备食品挑选上甄别所需的专门知识，倘若她有本领检验她的牛奶、识破她咖啡与香料里的杂物、正确估量她肉食的品质与她果蔬的时令，那么她至少还能对货源提出抗议，并在时间、距离与财力允许的范围内另寻较好的市场。然而这种专门知识，唯有通过专门的学习与历练方能获得；而对私家购买者来说，获得了它，也只是平添苦恼与烦难——除非同时握有强制实行这知识所要求之事的力量。

===Original===
As it is, woman brings to her selection from the world’s food only the empirical experience gained by practising upon her helpless family, and this during the very time when her growing children need the wise care which she is only able to give them in later years. This experience, with its pitiful limitation and its practical check by the personal taste and pecuniary standing of the family, is lost where it was found. Each mother slowly acquires some knowledge of her business by practising it upon the lives and health of her family and by observing its effect on the survivors; and each daughter begins again as ignorant as her mother was before her. This “rule of thumb” is not transmissible. It is not a genuine education such as all important work demands, but a slow animal process of soaking up experience﻿—hopelessly ineffectual in protecting the health of society. As the ultimate selecting agent in feeding humanity, the private housewife fails, and this not by reason of any lack of effort on her part, but by the essential defect of her position as individual purchaser. Only organization can oppose such evils as the wholesale adulteration of food; and woman, the house-servant, belongs to the lowest grade of unorganized labor.

===Chinese===
而在现状之下，女人从世界的食物中做挑选时，所能凭借的只是拿她那无告的家人练手换来的经验之谈，而这练手又恰逢其时——正是她成长中的儿女需要明智照料的年头，那样的照料，要过许多年她才给得起。这一点经验，连同它可悲的局限、连同它实际上受制于家庭个人口味与钱财状况的事实，在它得来的地方便丢失了。每一位母亲靠拿家人的性命与健康练手、靠观察它对幸存者的效验，慢慢攒下一点本行的知识；而每一个女儿又从头开始，同她母亲从前一样无知。这种「凭手感摸索的路数」传授不下去。它不是一切重要工作所要求的那种真正的教育，而是一种动物式缓慢泡取经验的过程——在保护社会健康上全然无济于事。作为喂养人类的最终挑选者，私家的家庭主妇是失败的——这失败并非因为她不够尽力，而是因为她作为个体购买者的位置有着本质的缺陷。唯有组织才能对抗食品大宗掺假这样的祸害；而作为家内佣工（house-servant）的女人，恰恰属于无组织劳工的最低一等。

===Original===
Leaving the selection of food, and examining its preparation, one would naturally suppose that the segregation of an entire sex to the fulfilment of this function would insure most remarkable results. It has, but they are not so favorable as might be expected. The art and science of cooking involve a large and thorough knowledge of nutritive value and of the laws of physiology and hygiene. As a science, it verges on preventive medicine. As an art, it is capable of noble expression within its natural bounds. As it stands among us today, it is so far from being a science and akin to preventive medicine, that it is the lowest of amateur handicrafts and a prolific source of disease; and, as an art, it has developed under the peculiar stimulus of its position as a sex-function into a voluptuous profusion as false as it is evil. Our innocent proverb, “The way to a man’s heart is through his stomach,” is a painfully plain comment on the way in which we have come to deprave our bodies and degrade our souls at the table.

===Chinese===
撇开食物的挑选，再来考察它的备办，人们自然会以为，把整整一个性别隔离出来专任此职，必定保证了最为可观的成果。成果确乎有，只是不如人所期望的那般好。烹饪的艺术与科学，牵涉到对营养价值、对生理学与卫生学诸法则的广博而透彻的知识。作为一门科学，它近于预防医学；作为一门艺术，在它的天然界限之内，它能作出高贵的表达。可是照它今天在我们中间的这副样子，它离一门科学、离预防医学的近邻关系远之又远——它是最末流的业余手艺，是滋生病患的丰沛源头；而作为一门艺术，它在它身为性功能这一位置的奇特刺激之下，发展出了一种既错又恶、耽欲铺张的繁缛。我们那句天真的谚语——「通往男人心的路，要经过他的胃」——正是对我们在餐桌上败坏身体、贬损灵魂之方式的痛切而明白的注脚。

===Original===
On the side of knowledge it is permanently impossible that half the world, acting as amateur cooks for the other half, can attain any high degree of scientific accuracy or technical skill. The development of any human labor requires specialization, and specialization is forbidden to our cook-by-nature system. What progress we have made in the science of cooking has been made through the study and experience of professional men cooks and chemists, not through the Sisyphean labors of our endless generations of isolated women, each beginning again where her mother began before her.

===Chinese===
在知识这一面，世界的一半人给另一半人当业余厨子，却要达到科学精确或高超技艺的任何高度，这是永远不可能的。任何人类劳作的发展都要求专门化，而我们的「天生厨娘」制度偏偏禁止专门化。我们在烹饪科学上已有的那点进步，是靠职业男厨与化学家的研究和历练取得的，不是靠我们一代又一代孤立无援的女人那西西弗斯式（Sisyphean）的劳作——她们每一个人，都又从她母亲当年起步的地方从头开始。

===Original===
Here, of course, will arise a pained outcry along the “mother’s doughnuts” line, in answer to which we refer to our second premise in the last chapter. The fact that we like a thing does not prove it to be right. A Missouri child may regard his mother’s saleratus biscuit with fond desire, but that does not alter their effect upon his spirits or his complexion. Cooking is a matter of law, not the harmless play of fancy. Architecture might be more sportive and varied if every man built his own house, but it would not be the art and science that we have made it; and, while every woman prepares food for her own family, cooking can never rise beyond the level of the amateur’s work.

===Chinese===
说到这里，照例会响起一阵「母亲的炸面圈」式的痛心疾首的抗议；对此，我们请读者回到上一章的第二条前提。我们喜欢一样东西，并不能证明它是对的。密苏里（Missouri）的孩子或许满怀渴望地想念他母亲的苏打饼干（saleratus biscuit），可这改变不了那饼干对他的心气或脸色的影响。烹饪是一件关乎法则的事，不是无伤大雅的奇想游戏。倘若人人都自建其屋，建筑或许会更活泼多变些，但它就不会是我们已经把它造就成的那门艺术与科学了；同样，只要每个女人都在为自己的家庭备办食物，烹饪便永远升不出业余者的水准。

===Original===
But, low as is the status of cooking as a science, as an art it is lower. Since the wife-cook’s main industry is to please﻿—that being her chief means of getting what she wants or of expressing affection﻿—she early learned to cater to the palate instead of faithfully studying and meeting the needs of the stomach. For uncounted generations the grown man and the growing child have been subject to the constant efforts of her who cooked from affection, not from knowledge﻿—who cooked to please. This is one of the widest pathways of evil that has ever been opened. In every field of life it is an evil to put the incident before the object, the means before the end; and here it has produced that familiar result whereby we live to eat instead of eating to live.

===Chinese===
然而，烹饪作为科学，地位固然低下；作为艺术，它更为低下。既然主妇厨子的主要营生便是取悦于人——那既是她获取所求的主要手段，也是她表达爱意的主要方式——她便早早学会了迎合口舌，而不是诚心研究并照应肠胃的需要。数不清的世代以来，成年的男人与成长中的孩子，一直听凭那个凭爱意而非凭知识做饭的人——那个为取悦而做饭的人——不休的摆布。这是有史以来开辟出的最宽阔的作恶途径之一。在生活的每个领域里，把偶然之事置于目的之前、把手段置于归宿之前，都是一种恶；而在这里，它已酿成那个我们熟稔的后果：我们为了吃而活，而不是为了活而吃。

===Original===
This attitude of the woman has developed the rambling excess called “fancy cookery,”﻿—a thing as far removed from true artistic development as a swinging ice-pitcher from a Greek vase. Through this has come the limitless unhealthy folly of high living, in which human labor and time and skill are wasted in producing what is neither pure food nor pure pleasure, but an artificial performance, to be appreciated only by the virtuoso. Lower living could hardly be imagined than that which results from this unnatural race between artifice and appetite, in which body and soul are both corrupted.

===Chinese===
女人的这副态度，发展出了那名为「花式烹饪」（fancy cookery）的漫无节制的铺张——这东西离真正的艺术发展之远，正如一只摇晃的冰水壶之于一尊希腊花瓶。经由它，便有了豪奢饮食那无边而不健康的愚行：人的劳力、时间与技艺尽被虚掷，去产出那既非纯粹食物、亦非纯粹快乐的东西——一种人工造作的表演，唯有行家里手方能赏识。这场矫饰与口腹之欲之间的不自然竞赛，其结果几乎叫人想象不出更低的生活来；在其中，身体与灵魂同遭腐蚀。

===Original===
In the man, the subject of all this dining-room devotion, has been developed and maintained that cultivated interest in his personal tastes and their gratification﻿—that demand for things which he likes rather than for things which he knows to be good, wherein lies one of the most dangerous elements in character known to the psychologist. The sequences of this affectionate catering to physical appetites may be traced far afield to its last result in the unchecked indulgence in personal tastes and desires, in drug habits and all intemperance. The temperament which is unable to resist these temptations is constantly being bred at home.

===Chinese===
而男人——这一切餐厅里殷勤供奉的对象——身上，则被培养并维持着那种对自己个人口味及其满足的刻意讲究：那种对他喜欢之物的索求，压过对他明知有益之物的索求；心理学家所知的最危险的性格要素之一，正植根于此。这种对肉体口腹之欲的体贴供奉，其后果可以追溯到极远的去处，直抵它最终的结果——对个人口味与欲望的无节制放纵、药瘾，以及种种的毫无节制。经不起这些诱惑的那种脾性，正在家庭之中源源不断地被孕育出来。

===Original===
